```json
{
  "command": "python3 << 'EOF'\nimport os\nimport ast\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ndef get_function_info(filepath):\n    \"\"\"Parse a Python file and extract function info.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        with open(filepath, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n            content = f.read()\n        tree = ast.parse(content)\n    except Exception as e:\n        return []\n    \n    results = []\n    lines = content.split('\\n')\n    \n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, ast.FunctionDef):\n            start_line = node.lineno - 1\n            end_line = node.end_lineno - 1 if node.end_lineno else start_line\n            line_count = end_line - start_line + 1\n            \n            # Get function source to analyze for patterns\n            func_source = '\\n'.join(lines[start_line:end_line + 1])\n            \n            # Check for nested functions\n            nested_funcs = [n for n in ast.walk(node) if isinstance(n, ast.FunctionDef) and n != node]\n            has_nested = len(nested_funcs) > 0\n            \n            # Check for nested lambdas\n            lambdas = [n for n in ast.walk(node) if isinstance(n, ast.Lambda)]\n            has_lambdas = len(lambdas) > 0\n            \n            # Check for mutable-list pattern: name = [...]\n            has_mutable_list_hack = bool(re.search(r'^\\s*\\w+\\s*=\\s*\\[', func_source, re.MULTILINE))\n            \n            # Check nesting depth\n            max_indent = max((len(line) - len(line.lstrip())) for line in func_source.split('\\n') if line.strip())\n            nesting_level = max_indent // 4\n            \n            # Store results if any patterns are found\n            if line_count > 150 or has_nested or nesting_level >= 4 or has_mutable_list_hack or has_lambdas:\n                results.append({\n                    'file': filepath,\n                    'func': node.name,\n                    'lines': line_count,\n                    'start_line': start_line + 1,\n                    'end_line': end_line + 1,\n                    'nested_funcs': len(nested_funcs),\n                    'has_nested': has_nested,\n                    'nesting_level': nesting_level,\n                    'has_lambdas': len(lambdas) > 0,\n                    'mutable_list_hack': has_mutable_list_hack,\n                })\n    \n    return results\n\n# Scan all Python files in desloppify/\nall_files = list(Path([REDACTED]).rglob('*.py'))\nall_results = []\n\nfor filepath in sorted(all_files):\n    funcs = get_function_info(str(filepath))\n    all_results.extend(funcs)\n\n# Sort by line count (descending) and then by severity of issues\nfor r in sorted(all_results, key=lambda x: (-x['lines'], -x['nesting_level'], -x['nested_funcs'])):\n    rel_path = r['file'].replace([REDACTED], '')\n    issues = []\n    if r['lines'] > 150:\n        issues.append(f\"long ({r['lines']} lines)\")\n    if r['has_nested']:\n        issues.append(f\"nested closures ({r['nested_funcs']})\")\n    if r['nesting_level'] >= 4:\n        issues.append(f\"deep nesting ({r['nesting_level']} levels)\")\n    if r['has_lambdas']:\n        issues.append(\"lambdas\")\n    if r['mutable_list_hack']:\n        issues.append(\"mutable-list pattern\")\n    \n    issue_str = \", \".join(issues)\n    print(f\"{rel_path}::{r['func']} ({r['start_line']}-{r['end_line']}) - {issue_str}\")\n\nEOF\n",
  "description": "Parse all Python files to find functions with structural issues"
}
```

```
85→def append_subjective_dimensions(
    86→    results: dict,
    87→    findings: dict,
    88→    assessments: dict | None,
    89→    failure_set: frozenset[str],
    90→    allowed_dimensions: set[str] | None = None,
    91→) -> None:
    92→    """Append subjective review dimensions to results dict (mutates results).
    93→
    94→    Subjective scoring is evidence-first: open review findings for a dimension
    95→    determine pass-rate, while imported assessment scores are retained as
    96→    metadata for transparency.
    97→    """
    98→    # Deferred import to avoid circular (see _dimension_display_name).
    99→    from desloppify.intelligence.review.dimensions.holistic import DIMENSIONS
   100→
   101→    raw_defaults = DIMENSIONS
   102→    allowed = (
   103→        {_normalize_dimension_key(name) for name in allowed_dimensions}
   104→        if allowed_dimensions is not None
   105→        else None
   106→    )
   107→
   108→    default_dimensions = []
   109→    for raw_dim in raw_defaults:
   110→        dim = _normalize_dimension_key(raw_dim)
   111→        if not dim:
   112→            continue
   113→        if allowed is not None and dim not in allowed:
   114→            continue
   115→        default_dimensions.append(dim)
   116→
   117→    assessed: dict[str, dict] = {}
   118→    for raw_dim, payload in (assessments or {}).items():
   119→        dim = _normalize_dimension_key(raw_dim)
   120→        if not dim:
   121→            continue
   122→        # Real assessments (from review imports) always count regardless of
   123→        # allowed_dimensions — they were deliberately imported and should not
   124→        # be silently discarded.  However, system-generated placeholders
   125→        # (scan_reset_subjective) are not real assessments and should respect
   126→        # the allowed_dimensions scope like defaults.
   127→        is_placeholder = isinstance(payload, dict) and (
   128→            payload.get("placeholder") is True
   129→            or payload.get("source") == "scan_reset_subjective"
   130→            or payload.get("reset_by") == "scan_reset_subjective"
   131→        )
   132→        if is_placeholder and allowed is not None and dim not in allowed:
   133→            continue
   134→        assessed[dim] = payload
   135→    existing_lower = {k.lower() for k in results}
   136→    lang_name = _primary_lang_from_findings(findings)
   137→
   138→    all_dims = list(default_dimensions)
   139→    for dim_name in assessed:
   140→        if dim_name not in default_dimensions:
   141→            all_dims.append(dim_name)
   142→
   143→    for dim_name in all_dims:
   144→        is_default = dim_name in default_dimensions
   145→        assessment = assessed.get(dim_name)
   146→        has_assessment = isinstance(assessment, dict)
   147→        if not is_default and not assessment:
   148→            continue
   149→
   150→        display = _dimension_display_name(dim_name, lang_name=lang_name)
   151→        if display.lower() in existing_lower:
   152→            display = f"{display} (subjective)"
   153→
   154→        # Count open review/concern findings for display (work queue), but
   155→        # these do NOT drive the dimension score — only assessment scores do.
   156→        issue_count = sum(
   157→            1
   158→            for finding in findings.values()
   159→            if finding.get("detector") in ("review", "concerns")
   160→            and finding.get("status") in failure_set
   161→            and _normalize_dimension_key(
   162→                finding.get("detail", {}).get("dimension")
   163→            )
   164→            == dim_name
   165→        )
   166→
   167→        assessment_score = (
   168→            max(0.0, min(100.0, float(assessment.get("score", 0))))
   169→            if isinstance(assessment, dict)
   170→            else 0.0
   171→        )
   172→        integrity_penalty = (
   173→            assessment.get("integrity_penalty")
   174→            if isinstance(assessment, dict)
   175→            else None
   176→        )
   177→        reset_pending = bool(
   178→            isinstance(assessment, dict)
   179→            and (
   180→                assessment.get("reset_by") == "scan_reset_subjective"
   181→                or assessment.get("source") == "scan_reset_subjective"
   182→                or assessment.get("placeholder") is True
   183→            )
   184→        )
```

```
129→def _get_ts_fixers() -> dict[str, FixerConfig]:
   130→    """Build the TypeScript fixer registry (lazy-loaded).
   131→
   132→    Detection and fix functions use lazy imports so detector modules
   133→    aren't loaded until the fix command actually runs.
   134→    """
   135→
   136→    def _det_unused(cat):
   137→        def f(path):
   138→            return unused_detector_mod.detect_unused(path, category=cat)[0]
   139→
   140→        return f
   141→
   142→    def _det_logs(path):
   143→        return logs_detector_mod.detect_logs(path)[0]
   144→
   145→    def _det_smell(smell_id):
   146→        def f(path):
   147→            return next(
   148→                (
   149→                    e.get("matches", [])
   150→                    for e in smells_detector_mod.detect_smells(path)[0]
   151→                    if e["id"] == smell_id
   152→                ),
   153→                [],
   154→            )
   155→
   156→        return f
   157→
   158→    def _fix_vars(entries, *, dry_run=False):
   159→        results, skip_reasons = ts_fixers_mod.fix_unused_vars(entries, dry_run=dry_run)
   160→        return FixResult(entries=results, skip_reasons=skip_reasons)
   161→
   162→    def _fix_logs(entries, *, dry_run=False):
   163→        results = ts_fixers_mod.fix_debug_logs(entries, dry_run=dry_run)
   164→        for r in results:
   165→            r["removed"] = r.get("tags", r.get("removed", []))
   166→        return results
   167→
   168→    def _lazy_fix(name):
   169→        def f(entries, **kw):
   170→            return getattr(ts_fixers_mod, name)(entries, **kw)
   171→
   172→        return f
```

## Search Results: Functions with Structural Issues in Desloppify

I've completed a systematic analysis of the desloppify codebase and identified functions suffering from similar structural issues to those refactored in `runner_helpers.py`. Here are the most critical findings:

### CRITICAL CANDIDATES (Highest Priority)

---

#### 1. **`do_run_batches()` — desloppify/app/commands/review/batches.py:299-822**
- **Size:** 524 lines (EXTREME)
- **Issues:** 
  - 3 nested closures (`_append_run_log`, `_run_batch`, nested progress handlers)
  - 6+ levels of deep nesting
  - Mutable-list-as-ref pattern (`stall_warned_batches: set[int] = set()` passed through closures)
  - Multiple state dictionaries mutated across nested scopes
- **What to Extract:**
  - Progress reporting logic (`_emit_progress`, `_handle_completed_future`) into separate handler class
  - Run artifact preparation and logging into a dedicated orchestrator
  - Batch result collection and aggregation into helper functions
  - Configuration parsing (lines 326-369) into a dedicated config builder

---

#### 2. **`execute_batches()` — desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_helpers.py:756-923**
- **Size:** 168 lines
- **Issues:**
  - 3 nested closures (`_emit_progress`, `_run_one`, `_handle_completed_future`)
  - 8 levels of deep nesting (ThreadPoolExecutor context + nested handlers + try/except chains)
  - Mutable state tracked across closures: `futures`, `started_at`, `pending`, `failures`
  - Complex futures management with nested condition checks
- **What to Extract:**
  - Heartbeat/timeout monitoring into dedicated event handler class
  - Future completion handling (`_handle_completed_future`) into separate worker coordinator
  - Parallel execution logic into a dedicated executor wrapper

---

#### 3. **`_get_ts_fixers()` — desloppify/languages/typescript/__init__.py:129-214**
- **Size:** 86 lines (but 9 nested closures!)
- **Issues:**
  - **9 nested closures** (_det_unused, _det_logs, _det_smell, _fix_vars, _fix_logs, _lazy_fix, and multiple inner functions)
  - 5 levels of nesting depth
  - Heavy use of closure-over-closure patterns (lazy binding)
  - Returns dict with closures as values (difficult to test, debug, introspect)
- **What to Extract:**
  - Create `DetectorFactory` class with static methods for each detector type
  - Create `FixerFactory` class with static methods for each fixer type
  - Replace closure chain with explicit factory methods
  - Makes dependencies visible and testable

---

#### 4. **`do_import()` — desloppify/app/commands/review/import_cmd.py:182-364**
- **Size:** 183 lines
- **Issues:**
  - 5+ levels of deep nesting
  - Multiple nested conditional chains (lines 248-257)
  - State mutation across validation phases (`working_state` modified through multiple stages)
  - Mixed concerns: loading, validation, assessment policy, file operations
- **What to Extract:**
  - Assessment policy application into separate phase handler
  - Import validation checks into validator class
  - State mutation and provisional marking into dedicated updater

---

#### 5. **`show_subjective_paths()` — desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_reporting_subjective.py:334-530**
- **Size:** 197 lines
- **Issues:**
  - 6 levels of deep nesting
  - Mutable-list-as-ref pattern: `integrity_bits: list[str] = []` accumulated and mutated (line 419)
  - 3+ lambdas embedded in the function
  - Complex conditional chains for rendering different output paths
- **What to Extract:**
  - Integrity gap computation into separate analyzer function
  - Output formatting into dedicated renderer (one per section: unassessed, holistic, coverage)
  - Reason counting and display logic into helper functions

---

### SIGNIFICANT CANDIDATES

#### 6. **`append_subjective_dimensions()` — desloppify/engine/_scoring/subjective/core.py:85-249**
- **Size:** 165 lines
- **Issues:**
  - 6 levels of deep nesting
  - Mutable-list-as-ref: `default_dimensions = []`, `all_dims = list(...)` (lines 108, 138)
  - Multiple passes over dimensions/assessments with nested loops
- **Extract:** Dimension scoring into separate `DimensionScorer` class; default vs assessed logic into validators

#### 7. **`detect_review_coverage()` — desloppify/engine/detectors/review_coverage.py:47-203**
- **Size:** 157 lines
- **Issues:**
  - 8 levels of deep nesting
  - Complex nested conditionals for file filtering (lines 72-94)
  - State tracking: `candidates`, `holistic_fresh`, `coverage_*` variables mutated across phases
- **Extract:** File eligibility checker; review cache analyzer; holistic freshness detector

#### 8. **`generic_lang()` — desloppify/languages/_framework/generic.py:157-314**
- **Size:** 158 lines
- **Issues:**
  - 4 levels of deep nesting
  - Mutable-list pattern: `phases = [...]` (line 236) built incrementally
  - Large tree-sitter conditional branch (lines 219-233)
  - Tool registration loop with mutable `fixers` dict
- **Extract:** Tool registration into separate `ToolRegistrar` class; phase builder into `PhaseBuilder`

#### 9. **`_add_review_parser()` — desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin.py:124-380**
- **Size:** 257 lines
- **Issues:**
  - Mutable-list pattern: `p_review.add_argument()` called 35+ times on same parser object
  - Low cohesion: mixing argument groups that could be extracted
- **Extract:** Argument groups (prepare, import, validate, override, assessment) into separate registrar functions

---

### MEDIUM-PRIORITY PATTERNS

Other functions with notable issues but lower impact:

- **`_cmd_fix_review()` — desloppify/app/commands/fix/review_flow.py:21-162** (6 levels nesting, conditional chains)
- **`_make_structural_phase()` — desloppify/languages/_framework/generic.py:320-400** (2 nested closures, 5 levels, lambdas)
- **`prepare_review()` — desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare.py:97-177** (4 levels nesting, mutable-list)
- **`detect_cycles()` — desloppify/engine/detectors/graph.py:57-137** (1 nested closure, 7 levels nesting)
- **`build_work_queue()` — desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py:54-144** (5 levels nesting, mutable-list)

---

### RECOMMENDED REFACTORING STRATEGY

1. **Start with `_get_ts_fixers()`** — The nested-closure pattern is most extreme and teaches the most. Extract to factory classes for immediate payoff.

2. **Then tackle `execute_batches()`** — Most recent refactoring context; similar threading complexity as `runner_helpers.py`.

3. **Then `do_run_batches()`** — Large function; multiple extraction opportunities; good for teaching phased decomposition.

4. **Focus on `append_subjective_dimensions()`** — Data structure transformation; teaches loop extraction and state management.

5. **Polish with parser/config functions** — Lower risk; improves maintainability incrementally.

---

### KEY PATTERNS TO EXTRACT

Across all candidates, these patterns consistently appear:

1. **Nested closures over mutable state** → Extract to instance methods in handler classes
2. **Deep nesting (7+ levels)** → Extract inner conditionals; use early returns
3. **Mutable-list accumulation** → Use explicit builders or dataclasses instead of `[]` mutation
4. **Mixed concerns** → Split into validation → transformation → reporting phases
5. **Lambdas in comprehensions** → Extract to named functions for readability

Search the desloppify/ source directory (not tests/) for inconsistencies in coding patterns, conventions, and style. Look for things like:

1. **Naming inconsistencies**: mixed snake_case/camelCase, inconsistent prefixes (e.g., some private helpers use `_` prefix and some don't), inconsistent suffixes (e.g., `_fn` vs `_func` vs `_callback`)
2. **Import style inconsistencies**: some modules using `from X import Y` vs `import X` for the same thing
3. **Error handling inconsistencies**: some functions returning error codes, others raising exceptions, others returning None — for similar operations
4. **Type annotation inconsistencies**: some functions fully typed, others not; mixed `dict` vs `Dict`, `list` vs `List`, `X | None` vs `Optional[X]`
5. **Dataclass vs dict inconsistencies**: some structured data as dataclasses, others as plain dicts, for similar purposes
6. **String formatting inconsistencies**: mixed f-strings, .format(), % formatting
7. **Path handling inconsistencies**: mixed Path objects vs str paths
8. **Callback/dependency injection inconsistencies**: some deps passed as dataclass bundles (like CodexBatchRunnerDeps), others as loose kwargs

```
1→"""Canonical enums for finding attributes.
     2→
     3→StrEnum values compare equal to their string values (Confidence.HIGH == "high"),
     4→so existing code using raw strings continues to work during gradual migration.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import enum

    11→
    12→class Confidence(enum.StrEnum):

    14→    MEDIUM = "medium"
    15→    LOW = "low"
    16→
    17→
    18→class Status(enum.StrEnum):
    19→    OPEN = "open"
    20→    FIXED = "fixed"
    21→    WONTFIX = "wontfix"
    22→    FALSE_POSITIVE = "false_positive"
    23→    AUTO_RESOLVED = "auto_resolved"

    25→
    26→

    30→    JUDGMENT = 3
    31→    MAJOR_REFACTOR = 4
```

```
   323→_DISPLAY_ORDER = list(DISPLAY_ORDER)
   324→
   325→JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
   326→    name for name, meta in DETECTORS.items() if meta.needs_judgment
   327→)
   328→
   329→_on_register_callbacks: list[Callable[[], None]] = []
   330→
   331→
   332→def on_detector_registered(callback: Callable[[], None]) -> None:
   333→    """Register a callback invoked after register_detector(). No-arg."""
   334→    _on_register_callbacks.append(callback)
   335→
   336→
   337→def register_detector(meta: DetectorMeta) -> None:
   338→    """Register a detector at runtime (used by generic plugins)."""
   339→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   340→    DETECTORS[meta.name] = meta
   341→    if meta.name not in _DISPLAY_ORDER:
   342→        _DISPLAY_ORDER.append(meta.name)
   343→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = frozenset(
   344→        name for name, m in DETECTORS.items() if m.needs_judgment
   345→    )
   346→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   347→        cb()
   348→
   349→
   350→def detector_names() -> list[str]:
   351→    """All registered detector names, sorted."""
   352→    return sorted(DETECTORS.keys())
   353→
   354→
   355→def display_order() -> list[str]:
   356→    """Canonical display order for terminal output."""
   357→    return list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   358→
   359→
   360→_ACTION_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
   361→_ACTION_LABELS = {
   362→    "auto_fix": "fix",
   363→    "reorganize": "move",
   364→    "refactor": "refactor",
   365→    "manual_fix": "manual",
   366→}
   367→
   368→
   369→def dimension_action_type(dim_name: str) -> str:
   370→    """Return a compact action type label for a dimension based on its detectors."""
   371→    best = "manual"
   372→    best_pri = 99
   373→    for d in DETECTORS.values():
   374→        if d.dimension == dim_name:
   375→            pri = _ACTION_PRIORITY.get(d.action_type, 99)
   376→            if pri < best_pri:
   377→                best_pri = pri
   378→                best = d.action_type
   379→    return _ACTION_LABELS.get(best, "manual")
   380→
   381→
   382→def detector_tools() -> dict[str, dict]:
   383→    """Build detector tool metadata keyed by detector name."""
   384→    result = {}
   385→    for name, d in DETECTORS.items():
   386→        entry: dict = {
   387→            "fixers": list(d.fixers),
   388→            "action_type": d.action_type,
   389→        }
   390→        if d.tool:
   391→            entry["tool"] = d.tool
   392→        if d.guidance:
   393→            entry["guidance"] = d.guidance
   394→        result[name] = entry
   395→    return result
   396→
```

```
1→"""Compatibility facade over focused core utility modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→import re
     7→from dataclasses import dataclass
     8→
     9→from desloppify.core import tooling as _tooling
    10→from desloppify.core._internal import text_utils as _text_utils
    11→from desloppify.core.grep import grep_count_files, grep_files, grep_files_containing
    12→from desloppify.core.output import (
    13→    COLORS,
    14→    LOC_COMPACT_THRESHOLD,
    15→    NO_COLOR,
    16→    colorize,
    17→    display_entries,
    18→    log,
    19→    print_table,
    20→)
    21→
    22→__all__ = [
    23→    # Path constants
    24→    "PROJECT_ROOT",
    25→    "DEFAULT_PATH",
    26→    "SRC_PATH",
    27→    # Grep helpers
    28→    "read_code_snippet",
    29→    "grep_files",
    30→    "grep_files_containing",
    31→    "grep_count_files",
    32→    # Output formatting
    33→    "LOC_COMPACT_THRESHOLD",
    34→    "COLORS",
    35→    "NO_COLOR",
    36→    "colorize",
    37→    "log",
    38→    "print_table",
    39→    "display_entries",
    40→    # Tool staleness
    41→    "TOOL_DIR",
    42→    "compute_tool_hash",
    43→    "check_tool_staleness",
    44→    # Skill document tracking
    45→    "SKILL_VERSION",
    46→    "SKILL_VERSION_RE",
    47→    "SKILL_OVERLAY_RE",
    48→    "SKILL_BEGIN",
    49→    "SKILL_END",
    50→    "SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS",
    51→    "SKILL_TARGETS",
    52→    "SkillInstall",
    53→    "find_installed_skill",
    54→    "check_skill_version",
    55→]
    56→
    57→_get_project_root = _text_utils.get_project_root
    58→
    59→PROJECT_ROOT = _text_utils.PROJECT_ROOT
    60→DEFAULT_PATH = PROJECT_ROOT / "src"
    61→SRC_PATH = PROJECT_ROOT / os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_SRC", "src")
    62→TOOL_DIR = _tooling.TOOL_DIR
    63→
    64→
    65→def read_code_snippet(filepath: str, line: int, context: int = 1) -> str | None:
    66→    """Read a snippet around a 1-based line number."""
    67→    return _text_utils.read_code_snippet(
    68→        filepath,
    69→        line,
    70→        context,
    71→        project_root=_get_project_root(),
    72→    )
    73→
    74→
    75→def compute_tool_hash() -> str:
    76→    """Compute a content hash of tool code honoring ``utils.TOOL_DIR`` overrides."""
    77→    original_tool_dir = _tooling.TOOL_DIR
    78→    _tooling.TOOL_DIR = TOOL_DIR
    79→    try:
    80→        return _tooling.compute_tool_hash()
    81→    finally:
    82→        _tooling.TOOL_DIR = original_tool_dir
    83→
    84→
    85→def check_tool_staleness(state: dict) -> str | None:
    86→    """Return warning if tool code changed, honoring ``utils.TOOL_DIR`` overrides."""
    87→    original_tool_dir = _tooling.TOOL_DIR
    88→    _tooling.TOOL_DIR = TOOL_DIR
    89→    try:
    90→        return _tooling.check_tool_staleness(state)
    91→    finally:
    92→        _tooling.TOOL_DIR = original_tool_dir
    93→
    94→
    95→# ── Skill document version tracking ─────────────────────────
    96→# Bump this integer whenever docs/SKILL.md changes in a way that agents
    97→# should pick up (new commands, changed workflows, removed sections).
    98→SKILL_VERSION = 1
    99→
   100→SKILL_VERSION_RE = re.compile(r"<!--\s*desloppify-skill-version:\s*(\d+)\s*-->")
   101→SKILL_OVERLAY_RE = re.compile(r"<!--\s*desloppify-overlay:\s*(\w+)\s*-->")
   102→
   103→SKILL_BEGIN = "<!-- desloppify-begin -->"
   104→SKILL_END = "<!-- desloppify-end -->"
   105→
   106→# Locations where the skill doc might be installed, relative to PROJECT_ROOT.
   107→SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS = (
   108→    ".claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md",
   109→    "AGENTS.md",
   110→    "CLAUDE.md",
   111→    ".cursor/rules/desloppify.md",
   112→    ".github/copilot-instructions.md",
   113→)
   114→
   115→# Interface name → (target file, overlay filename, dedicated).
   116→# Dedicated files are overwritten entirely; shared files get section replacement.
   117→SKILL_TARGETS: dict[str, tuple[str, str, bool]] = {
   118→    "claude": (".claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md", "CLAUDE", True),
   119→    "codex": ("AGENTS.md", "CODEX", False),
   120→    "cursor": (".cursor/rules/desloppify.md", "CURSOR", True),
   121→    "copilot": (".github/copilot-instructions.md", "COPILOT", False),
   122→    "windsurf": ("AGENTS.md", "WINDSURF", False),
   123→    "gemini": ("AGENTS.md", "GEMINI", False),
   124→}
   125→
   126→
   127→@dataclass
   128→class SkillInstall:
   129→    """Detected skill document installation."""
   130→
   131→    rel_path: str
   132→    version: int
   133→    overlay: str | None
   134→    stale: bool
   135→
   136→
   137→def find_installed_skill() -> SkillInstall | None:
   138→    """Find installed skill document metadata, or None."""
   139→    for rel_path in SKILL_SEARCH_PATHS:
   140→        full = _get_project_root() / rel_path
   141→        if not full.is_file():
   142→            continue
   143→        try:
   144→            content = full.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   145→        except OSError:
   146→            continue
   147→        version_match = SKILL_VERSION_RE.search(content)
   148→        if not version_match:
   149→            continue
   150→        installed_version = int(version_match.group(1))
   151→        overlay_match = SKILL_OVERLAY_RE.search(content)
   152→        overlay = overlay_match.group(1) if overlay_match else None
   153→        return SkillInstall(
   154→            rel_path=rel_path,
   155→            version=installed_version,
   156→            overlay=overlay,
   157→            stale=installed_version < SKILL_VERSION,
   158→        )
   159→    return None
   160→
   161→
   162→def check_skill_version() -> str | None:
   163→    """Return a warning if installed skill doc is outdated."""
   164→    install = find_installed_skill()
   165→    if not install or not install.stale:
   166→        return None
   167→    return (
   168→        f"Your desloppify skill document is outdated "
   169→        f"(v{install.version}, current v{SKILL_VERSION}). "
   170→        "Run: desloppify update-skill"
   171→    )
   172→
```

```
1→"""CLI entry point: parse args, load shared context, dispatch command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import sys
     7→
     8→from desloppify import file_discovery as file_discovery_mod
     9→from desloppify import utils as utils_mod
    10→from desloppify.app.cli_support.parser import create_parser as _create_parser
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import LangResolutionError, resolve_lang
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import CommandRuntime
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    14→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import PROJECT_ROOT
    15→from desloppify.core.config import load_config
    16→from desloppify.core.runtime_state import runtime_scope
    17→from desloppify.languages import available_langs
    18→from desloppify.state import load_state
    19→from desloppify.utils import DEFAULT_PATH, colorize
    20→
    21→_DETECTOR_NAMES: list[str] | None = None
    22→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    23→
    24→
    25→def _get_detector_names() -> list[str]:
    26→    """Return cached detector names, computing on first access."""
    27→    global _DETECTOR_NAMES
    28→    if _DETECTOR_NAMES is None:
    29→        from desloppify.core.registry import detector_names
    30→
    31→        _DETECTOR_NAMES = detector_names()
    32→    return _DETECTOR_NAMES
    33→
    34→def create_parser():
    35→    """Return the top-level argparse parser."""
    36→    return _create_parser(langs=available_langs(), detector_names=_get_detector_names())
    37→
    38→
    39→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config: dict):
    40→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude and apply globally."""
    41→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
    42→    persisted = config.get("exclude", [])
    43→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
    44→    if not combined:
    45→        return
    46→    file_discovery_mod.set_exclusions(combined)
    47→    if cli_exclusions:
    48→        print(
    49→            utils_mod.colorize(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"),
    50→            file=sys.stderr,
    51→        )
    52→        return
    53→    print(
    54→        utils_mod.colorize(
    55→            f"  Excluding (from config): {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"
    56→        ),
    57→        file=sys.stderr,
    58→    )
    59→
    60→
    61→def _resolve_default_path(args) -> None:
    62→    """Fill args.path from detected language or default source path.
    63→
    64→    For the review command, the last scan path (stored in state) is used as the
    65→    default so that ``desloppify review --prepare`` works on the same scope as
    66→    the preceding scan even when the project files are not under ``src/``.
    67→    """
    68→    if getattr(args, "path", None) is not None:
    69→        return
    70→    if getattr(args, "command", None) == "review":
    71→        try:
    72→            state_file = state_path(args)
    73→            if state_file:
    74→                saved = load_state(state_file)
    75→                saved_path = saved.get("scan_path")
    76→                if saved_path:
    77→                    args.path = str((PROJECT_ROOT / saved_path).resolve())
    78→                    return
    79→        except (OSError, KeyError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
    80→            logger.debug("Failed to resolve default path from saved state: %s", exc)
    81→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    82→    if lang:
    83→        args.path = str(PROJECT_ROOT / lang.default_src)
    84→    else:
    85→        args.path = str(DEFAULT_PATH)
    86→
    87→
    88→def _load_shared_runtime(args) -> None:
    89→    """Load config/state and attach shared objects to parsed args."""
    90→    config = load_config()
    91→
    92→    state_file = state_path(args)
    93→    state = load_state(state_file)
    94→    _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
    95→
    96→    args.runtime = CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
    97→
    98→
    99→def _resolve_handler(command: str):
   100→    from desloppify.app.commands.registry import get_command_handlers
   101→
   102→    return get_command_handlers()[command]
   103→
   104→
   105→def _handle_help_command(args, parser) -> None:
   106→    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
   107→    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
   108→    try:
   109→        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
   110→    except SystemExit:
   111→        return
   112→
   113→
   114→def main() -> None:
   115→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   116→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   117→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   118→            try:
   119→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   120→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   121→                logger.debug(
   122→                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
   123→                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
   124→                )
   125→
   126→    parser = create_parser()
   127→    args = parser.parse_args()
   128→    if args.command == "help":
   129→        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
   130→        return
   131→
   132→    try:
   133→        with runtime_scope():
   134→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   135→            _load_shared_runtime(args)
   136→
   137→            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
   138→            handler(args)
   139→    except LangResolutionError as exc:
   140→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   141→        sys.exit(1)
   142→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   143→        print("\nInterrupted.")
   144→        sys.exit(1)
   145→
   146→
   147→if __name__ == "__main__":
   148→    main()
   149→
```

```
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from collections.abc import Callable
     7→from functools import lru_cache

    20→
    21→class LangResolutionError(SystemExit):
    22→    """Raised when language resolution fails with a user-facing message.
    23→
    24→    Inherits from SystemExit so that callers which don't catch it explicitly
    25→    will still terminate with a non-zero exit code, matching the previous
    26→    sys.exit(1) behaviour.  The CLI top-level catches it to print a clean
    27→    error without a traceback.
    28→    """
    29→
    30→    def __init__(self, message: str) -> None:
    31→        self.message = message
    32→        super().__init__(1)
    33→
    34→
    35→EXTRA_ROOT_MARKERS = (
    36→    "package.json",
    37→    "pyproject.toml",
    38→    "setup.py",
    39→    "setup.cfg",
    40→    "go.mod",
    41→    "Cargo.toml",
    42→)
    43→
    44→
    45→def _cache_once(fn: Callable[[], tuple[str, ...]]) -> Callable[[], tuple[str, ...]]:
    46→    """Cache a zero-arg function once while preserving cache_clear for tests."""
    47→    cached = lru_cache(maxsize=1)(fn)
    48→    return cached
    49→
    50→
    51→@_cache_once
    52→def _lang_config_markers() -> tuple[str, ...]:
    53→    """Collect project-root marker files from language plugins + fallback markers."""
    54→    markers = set(EXTRA_ROOT_MARKERS)
    55→
    56→    for lang_name in lang_api.available_langs():
    57→        try:
    58→            cfg = lang_api.get_lang(lang_name)
    59→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
    60→            logger.debug(
    61→                "Skipping language marker collection for %s: %s", lang_name, exc
    62→            )
    63→            continue
    64→        for marker in getattr(cfg, "detect_markers", []) or []:
    65→            if not isinstance(marker, str):
    66→                continue
    67→            cleaned = marker.strip()
    68→            if cleaned:
    69→                markers.add(cleaned)
    70→    return tuple(sorted(markers))
    71→
    72→
    73→def resolve_detection_root(
    74→    args,
    75→    *,
    76→    project_root: Path = PROJECT_ROOT,
    77→    marker_provider: Callable[[], tuple[str, ...]] | None = None,
    78→) -> Path:
    79→    """Best root to auto-detect language from."""
    80→    marker_provider = marker_provider or _lang_config_markers
    81→    markers = marker_provider()
    82→
    83→    raw_path = getattr(args, "path", None)
    84→    if not raw_path:
    85→        return project_root
    86→
    87→    candidate = Path(raw_path)
    88→    if not candidate.is_absolute():
    89→        candidate = project_root / candidate
    90→    candidate = candidate.resolve()
    91→    search_root = candidate if candidate.is_dir() else candidate.parent
    92→
    93→    for root in (search_root, *search_root.parents):
    94→        if any((root / marker).exists() for marker in markers):
    95→            return root
    96→    return search_root
    97→
    98→
    99→def auto_detect_lang_name(args) -> str | None:
   100→    """Auto-detect language using the most relevant root for this command."""
   101→    root = resolve_detection_root(args)
   102→    detected = lang_api.auto_detect_lang(root)
   103→    if detected is None and root != PROJECT_ROOT:
   104→        detected = lang_api.auto_detect_lang(PROJECT_ROOT)
   105→    return detected
   106→
   107→
   108→def resolve_lang(args) -> LangConfig | None:
   109→    """Resolve language config from args, with auto-detection fallback."""
   110→    lang_name = getattr(args, "lang", None)
   111→    if lang_name is None:
   112→        lang_name = auto_detect_lang_name(args)
   113→    if lang_name is None:
   114→        return None
   115→    try:
   116→        return lang_api.get_lang(lang_name)
   117→    except ValueError as exc:
   118→        langs = lang_api.available_langs()
   119→        langs_str = ", ".join(langs) if langs else "registered language plugins"
   120→        raise LangResolutionError(
   121→            f"{exc}\n  Hint: use --lang to select manually (available: {langs_str})"
   122→        ) from exc
   123→
   124→
   125→def resolve_lang_settings(config: dict, lang: LangConfig) -> dict[str, object]:
   126→    """Resolve persisted per-language settings from config.languages.<lang>."""
   127→    if not isinstance(config, dict):
   128→        return lang.normalize_settings({})
   129→    languages = config.get("languages", {})
   130→    if not isinstance(languages, dict):
   131→        return lang.normalize_settings({})
   132→    raw = languages.get(lang.name, {})
   133→    return lang.normalize_settings(raw if isinstance(raw, dict) else {})
   134→
```

```
1→"""Path resolution and exclusion matching helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→import tempfile
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import get_project_root
    10→
    11→
    12→def matches_exclusion(rel_path: str, exclusion: str) -> bool:
    13→    """Check if a relative path matches an exclusion pattern."""
    14→    parts = Path(rel_path).parts
    15→    if exclusion in parts:
    16→        return True
    17→    if "*" in exclusion:
    18→        import fnmatch
    19→
    20→        if any(fnmatch.fnmatch(part, exclusion) for part in parts):
    21→            return True
    22→    if "/" in exclusion or os.sep in exclusion:
    23→        normalized = exclusion.rstrip("/").rstrip(os.sep)
    24→        return rel_path.startswith(normalized + "/") or rel_path.startswith(
    25→            normalized + os.sep
    26→        )
    27→    return False
    28→
    29→
    30→def normalize_path_separators(path: str) -> str:
    31→    return path.replace("\\", "/")
    32→
    33→
    34→def safe_relpath(path: str | Path, start: str | Path) -> str:
    35→    try:
    36→        return os.path.relpath(str(path), str(start))
    37→    except ValueError:
    38→        return str(Path(path).resolve())
    39→
    40→
    41→def rel(path: str) -> str:
    42→    root = get_project_root()
    43→    resolved = Path(path).resolve()
    44→    try:
    45→        return normalize_path_separators(str(resolved.relative_to(root)))
    46→    except ValueError:
    47→        return normalize_path_separators(safe_relpath(resolved, root))
    48→
    49→
    50→def resolve_path(filepath: str) -> str:
    51→    """Resolve a filepath to absolute, handling both relative and absolute."""
    52→    p = Path(filepath)
    53→    if p.is_absolute():
    54→        return str(p.resolve())
    55→    return str((get_project_root() / filepath).resolve())
    56→
    57→
    58→def safe_write_text(filepath: str | Path, content: str) -> None:
    59→    """Atomically write text to a file using temp+rename."""
    60→    p = Path(filepath)
    61→    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    62→    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=p.parent, suffix=".tmp")
    63→    try:
    64→        with os.fdopen(fd, "w") as f:
    65→            f.write(content)
    66→        os.replace(tmp, str(p))
    67→    except OSError:
    68→        if os.path.exists(tmp):
    69→            os.unlink(tmp)
    70→        raise
    71→
    72→
    73→__all__ = [
    74→    "matches_exclusion",
    75→    "normalize_path_separators",
    76→    "rel",
    77→    "resolve_path",
    78→    "safe_relpath",
    79→    "safe_write_text",
    80→]
    81→
```

```
    70→def _ts_treesitter_phases() -> list[DetectorPhase]:
    71→    """Cherry-pick tree-sitter phases that complement TS's own detectors.
    72→
    73→    TS already has its own smells and unused detection — only add cohesion
    74→    which TS lacks.  (Signature analysis is added separately since it's
    75→    backend-agnostic and doesn't require tree-sitter.)
    76→    """
    77→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter import is_available
    78→
    79→    if not is_available():
    80→        return []
    81→
    82→    from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._specs import TREESITTER_SPECS
    83→
    84→    spec = TREESITTER_SPECS.get("typescript")
    85→    if spec is None:
    86→        return []
    87→
    88→    return [
    89→        make_cohesion_phase(spec),
    90→    ]
    91→
    92→

    94→    ts_test_coverage_hooks.has_testable_logic,
    95→    ts_test_coverage_hooks.resolve_import_spec,
    96→    ts_test_coverage_hooks.map_test_to_source,
    97→)
```

```
1→"""fix command: auto-fix mechanical issues with fixer registry and pipeline."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.commands._show_terminal import show_fix_dry_run_samples
     9→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import FixResult
    10→from desloppify.utils import colorize
    11→
    12→from .apply_flow import (
    13→    _apply_and_report,
    14→    _detect,
    15→    _print_fix_summary,
    16→    _report_dry_run,
    17→    _warn_uncommitted_changes,
    18→)
    19→from .options import _load_fixer
    20→from .review_flow import _cmd_fix_review
    21→
    22→
    23→def cmd_fix(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    24→    """Auto-fix mechanical issues."""
    25→    fixer_name = args.fixer
    26→    if fixer_name == "review":
    27→        _cmd_fix_review(args)
    28→        return
    29→
    30→    dry_run = getattr(args, "dry_run", False)
    31→    path = Path(args.path)
    32→
    33→    lang, fixer = _load_fixer(args, fixer_name)
    34→
    35→    if not dry_run:
    36→        _warn_uncommitted_changes()
    37→    entries = _detect(fixer, path)
    38→    if not entries:
    39→        print(colorize(f"No {fixer.label} found.", "green"))
    40→        return
    41→
    42→    raw = fixer.fix(entries, dry_run=dry_run)
    43→    if isinstance(raw, FixResult):
    44→        results = raw.entries
    45→        skip_reasons = raw.skip_reasons
    46→    else:
    47→        results = raw
    48→        skip_reasons = {}
    49→    total_items = sum(len(r["removed"]) for r in results)
    50→    total_lines = sum(r.get("lines_removed", 0) for r in results)
    51→    _print_fix_summary(fixer, results, total_items, total_lines, dry_run)
    52→
    53→    if dry_run and results:
    54→        show_fix_dry_run_samples(entries, results)
    55→
    56→    if not dry_run:
    57→        _apply_and_report(
    58→            args,
    59→            path,
    60→            fixer,
    61→            fixer_name,
    62→            entries,
    63→            results,
    64→            total_items,
    65→            lang,
    66→            skip_reasons,
    67→        )
    68→    else:
    69→        _report_dry_run(args, fixer_name, entries, results, total_items)
    70→    print()
    71→
```

```
1→"""File discovery: source file finding, exclusion matching, and traversal caching."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import get_project_root
     9→from desloppify.core.file_paths import (
    10→    matches_exclusion,
    11→    rel,
    12→    resolve_path,
    13→    safe_write_text,
    14→)
    15→from desloppify.core.file_paths import (
    16→    normalize_path_separators as _normalize_path_separators,
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.core.file_paths import (
    19→    safe_relpath as _safe_relpath,
    20→)
    21→from desloppify.core.runtime_state import current_runtime_context
    22→
    23→__all__ = [
    24→    "DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS",
    25→    "set_exclusions",
    26→    "get_exclusions",
    27→    "matches_exclusion",
    28→    "rel",
    29→    "resolve_path",
    30→    "safe_write_text",
    31→    "enable_file_cache",
    32→    "disable_file_cache",
    33→    "is_file_cache_enabled",
    34→    "read_file_text",
    35→    "find_source_files",
    36→    "find_ts_files",
    37→    "find_tsx_files",
    38→    "find_py_files",
    39→]
    40→
    41→
    42→# Directories that are never useful to scan — always pruned during traversal.
    43→DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS = frozenset(
    44→    {
    45→        "node_modules",
    46→        ".git",
    47→        "__pycache__",
    48→        ".venv*",
    49→        "venv",
    50→        ".env",
    51→        "dist",
    52→        "build",
    53→        ".next",
    54→        ".nuxt",
    55→        ".output",
    56→        ".tox",
    57→        ".mypy_cache",
    58→        ".pytest_cache",
    59→        ".ruff_cache",
    60→        ".eggs",
    61→        "*.egg-info",
    62→        ".svn",
    63→        ".hg",
    64→    }
    65→)
    66→
    67→
    68→def set_exclusions(patterns: list[str]):
    69→    """Set global exclusion patterns (called once from CLI at startup)."""
    70→    runtime = current_runtime_context()
    71→    runtime.exclusions = tuple(patterns)
    72→    runtime.source_file_cache.clear()
    73→
    74→
    75→def get_exclusions() -> tuple[str, ...]:
    76→    """Return current extra exclusion patterns."""
    77→    return current_runtime_context().exclusions
    78→
    79→
    80→# ── File content cache & reading ──────────────────────────────
    81→
    82→
    83→def enable_file_cache():
    84→    """Enable scan-scoped file content cache."""
    85→    runtime = current_runtime_context()
    86→    runtime.file_text_cache.enable()
    87→    runtime.cache_enabled = True
    88→
    89→
    90→def disable_file_cache():
    91→    """Disable file content cache and free memory."""
    92→    runtime = current_runtime_context()
    93→    runtime.file_text_cache.disable()
    94→    runtime.cache_enabled = False
    95→
    96→
    97→def is_file_cache_enabled() -> bool:
    98→    """Return whether scan-scoped file cache is currently enabled."""
    99→    return current_runtime_context().cache_enabled
   100→
   101→
   102→def read_file_text(filepath: str) -> str | None:
   103→    """Read a file as text, with optional caching."""
   104→    return current_runtime_context().file_text_cache.read(filepath)
   105→
   106→
   107→def _is_excluded_dir(name: str, rel_path: str, extra: tuple[str, ...]) -> bool:
   108→    in_default_exclusions = name in DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS or name.endswith(".egg-info")
   109→    is_virtualenv_dir = name.startswith(".venv") or name.startswith("venv")
   110→    matches_extra_exclusion = bool(
   111→        extra
   112→        and any(
   113→            matches_exclusion(rel_path, exclusion) or exclusion == name
   114→            for exclusion in extra
   115→        )
   116→    )
   117→    return in_default_exclusions or is_virtualenv_dir or matches_extra_exclusion
   118→
   119→
   120→def _clear_source_file_cache() -> None:
   121→    current_runtime_context().source_file_cache.clear()
   122→
   123→
   124→def _find_source_files_cached(
   125→    path: str,
   126→    extensions: tuple[str, ...],
   127→    exclusions: tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
   128→    extra_exclusions: tuple[str, ...] = (),
   129→) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   130→    """Cached file discovery using os.walk — cross-platform, prunes during traversal."""
   131→    cache_key = (path, extensions, exclusions, extra_exclusions)
   132→    cache = current_runtime_context().source_file_cache
   133→    cached = cache.get(cache_key)
   134→    if cached is not None:
   135→        return cached
   136→
   137→    project_root = get_project_root()
   138→    root = Path(path)
   139→    if not root.is_absolute():
   140→        root = project_root / root
   141→    all_exclusions = (exclusions or ()) + extra_exclusions
   142→    ext_set = set(extensions)
   143→    files: list[str] = []
   144→    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
   145→        rel_dir = _normalize_path_separators(_safe_relpath(dirpath, project_root))
   146→        dirnames[:] = sorted(
   147→            d
   148→            for d in dirnames
   149→            if not _is_excluded_dir(d, rel_dir + "/" + d, all_exclusions)
   150→        )
   151→        for fname in filenames:
   152→            if any(fname.endswith(ext) for ext in ext_set):
   153→                full = os.path.join(dirpath, fname)
   154→                rel_file = _normalize_path_separators(_safe_relpath(full, project_root))
   155→                if all_exclusions and any(
   156→                    matches_exclusion(rel_file, ex) for ex in all_exclusions
   157→                ):
   158→                    continue
   159→                files.append(rel_file)
   160→    result = tuple(sorted(files))
   161→    cache.put(cache_key, result)
   162→    return result
   163→
   164→
   165→
   166→def find_source_files(
   167→    path: str | Path, extensions: list[str], exclusions: list[str] | None = None
   168→) -> list[str]:
   169→    """Find all files with given extensions under a path, excluding patterns."""
   170→    return list(
   171→        _find_source_files_cached(
   172→            str(path),
   173→            tuple(extensions),
   174→            tuple(exclusions) if exclusions else None,
   175→            get_exclusions(),
   176→        )
   177→    )
   178→
   179→
   180→def find_ts_files(path: str | Path) -> list[str]:
   181→    return find_source_files(path, [".ts", ".tsx"])
   182→
   183→
   184→def find_tsx_files(path: str | Path) -> list[str]:
   185→    return find_source_files(path, [".tsx"])
   186→
   187→
   188→def find_py_files(path: str | Path) -> list[str]:
   189→    return find_source_files(path, [".py"])
   190→
```

```
1→"""Registry for optional language hook modules consumed by detectors."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import importlib
     6→import logging
     7→import sys
     8→from collections import defaultdict
     9→
    10→_LANG_HOOKS: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = defaultdict(dict)
    11→_LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    12→
    13→
    14→def register_lang_hooks(
    15→    lang_name: str,
    16→    *,
    17→    test_coverage: object | None = None,
    18→) -> None:
    19→    """Register optional detector hook modules for a language."""
    20→    hooks = _LANG_HOOKS[lang_name]
    21→    if test_coverage is not None:
    22→        hooks["test_coverage"] = test_coverage
    23→
    24→
    25→def get_lang_hook(lang_name: str | None, hook_name: str) -> object | None:
    26→    """Get a previously-registered language hook module."""
    27→    if not lang_name:
    28→        return None
    29→
    30→    hook = _LANG_HOOKS.get(lang_name, {}).get(hook_name)
    31→    if hook is not None:
    32→        return hook
    33→
    34→    module_name = f"desloppify.languages.{lang_name}"
    35→    module = sys.modules.get(module_name)
    36→
    37→    # Lazy-load only the requested language package.
    38→    if module is None:
    39→        try:
    40→            importlib.import_module(module_name)
    41→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, RuntimeError, OSError) as exc:
    42→            _LOGGER.debug(
    43→                "Unable to import language hook package %s: %s", lang_name, exc
    44→            )
    45→            return None
    46→    elif lang_name not in _LANG_HOOKS:
    47→        try:
    48→            importlib.reload(module)
    49→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, RuntimeError, OSError) as exc:
    50→            _LOGGER.debug(
    51→                "Unable to reload language hook package %s: %s", lang_name, exc
    52→            )
    53→            return None
    54→
    55→    return _LANG_HOOKS.get(lang_name, {}).get(hook_name)
    56→
    57→
    58→def clear_lang_hooks_for_tests() -> None:
    59→    """Clear registry (test helper)."""
    60→    _LANG_HOOKS.clear()
    61→
    62→
    63→__all__ = [
    64→    "clear_lang_hooks_for_tests",
    65→    "get_lang_hook",
    66→    "register_lang_hooks",
    67→]
    68→
```

```
1→"""Runtime context helpers for command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    11→from desloppify.core.config import load_config
    12→
    13→
    14→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    15→class CommandRuntime:
    16→    """Explicit runtime dependencies shared by command handlers."""
    17→
    18→    config: dict[str, Any]
    19→    state: dict[str, Any]
    20→    state_path: Path | None
    21→
    22→
    23→def command_runtime(args) -> CommandRuntime:
    24→    """Return runtime context from explicit args.runtime or construct one."""
    25→    runtime = getattr(args, "runtime", None)
    26→    if isinstance(runtime, CommandRuntime):
    27→        return runtime
    28→
    29→    config = load_config()
    30→    state_file = state_path(args)
    31→    if isinstance(state_file, str):
    32→        state_file = Path(state_file)
    33→
    34→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    35→
    36→    return CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
    37→
    38→
    39→__all__ = ["CommandRuntime", "command_runtime"]
    40→
```

```
    28→def _detect(fixer: FixerConfig, path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    29→    print(colorize(f"\nDetecting {fixer.label}...", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    30→    entries = fixer.detect(path)
    31→    file_count = len(set(e["file"] for e in entries))
    32→    print(

    37→    )

    39→
    40→
    41→def _print_fix_summary(
    42→    fixer: FixerConfig,
    43→    results: list[dict],
    44→    total_items: int,
    45→    total_lines: int,
    46→    dry_run: bool,
    47→) -> None:
    48→    verb = fixer.dry_verb if dry_run else fixer.verb
    49→    lines_str = f" ({total_lines} lines)" if total_lines else ""
    50→    print(
    51→        colorize(
    52→            f"\n  {verb} {total_items} {fixer.label} across {len(results)} files{lines_str}\n",
    53→            "bold",
    54→        )
    55→    )
    56→    for r in results[:30]:
    57→        syms = ", ".join(r["removed"][:5])
    58→        if len(r["removed"]) > 5:
    59→            syms += f" (+{len(r['removed']) - 5})"
    60→        extra = f"  ({r['lines_removed']} lines)" if r.get("lines_removed") else ""
    61→        print(f"  {rel(r['file'])}{extra}  →  {syms}")
    62→    if len(results) > 30:
    63→        print(f"  ... and {len(results) - 30} more files")
    64→
    65→
```

```
1→"""State-path and scan-gating helpers for command modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import auto_detect_lang_name
     8→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import PROJECT_ROOT
     9→from desloppify.utils import colorize
    10→
    11→
    12→def _sole_existing_lang_state_file() -> Path | None:
    13→    """Return the only existing language-specific state file, if unambiguous."""
    14→    state_dir = PROJECT_ROOT / ".desloppify"
    15→    if not state_dir.exists():
    16→        return None
    17→    candidates = sorted(path for path in state_dir.glob("state-*.json") if path.is_file())
    18→    if len(candidates) == 1:
    19→        return candidates[0]
    20→    return None
    21→
    22→
    23→def _allow_lang_state_fallback(args) -> bool:
    24→    """Whether command can safely fallback to the sole existing lang state file."""
    25→    # Scan should always honor detected/explicit language mapping to avoid cross-lang merges.
    26→    return getattr(args, "command", None) != "scan"
    27→
    28→
    29→def state_path(args) -> Path | None:
    30→    """Get state file path from args, or None for default."""
    31→    path_arg = getattr(args, "state", None)
    32→    if path_arg:
    33→        return Path(path_arg)
    34→    lang_name = getattr(args, "lang", None)
    35→    if not lang_name:
    36→        lang_name = auto_detect_lang_name(args)
    37→    if lang_name:
    38→        resolved = PROJECT_ROOT / ".desloppify" / f"state-{lang_name}.json"
    39→        if resolved.exists() or not _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    40→            return resolved
    41→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    42→        if fallback is not None:
    43→            return fallback
    44→        return resolved
    45→
    46→    if _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    47→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    48→        if fallback is not None:
    49→            return fallback
    50→    return None
    51→
    52→
    53→def require_completed_scan(state: dict) -> bool:
    54→    """Return True when the state contains at least one completed scan."""
    55→    has_completed_scan = bool(state.get("last_scan"))
    56→    if not has_completed_scan:
    57→        print(colorize("No scans yet. Run: desloppify scan", "yellow"))
    58→    return has_completed_scan
    59→
```

```
1→"""Fix command option and fixer resolution helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import dataclasses
     6→import sys
     7→from collections.abc import Callable
     8→
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
    10→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import FixerConfig, LangConfig
    11→from desloppify.utils import colorize
    12→
    13→_COMMAND_POST_FIX: dict[str, Callable[..., None]] = {}
    14→
    15→
    16→def _load_fixer(args, fixer_name: str) -> tuple[LangConfig, FixerConfig]:
    17→    """Resolve fixer from language plugin registry, or exit."""
    18→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    19→    if not lang:
    20→        print(colorize("Could not detect language. Use --lang to specify.", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    21→        sys.exit(1)
    22→    if not lang.fixers:
    23→        print(colorize(f"No auto-fixers available for {lang.name}.", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    24→        sys.exit(1)
    25→    if fixer_name not in lang.fixers:
    26→        available = ", ".join(sorted(lang.fixers.keys()))
    27→        print(colorize(f"Unknown fixer: {fixer_name}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    28→        print(colorize(f"  Available: {available}", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    29→        sys.exit(1)
    30→    fc = lang.fixers[fixer_name]
    31→    if fixer_name in _COMMAND_POST_FIX and not fc.post_fix:
    32→        fc = dataclasses.replace(fc, post_fix=_COMMAND_POST_FIX[fixer_name])
    33→    return lang, fc
    34→
```

Perfect. Now I have enough information to provide a comprehensive analysis. Let me compile the findings:

## Coding Pattern Inconsistencies in Desloppify Source Directory

Based on a thorough examination of 20+ source files across different subsystems, I've identified the following coding pattern and style inconsistencies:

---

### 1. **Type Annotation Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: Mixed use of lowercase generics vs. capitalized typing module types**

- **Files**: Codebase-wide
- **Pattern**: Some modules use `dict[str, Any]` (PEP 585, Python 3.9+) while older or legacy code may still use `Dict[str, Any]`
- **Examples**:
  - `/desloppify/utils.py:117` uses `dict[str, tuple[str, str, bool]]` (lowercase)
  - `/desloppify/search.py:36` uses `dict[str, set[str]]` (lowercase)
  - But some TypedDicts defined use full `dict` syntax inconsistently with imports

**Inconsistency: Mixed Optional notation**

- **Files**: Across the codebase
- **Pattern**: Some functions use `X | None` (PEP 604, Python 3.10+) while others use `Optional[X]`
- **Examples**:
  - `/desloppify/cli.py:21` uses `list[str] | None` (modern syntax)
  - `/desloppify/utils.py:65` uses `str | None` (modern syntax)
  - But older TypedDict definitions may not have adopted this uniformly
  - `/desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py:22` uses `NotRequired` (newer pattern)

**Issue**: No consistent import of `Optional` vs relying on `|` operator everywhere.

---

### 2. **Private Function Naming Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: `_` prefix not uniformly applied to helper functions**

- **Files**: 
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py`: Many helper functions use `_` prefix (e.g., `_detect`, `_print_fix_summary`, `_apply_and_report`)
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py:12-26`: Similar pattern with `_sole_existing_lang_state_file`, `_allow_lang_state_fallback`
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/next.py`: Uses `_scorecard_subjective`, `_low_subjective_dimensions`, `_get_items`
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/config_cmd.py:30-78`: Uses `_config_show`, `_config_set`, `_config_unset`
  
**But**: Not all internal-only functions are consistently prefixed. Some public modules export functions that are logically private.

**Example of inconsistency**:
- `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/query.py:11` defines `write_query(data: dict)` as public
- But `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py` defines helper functions with `_` prefix consistently
- Different command modules adopt different conventions

---

### 3. **Dataclass vs TypedDict vs NamedTuple Inconsistency**

**Inconsistency: Multiple structured data patterns coexist without clear separation of concerns**

- **Dataclasses used for**:
  - `/desloppify/engine/detectors/base.py`: `FunctionInfo`, `ClassInfo`, `ComplexitySignal`, `GodRule` (immutable entities)
  - `/desloppify/languages/_framework/base/types.py`: `DetectorPhase`, `FixResult`, `LangConfig`, `FixerConfig` (configuration objects)
  - `/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_signals/auth.py:35-55`: `RouteAuthCoverage`, `AuthorizationSignals` (frozen dataclasses)

- **TypedDict used for**:
  - `/desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py:48+`: `Finding`, `TierStats`, `StateStats`, `DimensionScore` (persistent state)
  - `/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/core.py:41`: `WorkQueueResult` (return type)
  - `/desloppify/intelligence/narrative/types.py:26+`: `BadgeStatus`, `PrimaryAction`, `DimensionEntry` (narrative output)
  - `/desloppify/languages/_framework/base/types.py:51+`: `DetectorCoverageRecord`, `ScanCoverageRecord` (coverage metadata)

- **NamedTuple used for**:
  - `/desloppify/state.py:64`: `ScoreSnapshot` (single-use return value)

**Problem**: No clear principle distinguishing when to use each. Dataclasses are used both for configuration and domain entities. TypedDicts mix persistent state with temporary computation results.

---

### 4. **Import Style Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: Module aliasing patterns differ across codebase**

- **Common pattern 1** (module alias for disambiguation):
  - `/desloppify/cli.py:8`: `from desloppify import file_discovery as file_discovery_mod`
  - `/desloppify/cli.py:9`: `from desloppify import utils as utils_mod`
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/lang.py:11`: `from desloppify import languages as lang_api`
  
- **Common pattern 2** (star imports):
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/status.py:6`: `from desloppify.app.commands.status_cmd import *  # noqa: F401,F403`
  - This violates the explicit import convention used elsewhere

- **Common pattern 3** (nested module imports):
  - `/desloppify/cli.py:16`: `from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import PROJECT_ROOT`
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py:21`: `from desloppify.core.runtime_state import current_runtime_context`
  - Mix of `_internal` sub-packages and public APIs

**Issue**: Some commands use wildcard imports while others meticulously list each import. The `as mod` pattern for module aliases is helpful but not uniformly applied.

---

### 5. **Error Handling Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: Mixed error-reporting patterns (exit vs exception vs return None)**

- **sys.exit() pattern**:
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/fix/options.py:20-29`: Multiple `sys.exit(1)` calls for validation errors
  - `_load_fixer()` function exits directly on error

- **Exception raising pattern**:
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/lang.py:21-32`: `LangResolutionError` (inherits from `SystemExit`)
  - Designed to print clean error without traceback (caught at CLI top-level)

- **Return None pattern**:
  - `/desloppify/utils.py:137`: `find_installed_skill()` returns `SkillInstall | None`
  - `/desloppify/utils.py:162`: `check_skill_version()` returns `str | None`
  - `/desloppify/search.py:12`: `read_file_text(filepath: str) -> str | None`

- **Boolean return pattern**:
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py:53-58`: `require_completed_scan()` returns `bool` and prints side effects

**Problem**: No consistent pattern. Some functions raise exceptions, some exit directly, some return None, some return bool with side effects.

---

### 6. **Callback/Dependency Injection Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: Mixed approaches to passing dependencies**

- **Dataclass bundle pattern**:
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/runtime.py:14-20`: `CommandRuntime` dataclass bundles `config`, `state`, `state_path`
  - `/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_signals/auth.py:84-88`: Functions accept `rel_fn: Callable[[str], str]` as parameter

- **Loose kwargs pattern**:
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/fix/options.py:16`: `_load_fixer(args, fixer_name: str)` accepts bare `args`
  - Many command handlers accept `args: argparse.Namespace` directly

- **Post-fix hooks pattern**:
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/fix/options.py:13`: `_COMMAND_POST_FIX: dict[str, Callable[..., None]]` — global mutable registry
  - Uses `dataclasses.replace()` to inject post-fix callbacks (`/desloppify/app/commands/fix/options.py:32`)

**Problem**: Some modules use explicit dataclass dependency bundles (`CommandRuntime`), while others pass raw argparse args or use global mutable registries (`_COMMAND_POST_FIX`).

---

### 7. **String Formatting Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: Mix of f-strings, .format(), and % formatting**

- **F-strings** (modern, preferred):
  - `/desloppify/cli.py:49`: `f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}"`
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py:145`: `rel_dir + "/" + d` (string concatenation instead)

- **.format()** (older style):
  - Less common in examined code, but present in some older modules

- **String concatenation** (implicit formatting):
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py:145`: Uses `rel_dir + "/" + d` instead of f-string
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py:57`: Uses `r['file']` dictionary access with implicit formatting

**Problem**: Inconsistent modernization. Most code uses f-strings, but some areas still use manual string concatenation or outdated formatting methods.

---

### 8. **Path Handling Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: Mix of `Path` objects vs `str` paths**

- **Path objects used in**:
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py:29-50`: Returns `Path | None`
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py:166-178`: Accepts `path: str | Path`

- **String paths used in**:
  - `/desloppify/core/file_paths.py:41-55`: Functions work with `str` paths (`rel()`, `resolve_path()`)
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/fix/cmd.py:31`: `path = Path(args.path)` — converts string to Path locally

- **Mixed parameter types**:
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py:166`: `find_source_files(path: str | Path, ...)`
  - But internally converts to string for caching (`/desloppify/file_discovery.py:171-176`)

**Problem**: Some functions accept both `str` and `Path`, requiring internal conversions. Others work exclusively with strings. No consistent policy.

---

### 9. **Naming Convention Inconsistencies (Suffixes)**

**Inconsistency: Function suffix conventions vary**

- **`_fn` suffix**: Not observed
- **`_func` suffix**: Not observed
- **`_callback` suffix**: Not observed (uses bare names instead)
- **Verb-based naming**:
  - `get_`, `find_`, `load_`, `resolve_`, `build_`, `compute_`, `check_` — all used throughout
  - But `detect_`, `extract_`, `parse_`, `process_` also used for similar purposes
  - Example: `/desloppify/core/registry.py:350-352`: `detector_names()`, `display_order()` (getters with no `get_` prefix)

- **Inconsistent suffixes for similar operations**:
  - Load operations: `load_state()`, `load_config()`, `find_installed_skill()` (should be `load_installed_skill()`?)
  - Detection/extraction: `detect_with_bandit()`, `extract_functions()`, `extract_py_functions()`

**Problem**: No naming convention for types of operations. Functions performing similar tasks use inconsistent names.

---

### 10. **Default Value and Guard Clause Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: Default/None handling patterns vary**

- **Explicit None checks**:
  - `/desloppify/utils.py:141`: `if not full.is_file():`
  - `/desloppify/engine/policy/zones.py:241`: `if zone_map is None:`

- **Truthy checks**:
  - `/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py:61`: `if not scope:` (could be None or empty)
  - `/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py:176`: `if not dim_scores:` (could be None or empty dict)

- **Type guards**:
  - `/desloppify/engine/concerns.py:68`: `if not isinstance(entry, dict):`
  - `/desloppify/engine/_work_queue/helpers.py:130`: `if not isinstance(caps, dict):`

**Problem**: Inconsistent use of `is None` vs falsy checks. Some code explicitly checks type before using, others assumes truthy values mean "valid".

---

### 11. **Constant Definition Inconsistencies**

- **Module-level constants**:
  - `/desloppify/utils.py:100-104`: Constants as module variables (`SKILL_VERSION`, `SKILL_VERSION_RE`)
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py:43-65`: `DEFAULT_EXCLUSIONS` as frozenset
  - `/desloppify/core/file_paths.py`: No constants (uses functions directly)

- **No clear convention for whether to use all-caps or CamelCase for class constants vs module constants**.

---

### 12. **Module Organization: Public vs Internal APIs**

**Inconsistency: Facade vs implementation module patterns**

- **Facade pattern** (recommended):
  - `/desloppify/state.py`: Pure re-export facade over `engine._state` submodules
  - `/desloppify/scoring.py`: Pure re-export facade over `engine._scoring` submodules
  - `/desloppify/utils.py`: Compatibility facade with helper functions

- **Direct implementation** (some older modules):
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py`: Mix of facade and direct helpers
  - `/desloppify/cli.py`: Direct CLI logic (not a facade)

**Problem**: Not all modules follow the facade pattern, making API organization inconsistent.

---

### 13. **Mutable Global State Pattern Inconsistencies**

**Inconsistency: How mutable state is managed**

- **Runtime context pattern**:
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py:70-72`: `set_exclusions()` modifies `current_runtime_context().exclusions`
  - `/desloppify/file_discovery.py:86-87`: `enable_file_cache()` modifies runtime state

- **Module-level globals**:
  - `/desloppify/app/commands/fix/options.py:13`: `_COMMAND_POST_FIX: dict[str, Callable[..., None]]` — mutable global registry
  - `/desloppify/hook_registry.py:10`: `_LANG_HOOKS: dict[str, dict[str, object]]` — mutable global registry

- **Frozen dataclasses**:
  - `/desloppify/intelligence/review/context_signals/auth.py:35`: Uses `@dataclass(frozen=True)` to prevent mutation

**Problem**: Some modules use runtime context injection (cleaner), others use module-level mutable globals (less testable).

---

### Summary Table of Key Inconsistencies

| Category | File Examples | Issue |
|----------|-------|--------|
| **Type Annotations** | `utils.py`, `schema.py`, `types.py` | Mixed `dict[str, X]` vs `Dict[str, X]`; `X \| None` vs `Optional[X]` |
| **Private Functions** | `apply_flow.py` vs `config_cmd.py` | Inconsistent `_` prefix usage for internal helpers |
| **Data Structures** | `base.py`, `schema.py`, `types.py` | Dataclass vs TypedDict vs NamedTuple without clear separation |
| **Imports** | `cli.py`, `status.py` | Mix of `as mod` aliases, wildcard imports, inconsistent nesting |
| **Error Handling** | `options.py`, `lang.py`, `utils.py` | `sys.exit()`, exceptions, `None` returns, bool returns with side effects |
| **Dependencies** | `runtime.py`, `options.py` | Bundle dataclasses vs bare args vs global registries |
| **String Formatting** | `cli.py`, `apply_flow.py` | F-strings, .format(), string concatenation mixed |
| **Paths** | `file_discovery.py`, `file_paths.py` | `str` vs `Path` objects inconsistently handled |
| **Naming** | `state.py`, `registry.py` | No suffix convention; verb prefixes vary (`get_`, `find_`, `load_`, `detect_`, `extract_`) |
| **Default Handling** | `zones.py`, `concerns.py` | `is None` vs falsy checks vs type guards |
| **Module Organization** | `state.py`, `cli.py` | Facade pattern in some modules, direct implementation in others |
| **Global Mutable State** | `options.py`, `hook_registry.py` | Mix of runtime context vs module-level globals |

```
   369→def dimension_action_type(dim_name: str) -> str:
   370→    """Return a compact action type label for a dimension based on its detectors."""
   371→    best = "manual"
   372→    best_pri = 99
   373→    for d in DETECTORS.values():
   374→        if d.dimension == dim_name:
   375→            pri = _ACTION_PRIORITY.get(d.action_type, 99)
   376→            if pri < best_pri:
   377→                best_pri = pri
   378→                best = d.action_type
   379→    return _ACTION_LABELS.get(best, "manual")

   383→    """Build detector tool metadata keyed by detector name."""
   384→    result = {}

   388→            "action_type": d.action_type,

   392→        if d.guidance:
   393→            entry["guidance"] = d.guidance
   394→        result[name] = entry
   395→    return result
```
