Perfect! Now I have comprehensive coverage. Let me compile the report based on all the information I've gathered:

## Analysis Summary

Based on my thorough investigation of the desloppify codebase, here is a comprehensive report of what's currently detected and the gaps that explain why certain issues weren't flagged:

---

## 1. **DETECTOR REGISTRY** (desloppify/core/registry.py)

**Registered detectors (42 total):**

Organized by action type:
- **Auto-fix (3)**: unused, logs, smells
- **Reorganize/Move (7)**: orphaned, uncalled_functions, flat_dirs, naming, single_use, coupling, facade
- **Refactor (13)**: structural, props, react, dupes, patterns, dict_keys, test_coverage, signature, responsibility_cohesion, boilerplate_duplication, private_imports, layer_violation, signature
- **Manual fix (5)**: exports, deprecated, stale_exclude, security, global_mutable_config
- **Subjective review (2)**: review, subjective_review, concerns

---

## 2. **MECHANICAL DIMENSIONS** (desloppify/engine/_scoring/policy/core.py)

The scoring system defines 5 mechanical dimensions:

| Dimension | Detectors | Tier Weight |
|-----------|-----------|-------------|
| **File health** | structural | 2.0x |
| **Code quality** | unused, logs, exports, deprecated, props, smells, react, dict_keys, global_mutable_config, orphaned, flat_dirs, naming, facade, stale_exclude, patterns, single_use, coupling, responsibility_cohesion, private_imports, layer_violation | 1.0x |
| **Duplication** | dupes, boilerplate_duplication | 1.0x |
| **Test health** | test_coverage, subjective_review | 1.0x |
| **Security** | security, cycles | 1.0x |

---

## 3. **SUBJECTIVE DIMENSIONS** (desloppify/languages/_framework/review_data/dimensions.json)

**20 default dimensions** evaluated by AI code review:

**Per-file dimensions:**
1. `naming_quality` - Names vs. behavior mismatch, generic verbs
2. `logic_clarity` - Dead code, always-true/false conditions, async-with-no-await
3. `type_safety` - Type annotations mismatching runtime behavior
4. `contract_coherence` - Function contracts (names, params, return types) matching behavior
5. `error_consistency` - Consistent error handling strategies
6. `abstraction_fitness` - Pass-through wrappers, leaky abstractions
7. `api_surface_coherence` - Consistent API shapes, mixed sync/async
8. `naming_quality` - Intent-revealing names

**Holistic (cross-module) dimensions:**
9. `high_level_elegance` - Decomposition coherence, ownership clarity
10. `mid_level_elegance` - Boundary handoffs, data translation
11. `low_level_elegance` - Control flow directness, state mutation bounds
12. `cross_module_architecture` - Dependency direction, cycles, hub modules
13. `initialization_coupling` - Import-time side effects, boot-order dependencies
14. `convention_outlier` - Naming/style consistency drift
15. `dependency_health` - Unused/overlapping dependencies
16. `test_strategy` - Coverage of critical paths, test fragility
17. `authorization_consistency` - Uniform auth patterns
18. `incomplete_migration` - Old+new APIs coexisting
19. `package_organization` - Directory structure vs. change boundaries
20. `design_coherence` - Multi-responsibility functions, deep nesting, abstraction clarity
21. `ai_generated_debt` - LLM-hallmark patterns (restating comments, over-defensive code)

---

## 4. **STRUCTURAL COMPLEXITY DETECTORS** (desloppify/languages/python/detectors/complexity.py)

**Python signals:**
- `compute_max_params()` - Flags functions with >7 real parameters
- `compute_nesting_depth()` - Flags max indentation depth >4 (5+ nested blocks)
- `compute_long_functions()` - Flags functions >80 LOC

**TypeScript complexity** (similar signals via tree-sitter)

---

## 5. **CODE SMELL DETECTORS**

### Python (desloppify/languages/python/detectors/smells.py):

**Regex-based (28):**
- eval/exec usage, magic numbers, TODO/FIXME, hardcoded URLs, debug tags
- Star imports without `__all__`, empty except blocks, swallowed errors
- Monster functions (>150 LOC), dead functions (pass/return-only)
- Deferred imports, subprocess without timeout, lru_cache on mutable state
- Unsafe file writes, duplicate constants, unreachable code
- Constant returns, ReDoS-risk regex, comment-stripping awareness issues
- Callback logging, hardcoded path separators, vestigial parameters
- Non-op functions, stderr traceback bypasses, sys.path/env mutations at import time
- sys.exit in library code, silent exception handlers
- Optional parameter sprawl, loose type annotations

### TypeScript/React (desloppify/languages/typescript/detectors/smells.py):

**Regex-based (15):**
- Empty catch blocks, explicit `any` types, @ts-ignore, @ts-nocheck
- Non-null assertions, hardcoded colors/RGB, magic numbers
- Hardcoded URLs, TODO/FIXME, debug tags, workaround tags
- as any casts, .sort() without comparator, dead symbols (void-suppressed)

**Multi-line analysis (brace-tracked):**
- `async_no_await` - async functions without await statements
- `console_error_no_throw` - console.error without throw/return
- `empty_if_chain` - Empty if/else chains
- `dead_useeffect` - useEffect with empty body (React-specific)
- `swallowed_error` - Catch that only logs
- `monster_function` - >150 LOC
- `dead_function` - Body is empty/return-only
- `window_global` - window.__* escape hatches
- `catch_return_default` - Catch block returns default (silent failure)
- `switch_no_default` - Switch statements without default
- CSS monoliths (>300 LOC), !important overuse

**React-specific (desloppify/languages/typescript/detectors/react.py):**
- `useState+useEffect state sync` - useEffect only calling setState (causes extra render)
- `context_nesting` - >5 levels of nested React Context providers
- `hook_bloat` - (likely hooks with too many dependencies)
- `provider_nesting` - Deep Provider trees

---

## 6. **COVERAGE GAPS** — Issues NOT Currently Detected

Based on your exploration findings, here are the major gaps:

### **A. Monolithic Functions (PARTIALLY covered)**

**What's detected:**
- Functions >80 LOC (Python complexity signal)
- Functions >150 LOC (Python & TS smells)
- Nesting depth >4 (Python complexity)
- Max parameters >7 (Python)

**What's NOT detected:**
- **"Monolithic but under threshold"** — Functions with 60-80 LOC but doing many unrelated things
- **Responsibility mixing** — Multiple distinct tasks in <80 LOC (cyclomatic complexity not measured)
- **Decision density** — High density of if/switch statements without extraction
- **Evidence gap**: `design_coherence` subjective dimension asks for this, but only via AI review, not mechanical detection

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **cyclomatic complexity** or **decision density**
- **Type**: New mechanical detector or enhancement to `smells`
- **Effort**: Moderate (need tree-sitter AST analysis to count conditional branches)
- **Implementation**: Count if/switch/ternary/&&/|| branches; flag when ratio > ~5 per 40 LOC

---

### **B. Nested Closures & Callback Hell (NOT covered)**

**What's detected:**
- Empty if chains (TS only)
- async-no-await (TS only)
- General nesting depth (but doesn't focus on callbacks)

**What's NOT detected:**
- **Promise chains** (.then().then().then()) — no callback depth analysis
- **Nested function definitions** (functions defined inside functions) — no tracking
- **Closure chains** (function returning function returning function) — no nesting analysis beyond indentation
- **Callback parameter depth** (functions taking callbacks that take callbacks) — no parameter-type analysis
- **Evidence gap**: These issues are expected to appear in `logic_clarity` or `low_level_elegance`, but no mechanical signal triggers review focus

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **callback nesting depth** and **promise chain length**
- **Type**: New smell detectors in TS plugin
- **Effort**: Moderate (tree-sitter AST to track function calls and closures)
- **Implementation**:
  - Count consecutive .then() calls; flag chains >3
  - Count function-returning-function nesting; flag >2 levels
  - Flag functions with >2 levels of nested function definitions

---

### **C. Inconsistent Patterns & Error Handling (PARTIALLY covered)**

**What's detected:**
- `error_consistency` subjective dimension (AI review only)
- `swallowed_error` smell (catch blocks that only log)
- `empty_except` (Python only)
- `silent_except` (Python only)
- `catch_return_default` (TS only)

**What's NOT detected:**
- **Mixed error strategies** within same module (some functions throw, others return null/Result) — no detector
- **Error context loss** (catch-rethrow without wrapping) — no detector
- **Inconsistent error types** (custom errors vs. bare strings) — no detector
- **Null vs. Exception inconsistency** (some APIs return null, siblings throw) — no detector
- **Evidence gap**: Covered only by subjective `error_consistency` dimension; no mechanical signal flags it

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **error strategy inconsistency**
- **Type**: New mechanical detector (pattern-based, no fix)
- **Effort**: Moderate-High (need cross-module analysis)
- **Implementation**:
  - Per-file: identify error strategy (throw, return null, return Result<T, E>, etc.)
  - Flag files where siblings use different strategies
  - Example: `api/users.ts throws`, `api/posts.ts returns null` — flag inconsistency

---

### **D. Inconsistent Naming Conventions (PARTIALLY covered)**

**What's detected:**
- `convention_outlier` subjective dimension (AI review only)
- `naming_quality` subjective dimension (AI review only)
- No mechanical detector for style drift

**What's NOT detected:**
- **snake_case vs. camelCase drift** across modules — no linter-level flag
- **Verb inconsistency** (get vs. fetch vs. load for same semantic operation) — no detector
- **Boolean naming** (is/has/can prefix inconsistency) — no detector
- **Evidence gap**: Detected only by AI review; no automation flags it for enforcement

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **naming convention drift**
- **Type**: New linter rule (mechanical enforcement)
- **Effort**: Low-Moderate (regex/AST pattern matching)
- **Implementation**:
  - Establish baseline (count camelCase vs. snake_case functions in first N files)
  - Flag outliers (e.g., find_users in camelCase codebase)
  - Flag verb inconsistency (get_user, fetch_post, load_item in same codebase)

---

### **E. Mixed Patterns (Object Creation, Data Flow, etc.) (NOT covered)**

**What's detected:**
- `patterns` detector (refactor category, but no mechanical implementation)
- `dupes` detector (duplication)
- No specific pattern tracking

**What's NOT detected:**
- **Object creation inconsistency** (constructor vs. factory vs. builder across modules) — no detector
- **Data flow pattern inconsistency** (pure functions vs. side effects) — no detector
- **Configuration pattern drift** (env vars vs. config files vs. DI) — no detector
- **Evidence gap**: `patterns` detector exists but has no mechanical implementation; relies on manual detection

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **design pattern consistency**
- **Type**: New mechanical detector (pattern library-based)
- **Effort**: High (requires domain-specific pattern library)
- **Implementation**: Define patterns (factory, singleton, builder), detect usage across codebase, flag inconsistency

---

### **F. Parameter List Bloat & Wide APIs (PARTIALLY covered)**

**What's detected:**
- `compute_max_params()` — flags >7 parameters
- `optional_param_sprawl` smell — too many optional params
- `api_surface_coherence` subjective dimension (AI review)

**What's NOT detected:**
- **Config/options object usage inconsistency** (some functions use options bag, others don't) — no detector
- **Variadic parameter abuse** (functions accepting ...args) — no detector
- **Callback-heavy APIs** (many callback parameters) — no detector
- **Evidence gap**: Mechanical detection covers >7 hard params, but not parameter *types* or *patterns*

**Recommendation**: Enhance existing parameter detectors
- **Type**: Enhancement to `compute_max_params()`
- **Effort**: Low-Moderate
- **Implementation**:
  - Flag functions with many callback/function parameters
  - Flag functions with >3 optional parameters grouped by theme (should be one options object)

---

### **G. Type Safety Gaps (PARTIALLY covered)**

**What's detected:**
- `type_safety` subjective dimension (AI review)
- `ts_ignore`, `ts_nocheck` (TS smells)
- `non_null_assert`, `as_any_cast` (TS smells)
- `annotation_quality` (Python smell)

**What's NOT detected:**
- **any type usage patterns** (count of `any` in hot paths) — no aggregation
- **Type assertion clusters** (multiple as any in same function) — no detector
- **Generic parameter inference failures** (explicit type params needed) — no detector
- **Return type narrowing opportunities** (Union<X, Y, Z> could be discriminated) — no detector
- **Evidence gap**: Symptoms detected, but root patterns not flagged

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **type safety anti-patterns**
- **Type**: Enhancement to TS smells
- **Effort**: Low-Moderate
- **Implementation**:
  - Count `any` occurrences per file; flag high density (>10% of annotations)
  - Detect `as any` near error-prone operations (network, parsing, user input)
  - Suggest tagged unions over Union types

---

### **H. Initialization & Module-Level Code (PARTIALLY covered)**

**What's detected:**
- `initialization_coupling` subjective dimension (AI review)
- `import_path_mutation`, `import_env_mutation`, `import_runtime_init` (Python smells)
- `deferred_import` (Python smell — possible circular import workaround)

**What's NOT detected:**
- **Lazy initialization chains** (function A depends on B being initialized first) — no detector
- **Conditional imports** (if DEBUG: import X) — flagged only if obvious
- **Dynamic import paths** (import(variable)) — no detector
- **Module-level mutable state** (exposed, not just created) — partial coverage via `global_mutable_config`
- **Evidence gap**: Some symptoms detected, but initialization order dependencies not tracked

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **initialization order violations**
- **Type**: New mechanical detector (flow-sensitive, hard)
- **Effort**: High (requires control-flow analysis)
- **Implementation**: Build import dependency graph; detect cycles and deep chains

---

### **I. Cyclomatic Complexity & Control Flow Density (NOT covered)**

**What's detected:**
- Nesting depth >4 (Python)
- Monster functions >150 LOC
- Dead code after unconditional return

**What's NOT detected:**
- **Cyclomatic complexity** (count of independent code paths) — no measurement
- **Deep ternary chains** (a ? b : c ? d : e ? f : g) — no detector
- **Multiple if statements** without extraction — no aggregation
- **Switch statement complexity** (many cases) — no detector
- **Evidence gap**: Nesting depth caught, but complexity ignored if spread horizontally

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **cyclomatic complexity**
- **Type**: New mechanical detector
- **Effort**: Moderate (tree-sitter AST analysis)
- **Implementation**: Count decision points (if, switch, ?:, ||, &&, catch); flag when >10 per function

---

### **J. Duplication Specifics (PARTIALLY covered)**

**What's detected:**
- `dupes` detector (refactor category)
- `duplicate_constant` (Python smell)
- `boilerplate_duplication` (refactor category)

**What's NOT detected:**
- **Structural duplication** (similar class/function shapes across modules) — not measured
- **Boilerplate imports** (identical import blocks) — flagged under AI review only
- **Copy-paste error patterns** (near-identical blocks with suspicious variations) — no detector
- **Evidence gap**: Exact duplication detected, but structural/pattern duplication ignored

**Recommendation**: Add detector for **structural duplication**
- **Type**: Enhancement to `dupes`
- **Effort**: High (requires semantic similarity analysis or template detection)
- **Implementation**: Normalize code (remove comments, rename variables) and detect similar AST shapes

---

### **K. Dead Code & Reachability (PARTIALLY covered)**

**What's detected:**
- `unreachable_code` (Python — code after unconditional return)
- `dead_function` (functions with only pass/return)
- `dead_useeffect` (TS React)
- `uncalled_functions` (refactor detector)

**What's NOT detected:**
- **Dead branches** (if conditions always true/false, but no unconditional return) — no detector
- **Unreferenced exports** (exports no longer imported) — likely caught by `exports` detector
- **Orphaned test cases** (test functions not in test runner) — no detector
- **Unreachable catch blocks** (catch after catch without narrowing) — no detector
- **Evidence gap**: Some patterns caught, but edge cases missed

**Recommendation**: Enhance `unreachable_code` detector
- **Type**: Enhancement
- **Effort**: Low-Moderate
- **Implementation**: Detect always-true/false conditions; flag unreachable branches

---

## Summary Table: Gap Analysis

| Issue Category | Current Coverage | Gap Severity | Best Fix Type | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Monolithic functions | Partial (size only) | Medium | Cyclomatic complexity detector | Moderate |
| Nested closures | None | High | Callback nesting depth detector | Moderate |
| Callback hell | None | High | Promise chain length detector | Moderate |
| Error handling inconsistency | Partial (AI only) | High | Error strategy detector | Moderate-High |
| Naming convention drift | Partial (AI only) | Medium | Convention analyzer | Low-Moderate |
| Mixed patterns | None | Medium | Pattern consistency detector | High |
| Parameter bloat patterns | Partial (count only) | Low | Parameter type analyzer | Low-Moderate |
| Type safety gaps | Partial | Medium | Type safety anti-pattern detector | Low-Moderate |
| Initialization order | Partial (AI only) | Medium | Dependency order analyzer | High |
| Cyclomatic complexity | None | High | Complexity analyzer | Moderate |
| Duplication (structural) | Partial (exact only) | Medium | Semantic similarity detector | High |
| Dead code (branches) | Partial | Low | Control flow analyzer | Low-Moderate |

---

## Recommendations for Priority

**High-Impact, Lower-Effort Additions:**
1. **Cyclomatic complexity detector** — Catches the "monolithic but fast" functions
2. **Callback nesting depth** (TS) — Flags promise chains and closure nesting
3. **Error strategy consistency** — Mechanical detector for mixed error handling
4. **Convention drift analyzer** — Automates naming consistency

**Medium-Impact, Higher-Effort:**
5. **Promise chain length** (TS) — Specific to async patterns
6. **Initialization order analyzer** — Complex but valuable for boot-order fragility
7. **Parameter pattern analyzer** — Detects callback-heavy and overly-optional APIs

**Lower Priority (Covered by AI Review or Complex):**
8. Mixed patterns (best handled by subjective review for domain specifics)
9. Structural duplication (requires semantic similarity, best for custom projects)

```
1→"""Python complexity signal compute functions.
     2→
     3→Used by ComplexitySignal definitions in __init__.py for the structural phase.
     4→"""
     5→
     6→import re
     7→from math import gcd
     8→
     9→
    10→def compute_max_params(content: str, lines: list[str]) -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    11→    """Find the function with the most parameters. Returns (count, label) or None."""
    12→    def_re = re.compile(r"def\s+\w+\s*\(", re.MULTILINE)
    13→    max_params = 0
    14→    for m in def_re.finditer(content):
    15→        # Track paren depth to find matching close-paren (handles nested parens)
    16→        depth = 1
    17→        start = m.end()
    18→        i = start
    19→        while i < len(content) and depth > 0:
    20→            ch = content[i]
    21→            if ch == "(":
    22→                depth += 1
    23→            elif ch == ")":
    24→                depth -= 1
    25→            i += 1
    26→        if depth != 0:
    27→            continue
    28→        param_str = content[start : i - 1]
    29→        params = [p.strip() for p in param_str.split(",") if p.strip()]
    30→        real_params = [
    31→            p for p in params if p not in ("self", "cls") and not p.startswith("*")
    32→        ]
    33→        if len(real_params) > max_params:
    34→            max_params = len(real_params)
    35→    if max_params > 7:
    36→        return max_params, f"function with {max_params} params"
    37→    return None
    38→
    39→
    40→def _detect_indent_unit(lines: list[str]) -> int:
    41→    """Detect indentation unit from file content using GCD of indentations."""
    42→    indents = set()
    43→    for line in lines:
    44→        stripped = line.lstrip()
    45→        if stripped and not stripped.startswith("#"):
    46→            indent = len(line) - len(stripped)
    47→            if 0 < indent <= 16:
    48→                indents.add(indent)
    49→    if not indents:
    50→        return 4
    51→    unit = 0
    52→    for i in indents:
    53→        unit = gcd(unit, i)
    54→    return max(unit, 1)
    55→
    56→
    57→def compute_nesting_depth(content: str, lines: list[str]) -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    58→    """Find maximum nesting depth by indentation. Returns (depth, label) or None."""
    59→    indent_unit = _detect_indent_unit(lines)
    60→    max_depth = 0
    61→    for line in lines:
    62→        stripped = line.lstrip()
    63→        if not stripped or stripped.startswith("#"):
    64→            continue
    65→        indent = len(line) - len(stripped)
    66→        depth = indent // indent_unit
    67→        if depth > max_depth:
    68→            max_depth = depth
    69→    if max_depth > 4:
    70→        return max_depth, f"nesting depth {max_depth}"
    71→    return None
    72→
    73→
    74→def compute_long_functions(content: str, lines: list[str]) -> tuple[int, str] | None:
    75→    """Find functions >80 LOC. Returns (longest_loc, label) or None."""
    76→    results = []
    77→    fn_re = re.compile(r"^(\s*)def\s+(\w+)")
    78→
    79→    i = 0
    80→    while i < len(lines):
    81→        m = fn_re.match(lines[i])
    82→        if not m:
    83→            i += 1
    84→            continue
    85→
    86→        fn_indent = len(m.group(1))
    87→        fn_name = m.group(2)
    88→        fn_start = i
    89→
    90→        j = i + 1
    91→        while j < len(lines):
    92→            if lines[j].strip() == "":
    93→                j += 1
    94→                continue
    95→            line_indent = len(lines[j]) - len(lines[j].lstrip())
    96→            if line_indent <= fn_indent and lines[j].strip():
    97→                break
    98→            j += 1
    99→
   100→        fn_loc = j - fn_start
   101→        if fn_loc > 80:
   102→            results.append((fn_name, fn_loc))
   103→        i = j
   104→
   105→    if results:
   106→        longest = max(results, key=lambda x: x[1])
   107→        return longest[1], f"long function ({longest[0]}: {longest[1]} LOC)"
   108→    return None
   109→
```

```
   173→
   174→
   175→def _build_string_line_set(lines: list[str]) -> set[int]:
   176→    """Build a set of 0-indexed line numbers that are inside multi-line strings.
   177→
   178→    Tracks triple-quote state across lines so regex-based checks can skip
   179→    lines that are inside multi-line string literals.
   180→    """
   181→    in_multiline: str | None = None  # '"""' or "'''" or None
   182→    string_lines: set[int] = set()
   183→
   184→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   185→        if in_multiline is not None:
   186→            string_lines.add(i)
   187→            # Check if this line closes the multi-line string
   188→            if in_multiline in line:
   189→                # Find the closing triple-quote (skip escaped ones)
   190→                pos = 0
   191→                while pos < len(line):
   192→                    idx = line.find(in_multiline, pos)
   193→                    if idx == -1:
   194→                        break
   195→                    # Check it's not escaped
   196→                    backslashes = 0
   197→                    j = idx - 1
   198→                    while j >= 0 and line[j] == "\\":
   199→                        backslashes += 1
   200→                        j -= 1
   201→                    if backslashes % 2 == 0:
   202→                        in_multiline = None
   203→                        break
   204→                    pos = idx + 3
   205→            continue
   206→
   207→        # Check if this line opens a multi-line string
   208→        pos = 0
   209→        while pos < len(line):
   210→            ch = line[pos]
   211→            if ch == "#":
   212→                break  # Rest is comment
   213→            # Skip string prefixes
   214→            if ch in ("r", "b", "f", "u", "R", "B", "F", "U") and pos + 1 < len(line):
   215→                next_ch = line[pos + 1]
   216→                if next_ch in ('"', "'"):
   217→                    pos += 1
   218→                    ch = next_ch
   219→                elif (
   220→                    next_ch in ("r", "b", "f", "R", "B", "F")
   221→                    and pos + 2 < len(line)
   222→                    and line[pos + 2] in ('"', "'")
   223→                ):
   224→                    pos += 2
   225→                    ch = line[pos]
   226→            if ch in ('"', "'"):
   227→                triple = line[pos : pos + 3]
   228→                if triple in ('"""', "'''"):
   229→                    # Check if it closes on the same line
   230→                    close_idx = line.find(triple, pos + 3)
   231→                    if close_idx == -1:
   232→                        # Opens a multi-line string
   233→                        in_multiline = triple
   234→                        break
   235→                    else:
   236→                        pos = close_idx + 3
   237→                        continue
   238→                else:
   239→                    # Single-line string — skip to closing quote
   240→                    end = line.find(ch, pos + 1)
   241→                    while end != -1 and end > 0 and line[end - 1] == "\\":
   242→                        end = line.find(ch, end + 1)
   243→                    pos = (end + 1) if end != -1 else len(line)
   244→                    continue
   245→            pos += 1
   246→
   247→    return string_lines
   248→
   249→
   250→def _match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
   251→    """Check if a regex match position falls inside a string literal or comment on a single line."""
   252→    i, in_string = 0, None
   253→    while i < len(line):
   254→        if i == match_start:
   255→            return in_string is not None
   256→        ch = line[i]
   257→        if in_string is None:
   258→            if ch == "#":
   259→                return True  # In a comment, not real code
   260→            triple = line[i : i + 3]
   261→            if triple in ('"""', "'''"):
   262→                in_string = triple
   263→                i += 3
   264→                continue
   265→            if (
   266→                ch in ("r", "b", "f")
   267→                and i + 1 < len(line)
   268→                and line[i + 1] in ('"', "'")
   269→            ):
   270→                i += 1
   271→                ch = line[i]
   272→            if ch in ('"', "'"):
   273→                in_string = ch
   274→                i += 1
   275→                continue
   276→        else:
   277→            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
   278→                i += 2
   279→                continue
   280→            if in_string in ('"""', "'''"):
   281→                if line[i : i + 3] == in_string:
   282→                    in_string = None
   283→                    i += 3
   284→                    continue
   285→            elif ch == in_string:
   286→                in_string = None
   287→                i += 1
   288→                continue
   289→        i += 1
   290→    return in_string is not None
   291→
   292→
   293→def detect_smells(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   294→    """Detect Python code smell patterns. Returns (entries, total_files_checked)."""
   295→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {s["id"]: [] for s in SMELL_CHECKS}
   296→    files = find_py_files(path)
   297→    # Collect module-level constants for cross-file duplicate detection
   298→    constants_by_key: dict[tuple[str, str], list[tuple[str, int]]] = {}
   299→
   300→    for filepath in files:
   301→        try:
   302→            p = (
   303→                Path(filepath)
   304→                if Path(filepath).is_absolute()
   305→                else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
   306→            )
   307→            content = p.read_text()
   308→            lines = content.splitlines()
   309→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
   310→            log_best_effort_failure(
   311→                logger, f"read Python file for smell scan {filepath}", exc
   312→            )
   313→            continue
   314→
   315→        if _is_test_path(filepath):
   316→            continue
   317→
   318→        # Build set of lines inside multi-line strings to avoid false positives
   319→        multiline_string_lines = _build_string_line_set(lines)
   320→
   321→        for check in SMELL_CHECKS:
   322→            if check["pattern"] is None:
   323→                continue
   324→            for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   325→                # Skip lines inside multi-line strings
   326→                if i in multiline_string_lines:
   327→                    continue
   328→                m = re.search(check["pattern"], line)
   329→                if m and not _match_is_in_string(line, m.start()):
   330→                    # Skip URLs assigned to module-level constants (UPPER_CASE = "https://...")
   331→                    if check["id"] == "hardcoded_url" and re.match(
   332→                        r"^[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*\s*=", line.strip()
   333→                    ):
   334→                        continue
   335→                    smell_counts[check["id"]].append(
   336→                        {
   337→                            "file": filepath,
   338→                            "line": i + 1,
   339→                            "content": line.strip()[:100],
   340→                        }
   341→                    )
   342→
   343→        _detect_empty_except(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   344→        _detect_swallowed_errors(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   345→        detect_ast_smells(filepath, content, smell_counts)
   346→        detect_star_import_no_all(filepath, content, path, smell_counts)
   347→        detect_vestigial_parameter(filepath, content, lines, smell_counts)
   348→        collect_module_constants(filepath, content, constants_by_key)
   349→
   350→    # Cross-file: detect duplicate constants
   351→    detect_duplicate_constants(constants_by_key, smell_counts)
   352→
   353→    severity_order = {"high": 0, "medium": 1, "low": 2}
   354→    entries = []
   355→    for check in SMELL_CHECKS:
   356→        matches = smell_counts[check["id"]]
   357→        if matches:
   358→            entries.append(
   359→                {
   360→                    "id": check["id"],
   361→                    "label": check["label"],
   362→                    "severity": check["severity"],
   363→                    "count": len(matches),
   364→                    "files": len(set(m["file"] for m in matches)),
   365→                    "matches": matches[:50],
   366→                }
   367→            )
   368→    entries.sort(key=lambda e: (severity_order.get(e["severity"], 9), -e["count"]))
   369→    return entries, len(files)
   370→
   371→
   372→def _walk_except_blocks(lines: list[str]):
   373→    """Yield (line_index, except_line_stripped, body_lines) for each except block."""
   374→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   375→        stripped = line.strip()
   376→        if not re.match(r"except\s*(?:\w|\(|:)", stripped) and stripped != "except:":
   377→            continue
   378→        if not stripped.endswith(":"):
   379→            continue
   380→        indent = len(line) - len(line.lstrip())
   381→        j, body_lines = i + 1, []
   382→        while j < len(lines):
   383→            next_line = lines[j]
   384→            next_stripped = next_line.strip()
   385→            if next_stripped == "":
   386→                j += 1
   387→                continue
   388→            if len(next_line) - len(next_line.lstrip()) <= indent:
   389→                break
   390→            body_lines.append(next_stripped)
   391→            j += 1
   392→        yield i, stripped, body_lines
   393→
   394→
   395→def _is_broad_except(stripped: str) -> bool:
   396→    """Check if except clause catches broadly (bare, Exception, BaseException)."""
   397→    if stripped == "except:":
   398→        return True
   399→    m = re.match(r"except\s+(\w+)", stripped)
   400→    return bool(m and m.group(1) in ("Exception", "BaseException"))
   401→
   402→
   403→def _detect_empty_except(
   404→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list]
   405→):
   406→    """Find broad except blocks that just pass or have empty body."""
   407→    for i, stripped, body_lines in _walk_except_blocks(lines):
   408→        if (not body_lines or body_lines == ["pass"]) and _is_broad_except(stripped):
   409→            smell_counts["empty_except"].append(
   410→                {
   411→                    "file": filepath,
   412→                    "line": i + 1,
   413→                    "content": stripped[:100],
   414→                }
   415→            )
   416→
   417→
   418→def _detect_swallowed_errors(
   419→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list]
   420→):
   421→    """Find except blocks that only print/log the error."""
   422→    _LOG_RE = r"(?:print|logging\.\w+|logger\.\w+|log\.\w+)\s*\("
   423→    for i, stripped, body_lines in _walk_except_blocks(lines):
   424→        if body_lines and all(re.match(_LOG_RE, s) for s in body_lines):
   425→            smell_counts["swallowed_error"].append(
   426→                {
   427→                    "file": filepath,
   428→                    "line": i + 1,
   429→                    "content": stripped[:100],
   430→                }
   431→            )
   432→
```

```
   316→        "subjective review",
   317→        "Test health",
   318→        "manual_fix",
   319→        "run `desloppify fix review` to evaluate files against quality dimensions",
   320→    ),
   321→}
   322→
   323→_DISPLAY_ORDER = list(DISPLAY_ORDER)
   324→
   325→JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
   326→    name for name, meta in DETECTORS.items() if meta.needs_judgment
   327→)
   328→
   329→_on_register_callbacks: list[Callable[[], None]] = []
   330→
   331→
   332→def on_detector_registered(callback: Callable[[], None]) -> None:
   333→    """Register a callback invoked after register_detector(). No-arg."""
   334→    _on_register_callbacks.append(callback)
   335→
   336→
   337→def register_detector(meta: DetectorMeta) -> None:
   338→    """Register a detector at runtime (used by generic plugins)."""
   339→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   340→    DETECTORS[meta.name] = meta
   341→    if meta.name not in _DISPLAY_ORDER:
   342→        _DISPLAY_ORDER.append(meta.name)
   343→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = frozenset(
   344→        name for name, m in DETECTORS.items() if m.needs_judgment
   345→    )
   346→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   347→        cb()
   348→
   349→
   350→def detector_names() -> list[str]:
   351→    """All registered detector names, sorted."""
   352→    return sorted(DETECTORS.keys())
   353→
   354→
   355→def display_order() -> list[str]:
   356→    """Canonical display order for terminal output."""
   357→    return list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   358→
   359→
   360→_ACTION_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
   361→_ACTION_LABELS = {
   362→    "auto_fix": "fix",
   363→    "reorganize": "move",
   364→    "refactor": "refactor",
   365→    "manual_fix": "manual",
   366→}
   367→
   368→
   369→def dimension_action_type(dim_name: str) -> str:
   370→    """Return a compact action type label for a dimension based on its detectors."""
   371→    best = "manual"
   372→    best_pri = 99
   373→    for d in DETECTORS.values():
   374→        if d.dimension == dim_name:
   375→            pri = _ACTION_PRIORITY.get(d.action_type, 99)
   376→            if pri < best_pri:
   377→                best_pri = pri
   378→                best = d.action_type
   379→    return _ACTION_LABELS.get(best, "manual")
   380→
   381→
   382→def detector_tools() -> dict[str, dict]:
   383→    """Build detector tool metadata keyed by detector name."""
   384→    result = {}
   385→    for name, d in DETECTORS.items():
   386→        entry: dict = {
   387→            "fixers": list(d.fixers),
   388→            "action_type": d.action_type,
   389→        }
   390→        if d.tool:
   391→            entry["tool"] = d.tool
   392→        if d.guidance:
   393→            entry["guidance"] = d.guidance
   394→        result[name] = entry
   395→    return result
   396→
```

```
   189→def utc_now() -> str:
   190→    """Return current UTC timestamp with second-level precision."""
   191→    return datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   192→
   193→
   194→def empty_state() -> StateModel:
   195→    """Return a new empty state payload."""
   196→    return {
   197→        "version": CURRENT_VERSION,
   198→        "created": utc_now(),
   199→        "last_scan": None,
   200→        "scan_count": 0,
   201→        "overall_score": 0,
   202→        "objective_score": 0,
   203→        "strict_score": 0,
   204→        "verified_strict_score": 0,
   205→        "stats": {},
   206→        "findings": {},
   207→        "scan_coverage": {},
   208→        "score_confidence": {},
   209→        "subjective_integrity": {},
   210→        "subjective_assessments": {},
   211→    }
   212→
   213→
   214→def _as_non_negative_int(value: Any, default: int = 0) -> int:
   215→    try:
   216→        parsed = int(value)
   217→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   218→        return default
   219→    return parsed if parsed >= 0 else 0
   220→
   221→
   222→def ensure_state_defaults(state: StateModel | dict) -> StateModel:
   223→    """Normalize loose/legacy state payloads to a valid base shape."""
   224→    for key, value in empty_state().items():
   225→        state.setdefault(key, value)
   226→
   227→    if not isinstance(state.get("findings"), dict):
   228→        state["findings"] = {}
   229→    if not isinstance(state.get("stats"), dict):
   230→        state["stats"] = {}
   231→    if not isinstance(state.get("scan_history"), list):
   232→        state["scan_history"] = []
   233→    if not isinstance(state.get("scan_coverage"), dict):
   234→        state["scan_coverage"] = {}
   235→    if not isinstance(state.get("score_confidence"), dict):
   236→        state["score_confidence"] = {}
   237→    if not isinstance(state.get("subjective_integrity"), dict):
   238→        state["subjective_integrity"] = {}
   239→
   240→    findings = state["findings"]
   241→    to_remove: list[str] = []
   242→    for finding_id, finding in findings.items():
   243→        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
   244→            to_remove.append(finding_id)
   245→            continue
   246→
   247→        finding.setdefault("id", finding_id)
   248→        finding.setdefault("detector", "unknown")
   249→        finding.setdefault("file", "")
   250→        finding.setdefault("tier", 3)
   251→        finding.setdefault("confidence", "low")
   252→        finding.setdefault("summary", "")
   253→        finding.setdefault("detail", {})
   254→        finding.setdefault("status", "open")
   255→        # Migrate legacy "resolved" status (renamed to "fixed" during Status enum migration)
   256→        if finding["status"] == "resolved":
   257→            finding["status"] = "fixed"
   258→        if finding["status"] not in _ALLOWED_FINDING_STATUSES:
   259→            finding["status"] = "open"
   260→        finding.setdefault("note", None)
   261→        finding.setdefault("first_seen", state.get("created") or utc_now())
   262→        finding.setdefault("last_seen", finding["first_seen"])
   263→        finding.setdefault("resolved_at", None)
   264→        finding["reopen_count"] = _as_non_negative_int(
   265→            finding.get("reopen_count", 0), default=0
   266→        )
   267→        finding.setdefault("suppressed", False)
   268→        finding.setdefault("suppressed_at", None)
   269→        finding.setdefault("suppression_pattern", None)
   270→
   271→    for finding_id in to_remove:
   272→        findings.pop(finding_id, None)
   273→
   274→    for entry in state["scan_history"]:
   275→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   276→            continue
   277→        integrity = entry.get("subjective_integrity")
   278→        if integrity is not None and not isinstance(integrity, dict):
   279→            entry["subjective_integrity"] = None
   280→
   281→    state["scan_count"] = _as_non_negative_int(state.get("scan_count", 0), default=0)
   282→    return cast(StateModel, state)
   283→
   284→
   285→def validate_state_invariants(state: StateModel) -> None:
   286→    """Raise ValueError when core state invariants are violated."""
   287→    if not isinstance(state.get("findings"), dict):
   288→        raise ValueError("state.findings must be a dict")
   289→    if not isinstance(state.get("stats"), dict):
   290→        raise ValueError("state.stats must be a dict")
   291→
   292→    findings = state["findings"]
   293→    for finding_id, finding in findings.items():
   294→        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
   295→            raise ValueError(f"finding {finding_id!r} must be a dict")
   296→        if finding.get("id") != finding_id:
   297→            raise ValueError(f"finding id mismatch for {finding_id!r}")
   298→        if finding.get("status") not in _ALLOWED_FINDING_STATUSES:
   299→            raise ValueError(
   300→                f"finding {finding_id!r} has invalid status {finding.get('status')!r}"
   301→            )
   302→
   303→        tier = finding.get("tier")
   304→        if not isinstance(tier, int) or tier < 1 or tier > 4:
   305→            raise ValueError(f"finding {finding_id!r} has invalid tier {tier!r}")
   306→
   307→        reopen_count = finding.get("reopen_count")
   308→        if not isinstance(reopen_count, int) or reopen_count < 0:
   309→            raise ValueError(
   310→                f"finding {finding_id!r} has invalid reopen_count {reopen_count!r}"
   311→            )
   312→
   313→
   314→def json_default(obj: Any) -> Any:
   315→    """JSON serializer that handles known types and rejects unknowns."""
   316→    if isinstance(obj, set):
   317→        return sorted(obj)
   318→    if isinstance(obj, Path):
   319→        return str(obj).replace("\\", "/")
   320→    if hasattr(obj, "isoformat"):
   321→        return obj.isoformat()
   322→    raise TypeError(
   323→        f"Object of type {type(obj).__name__} is not JSON serializable: {obj!r}"
   324→    )
   325→
   326→
   327→def get_overall_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
   328→    """Canonical overall score (lenient, includes subjective dimensions)."""
   329→    return state.get("overall_score")
   330→
   331→
   332→def get_objective_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
   333→    """Canonical objective score (lenient, mechanical dimensions only)."""
   334→    return state.get("objective_score")
   335→
   336→
   337→def get_strict_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
   338→    """Canonical strict score (strict, includes subjective dimensions)."""
   339→    return state.get("strict_score")
   340→
   341→
   342→def get_verified_strict_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
   343→    """Strict score that only credits scan-verified fixes.
   344→
   345→    Returns None if no scan-verified score exists yet (fresh state or
   346→    no scan has run). Does not fall back to the unverified strict_score.
   347→    """
   348→    return state.get("verified_strict_score")
   349→
```

```
   100→def _py_extract_functions(path: Path) -> list[FunctionInfo]:
   101→    """Extract all Python functions for duplicate detection."""
   102→    functions = []
   103→    for filepath in find_py_files(path):
   104→        functions.extend(extract_py_functions(filepath))
   105→    return functions
   106→
   107→
   108→def _scan_root_from_files(files: list[str]) -> Path | None:
   109→    """Derive the common ancestor directory from a list of file paths."""
   110→    import os
   111→
   112→    py_files = [f for f in files if f.endswith(".py")]
   113→    if not py_files:
   114→        return None
   115→    try:
   116→        common = Path(os.path.commonpath(py_files))
   117→        return common if common.is_dir() else common.parent
   118→    except ValueError:
   119→        return None
   120→
   121→
   122→@register_lang("python")
   123→class PythonConfig(LangConfig):
   124→    def _missing_bandit_coverage(self) -> DetectorCoverageStatus:
   125→        return DetectorCoverageStatus(
   126→            detector="security",
   127→            status="reduced",
   128→            confidence=0.6,
   129→            summary="bandit is not installed — Python-specific security checks will be skipped.",
   130→            impact=(
   131→                "Python-specific security coverage is reduced; clean security output may "
   132→                "miss shell injection, unsafe deserialization, and risky SQL/subprocess patterns."
   133→            ),
   134→            remediation="Install Bandit: pip install bandit",
   135→            tool="bandit",
   136→            reason="missing_dependency",
   137→        )
   138→
   139→    def scan_coverage_prerequisites(self) -> list[DetectorCoverageStatus]:
   140→        if shutil.which("bandit") is not None:
   141→            return []
   142→        return [self._missing_bandit_coverage()]
   143→
   144→    def detect_lang_security_detailed(self, files, zone_map) -> LangSecurityResult:
   145→        scan_root = _scan_root_from_files(files)
   146→        if scan_root is None:
   147→            return LangSecurityResult(entries=[], files_scanned=0)
   148→
   149→        result = detect_with_bandit(scan_root, zone_map)
   150→        coverage = result.status.coverage()
   151→        return LangSecurityResult(
   152→            entries=result.entries,
   153→            files_scanned=result.files_scanned,
   154→            coverage=coverage,
   155→        )
   156→
   157→    def detect_lang_security(self, files, zone_map):
   158→        detailed = self.detect_lang_security_detailed(files, zone_map)
   159→        return detailed.entries, detailed.files_scanned
   160→
   161→    def detect_private_imports(self, graph, zone_map):
   162→        return detect_python_private_imports(graph, zone_map)
   163→
   164→    def __init__(self):
   165→        super().__init__(
   166→            name="python",
   167→            extensions=[".py"],
   168→            exclusions=["__pycache__", ".venv", "node_modules", ".eggs", "*.egg-info"],
   169→            default_src=".",
   170→            build_dep_graph=build_dep_graph,
   171→            entry_patterns=PY_ENTRY_PATTERNS,
```

```
1→"""Quality-focused Python detector phase runners."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.engine.detectors import signature as signature_detector_mod
     9→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import adjust_potential, filter_entries
    10→from desloppify.languages._framework.finding_factories import make_smell_findings
    11→from desloppify.languages._framework.runtime import LangRun
    12→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors import dict_keys as dict_keys_detector_mod
    13→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors import (
    14→    import_linter_adapter as import_linter_adapter_mod,
    15→)
    16→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors import (
    17→    mutable_state as mutable_state_detector_mod,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors import smells as smells_detector_mod
    20→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors.ruff_smells import detect_with_ruff_smells
    21→from desloppify.state import Finding
    22→from desloppify.utils import log
    23→
    24→
    25→def phase_smells(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
    26→    """Run file/code smell detectors plus cross-file signature variance."""
    27→    entries, total_files = smells_detector_mod.detect_smells(path)
    28→    # Supplement with ruff B/E/W rules not covered by the regex smells above.
    29→    ruff_entries = detect_with_ruff_smells(path)
    30→    if ruff_entries:
    31→        entries = entries + ruff_entries
    32→    results = make_smell_findings(entries, log)
    33→
    34→    functions = lang.extract_functions(path) if lang.extract_functions else []
    35→    sig_entries, _ = signature_detector_mod.detect_signature_variance(functions)
    36→    for entry in sig_entries:
    37→        results.append(
    38→            state_mod.make_finding(
    39→                "smells",
    40→                entry["files"][0],
    41→                f"sig_variance::{entry['name']}",
    42→                tier=3,
    43→                confidence="medium",
    44→                summary=(
    45→                    f"Signature variance: {entry['name']}() has {entry['signature_count']} "
    46→                    f"different signatures across {entry['file_count']} files"
    47→                ),
    48→                detail={
    49→                    "function": entry["name"],
    50→                    "file_count": entry["file_count"],
    51→                    "signature_count": entry["signature_count"],
    52→                    "variants": entry["variants"][:5],
    53→                },
    54→            )
    55→        )
    56→    if sig_entries:
    57→        log(
    58→            f"         signature variance: {len(sig_entries)} functions with inconsistent signatures"
    59→        )
    60→
    61→    return results, {
    62→        "smells": adjust_potential(lang.zone_map, total_files),
    63→    }
    64→
    65→
    66→def phase_mutable_state(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
    67→    """Find global mutable config patterns."""
    68→    entries, total_files = mutable_state_detector_mod.detect_global_mutable_config(path)
    69→    results = []
    70→    for entry in entries:
    71→        results.append(
    72→            state_mod.make_finding(
    73→                "global_mutable_config",
    74→                entry["file"],
    75→                entry["name"],
    76→                tier=3,

    78→                summary=entry["summary"],
    79→                detail={
    80→                    "mutation_count": entry["mutation_count"],
    81→                    "mutation_lines": entry["mutation_lines"],
    82→                },
    83→            )
    84→        )
    85→    if results:
    86→        log(f"         global mutable config: {len(results)} findings")

    88→        "global_mutable_config": adjust_potential(lang.zone_map, total_files),
    89→    }

    92→def phase_layer_violation(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
    93→    """Find package/layer boundary violations via import-linter."""
```

```
1→"""Finding factory functions — normalize raw detector output into Finding dicts."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections import defaultdict
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core.enums import Tier
     9→from desloppify.file_discovery import rel
    10→from desloppify.state import Finding, make_finding
    11→
    12→
    13→def make_unused_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
    14→    """Transform raw unused-detector entries into normalized findings.
    15→
    16→    Shared by both Python and TypeScript unused phases.
    17→    """
    18→    results = []
    19→    for e in entries:
    20→        tier = 1 if e["category"] == "imports" else 2
    21→        results.append(
    22→            make_finding(
    23→                "unused",
    24→                e["file"],
    25→                e["name"],
    26→                tier=tier,
    27→                confidence="high",
    28→                summary=f"Unused {e['category']}: {e['name']}",
    29→                detail={"line": e["line"], "category": e["category"]},
    30→            )
    31→        )
    32→    stderr_fn(f"         {len(entries)} instances -> {len(results)} findings")
    33→    return results
    34→
    35→
    36→def make_dupe_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
    37→    """Transform clustered duplicate entries into normalized findings.
    38→
    39→    Each entry represents a cluster of similar functions. One finding per cluster.
    40→    """
    41→    results = []
    42→    for e in entries:
    43→        a, b = e["fn_a"], e["fn_b"]
    44→        if a["loc"] < 10 and b["loc"] < 10:
    45→            continue
    46→        cluster_size = e.get("cluster_size", 2)
    47→        pair = sorted([(a["file"], a["name"]), (b["file"], b["name"])])
    48→        name = f"{pair[0][1]}::{rel(pair[1][0])}::{pair[1][1]}"
    49→        tier = 2 if e["kind"] == "exact" else 3
    50→        conf = "high" if e["kind"] == "exact" else "low"
    51→        kind_label = "Exact" if e["kind"] == "exact" else "Near"
    52→        if cluster_size > 2:
    53→            summary = (
    54→                f"{kind_label} dupe cluster ({cluster_size} functions, "
    55→                f"{e['similarity']:.0%} similar): "
    56→                f"{a['name']} ({rel(a['file'])}:{a['line']}), "
    57→                f"{b['name']} ({rel(b['file'])}:{b['line']}), ..."
    58→            )
    59→        else:
    60→            summary = (
    61→                f"{kind_label} dupe: "
    62→                f"{a['name']} ({rel(a['file'])}:{a['line']}) <-> "
    63→                f"{b['name']} ({rel(b['file'])}:{b['line']}) [{e['similarity']:.0%}]"
    64→            )
    65→        results.append(
    66→            make_finding(
    67→                "dupes",
    68→                pair[0][0],
    69→                name,
    70→                tier=tier,
    71→                confidence=conf,
    72→                summary=summary,
    73→                detail={
    74→                    "fn_a": a,
    75→                    "fn_b": b,
    76→                    "similarity": e["similarity"],
    77→                    "kind": e["kind"],
    78→                    "cluster_size": cluster_size,
    79→                    "cluster": e.get("cluster", [a, b]),
    80→                },
    81→            )
    82→        )
    83→    suppressed = sum(
    84→        1 for e in entries if e["fn_a"]["loc"] < 10 and e["fn_b"]["loc"] < 10
    85→    )
    86→    stderr_fn(f"         {len(entries)} clusters, {suppressed} suppressed (<10 LOC)")
    87→    return results
    88→
    89→
    90→def make_single_use_findings(
    91→    entries: list[dict],
    92→    get_area,
    93→    *,
    94→    loc_range: tuple[int, int] = (50, 200),
    95→    suppress_colocated: bool = True,
    96→    skip_dir_names: set[str] | None = None,
    97→    stderr_fn,
    98→) -> list[Finding]:
    99→    """Filter and normalize single-use entries into findings.
   100→
   101→    Suppresses entries within the LOC range (they're appropriately-sized abstractions),
   102→    entries co-located with their sole importer, and entries in skip_dir_names
   103→    directories (e.g., commands/ -- CLI modules are single-use by design).
   104→    """
   105→    results = []
   106→    colocated_suppressed = 0
   107→    lo, hi = loc_range
   108→    for e in entries:
   109→        if lo <= e["loc"] <= hi:
   110→            continue
   111→        # Skip files in directories that are single-use by design (e.g., commands/)
   112→        if skip_dir_names:
   113→            parts = Path(e["file"]).parts
   114→            if any(p in skip_dir_names for p in parts):
   115→                continue
   116→        if suppress_colocated and get_area:
   117→            src_area = get_area(rel(e["file"]))
   118→            imp_area = get_area(e["sole_importer"])
   119→            if src_area == imp_area:
   120→                colocated_suppressed += 1
   121→                continue
   122→        results.append(
   123→            make_finding(
   124→                "single_use",
   125→                e["file"],
   126→                "",
   127→                tier=3,
   128→                confidence="medium",
   129→                summary=f"Single-use ({e['loc']} LOC): only imported by {e['sole_importer']}",
   130→                detail={"loc": e["loc"], "sole_importer": e["sole_importer"]},
   131→            )
   132→        )
   133→    suppressed = len(entries) - len(results)
   134→    coloc_note = f", {colocated_suppressed} co-located" if colocated_suppressed else ""
   135→    stderr_fn(
   136→        f"         single-use: {len(entries)} found, {suppressed} suppressed "
   137→        f"({lo}-{hi} LOC{coloc_note})"
   138→    )
   139→    return results
   140→
   141→
   142→def make_cycle_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   143→    """Normalize import cycles into findings."""
   144→    results = []
   145→    for cy in entries:
   146→        cycle_files = [rel(f) for f in cy["files"]]
   147→        name = "::".join(cycle_files[:4])
   148→        if len(cycle_files) > 4:
   149→            name += f"::+{len(cycle_files) - 4}"
   150→        tier = 3 if cy["length"] <= 3 else 4
   151→        results.append(
   152→            make_finding(
   153→                "cycles",
   154→                cy["files"][0],
   155→                name,
   156→                tier=tier,
   157→                confidence="high",
   158→                summary=f"Import cycle ({cy['length']} files): "
   159→                + " -> ".join(cycle_files[:5])
   160→                + (f" -> +{len(cycle_files) - 5}" if len(cycle_files) > 5 else ""),
   161→                detail={"files": cycle_files, "length": cy["length"]},
   162→            )
   163→        )
   164→    if entries:
   165→        stderr_fn(f"         cycles: {len(entries)} import cycles")
   166→    return results
   167→
   168→
   169→def make_orphaned_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   170→    """Normalize orphaned file entries into findings."""
   171→    results = []
   172→    for e in entries:
   173→        results.append(
   174→            make_finding(
   175→                "orphaned",
   176→                e["file"],
   177→                "",
   178→                tier=3,
   179→                confidence="medium",
   180→                summary=f"Orphaned file ({e['loc']} LOC): zero importers, not an entry point",
   181→                detail={"loc": e["loc"]},
   182→            )
   183→        )
   184→    if entries:
   185→        stderr_fn(f"         orphaned: {len(entries)} files with zero importers")
   186→    return results
   187→
   188→
   189→SMELL_TIER_MAP = {"high": Tier.QUICK_FIX, "medium": Tier.JUDGMENT, "low": Tier.JUDGMENT}
   190→
   191→
   192→def make_smell_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]:
   193→    """Group smell entries by file and assign tiers from severity.
   194→
   195→    Input: list of smell dicts from detect_smells, each with id/label/severity/matches.
   196→    Output: findings grouped per (file, smell_id).
   197→    """
   198→    results = []
   199→    for e in entries:
   200→        by_file: dict[str, list] = defaultdict(list)
```

```
def make_finding(
    detector: str,
    file: str,
    name: str,
    *,
    tier: int,
    confidence: str,
    summary: str,
    detail: dict | None = None,
) -> Finding:
    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
    rfile = rel(file)
    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
    now = utc_now()
    return {
        "id": finding_id,
        "detector": detector,
        "file": rfile,
        "tier": tier,
        "confidence": confidence,
        "summary": summary,
        "detail": detail or {},
        "status": "open",
        "note": None,
        "first_seen": now,
        "last_seen": now,
        "resolved_at": None,
        "reopen_count": 0,
    }


def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
    """Check if a finding matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, or path."""
    return (
        finding_id == pattern
        or ("*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern))
        or ("::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern))
        or (
            finding.get("detector") == pattern
            or finding["file"] == pattern
            or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
```

```
   178→
   179→def _detect_ast_smells(filepath: str, content: str, smell_counts: dict[str, list]):
   180→    """Detect AST-based code smells using registry-driven collector dispatch."""
   181→    try:
   182→        tree = ast.parse(content)
   183→    except SyntaxError:
   184→        return
   185→
   186→    # Build a single-walk context index for node-level detectors.
   187→    all_nodes = tuple(ast.walk(tree))
   188→    fn_nodes = tuple(
   189→        node
   190→        for node in all_nodes
   191→        if isinstance(node, ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef)
   192→    )
   193→
   194→    for spec in NODE_DETECTORS:
   195→        matches: list[SmellMatch] = []
   196→        for fn_node in fn_nodes:
   197→            matches.extend(spec.collect(filepath, fn_node, tree))
   198→        merge_smell_matches(smell_counts, spec.smell_id, matches)
   199→
   200→    for spec in TREE_DETECTORS:
   201→        matches = spec.collect(filepath, tree, all_nodes)
   202→        merge_smell_matches(smell_counts, spec.smell_id, matches)
   203→
```

```
1→"""AST detectors that operate on function/class nodes."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→
     7→from desloppify.languages.python.detectors.smells_ast._shared import (
     8→    _is_docstring,
     9→    _is_return_none,
    10→)
    11→
    12→
    13→def _is_test_file(filepath: str) -> bool:
    14→    """Return True when a path clearly points to a test module."""
    15→    normalized = filepath.replace("\\", "/")
    16→    return normalized.startswith("tests/") or "/tests/" in normalized
    17→
    18→
    19→def _detect_monster_functions(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    20→    """Flag functions longer than 150 LOC."""
    21→    if not (hasattr(node, "end_lineno") and node.end_lineno):
    22→        return []
    23→    loc = node.end_lineno - node.lineno + 1
    24→    if loc > 150:
    25→        return [
    26→            {
    27→                "file": filepath,
    28→                "line": node.lineno,
    29→                "content": f"{node.name}() — {loc} LOC",
    30→            }
    31→        ]
    32→    return []
    33→
    34→
    35→def _detect_dead_functions(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    36→    """Flag functions whose body is only pass, return, or return None."""
    37→    if node.decorator_list:
    38→        return []
    39→    body = node.body
    40→    if len(body) == 1:
    41→        stmt = body[0]
    42→        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Pass) or _is_return_none(stmt):
    43→            return [
    44→                {
    45→                    "file": filepath,
    46→                    "line": node.lineno,
    47→                    "content": f"{node.name}() — body is only {ast.dump(stmt)[:40]}",
    48→                }
    49→            ]
    50→    elif len(body) == 2:
    51→        first, second = body
    52→        if not _is_docstring(first):
    53→            return []
    54→        if isinstance(second, ast.Pass):
    55→            desc = "docstring + pass"
    56→        elif _is_return_none(second):
    57→            desc = "docstring + return None"
    58→        else:
    59→            return []
    60→        return [
    61→            {
    62→                "file": filepath,
    63→                "line": node.lineno,
    64→                "content": f"{node.name}() — {desc}",
    65→            }
    66→        ]
    67→    return []
    68→
    69→
    70→def _detect_deferred_imports(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    71→    """Flag function-level imports (possible circular import workarounds)."""
    72→    if _is_test_file(filepath):
    73→        return []
    74→    _SKIP_MODULES = ("typing", "typing_extensions", "__future__")
    75→    for child in ast.walk(node):
    76→        if (
    77→            not isinstance(child, ast.Import | ast.ImportFrom)
    78→            or child.lineno <= node.lineno
    79→        ):
    80→            continue
    81→        module = getattr(child, "module", None) or ""
    82→        if module in _SKIP_MODULES:
    83→            continue
    84→        names = ", ".join(a.name for a in child.names[:3])
    85→        if len(child.names) > 3:
    86→            names += f", +{len(child.names) - 3}"
    87→        return [
    88→            {
    89→                "file": filepath,
    90→                "line": child.lineno,
    91→                "content": f"import {module or names} inside {node.name}()",
    92→            }
    93→        ]
    94→    return []
    95→
    96→
    97→def _detect_inline_classes(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    98→    """Flag classes defined inside functions."""
    99→    results: list[dict] = []
   100→    for child in node.body:
   101→        if isinstance(child, ast.ClassDef):
   102→            results.append(
   103→                {
   104→                    "file": filepath,
   105→                    "line": child.lineno,
   106→                    "content": f"class {child.name} defined inside {node.name}()",
   107→                }
   108→            )
   109→    return results
   110→
   111→
   112→def _detect_lru_cache_mutable(
   113→    filepath: str,
   114→    node: ast.AST,
   115→    tree: ast.Module,
   116→) -> list[dict]:
   117→    """Flag @lru_cache/@cache functions that reference module-level mutable variables.
   118→
   119→    Finds globals referenced in the function body that aren't in the parameter list,
   120→    checking if those names are assigned to mutable values at module level.
   121→    """
   122→    # Check if this function has @lru_cache or @cache decorator
   123→    has_cache = False
   124→    for dec in node.decorator_list:
   125→        if isinstance(dec, ast.Name) and dec.id in ("lru_cache", "cache"):
   126→            has_cache = True
   127→        elif isinstance(dec, ast.Call) and isinstance(dec.func, ast.Name):
   128→            if dec.func.id in ("lru_cache", "cache"):
   129→                has_cache = True
   130→        elif isinstance(dec, ast.Attribute) and dec.attr in ("lru_cache", "cache"):
   131→            has_cache = True
   132→    if not has_cache:
   133→        return []
   134→
   135→    # Get parameter names
   136→    param_names = set()
   137→    for arg in node.args.args + node.args.posonlyargs + node.args.kwonlyargs:
   138→        param_names.add(arg.arg)
   139→    if node.args.vararg:
   140→        param_names.add(node.args.vararg.arg)
   141→    if node.args.kwarg:
   142→        param_names.add(node.args.kwarg.arg)
   143→
   144→    # Collect module-level mutable assignments
   145→    module_mutables = set()
   146→    for stmt in tree.body:
   147→        if isinstance(stmt, ast.Assign):
   148→            for target in stmt.targets:
   149→                if isinstance(target, ast.Name) and isinstance(
   150→                    stmt.value, ast.List | ast.Dict | ast.Set | ast.Call
   151→                ):
   152→                    module_mutables.add(target.id)
   153→        elif (
   154→            isinstance(stmt, ast.AnnAssign)
   155→            and stmt.target
   156→            and isinstance(stmt.target, ast.Name)
   157→        ):
   158→            if stmt.value and isinstance(
   159→                stmt.value, ast.List | ast.Dict | ast.Set | ast.Call
   160→            ):
   161→                module_mutables.add(stmt.target.id)
   162→
   163→    # Find Name references in function body that point to module-level mutables
   164→    for child in ast.walk(node):
   165→        if (
   166→            isinstance(child, ast.Name)
   167→            and child.id in module_mutables
   168→            and child.id not in param_names
   169→        ):
   170→            return [
   171→                {
   172→                    "file": filepath,
   173→                    "line": node.lineno,
   174→                    "content": f"@lru_cache on {node.name}() reads mutable global '{child.id}'",
   175→                }
   176→            ]
   177→    return []
   178→
```

```
1→"""Typed models and helpers for Python smell detector internals."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→from collections.abc import Callable
     7→from typing import TypeAlias, TypedDict
     8→
     9→
    10→class SmellMatch(TypedDict):
    11→    """Normalized smell match shape."""
    12→
    13→    file: str
    14→    line: int
    15→    content: str
    16→
    17→
    18→SmellCounts: TypeAlias = dict[str, list[SmellMatch]]
    19→
    20→# Return-based detector contracts used by dispatch registries.
    21→NodeCollector: TypeAlias = Callable[[str, ast.AST, ast.Module], list[SmellMatch]]
    22→TreeCollector: TypeAlias = Callable[
    23→    [str, ast.Module, tuple[ast.AST, ...] | None], list[SmellMatch]
    24→]
    25→
    26→
    27→def _match_key(match: SmellMatch) -> tuple[str, int, str]:
    28→    return (match["file"], int(match["line"]), match["content"])
    29→
    30→
    31→def dedupe_smell_matches(matches: list[SmellMatch]) -> list[SmellMatch]:
    32→    """Return stable deterministic list of unique smell matches."""
    33→    seen: set[tuple[str, int, str]] = set()
    34→    unique: list[SmellMatch] = []
    35→    for match in sorted(matches, key=_match_key):
    36→        key = _match_key(match)
    37→        if key in seen:
    38→            continue
    39→        seen.add(key)
    40→        unique.append(match)
    41→    return unique
    42→
    43→
    44→def merge_smell_matches(
    45→    smell_counts: SmellCounts, smell_id: str, matches: list[SmellMatch]
    46→) -> None:
    47→    """Merge deduped matches into smell_counts[smell_id] without duplicate entries."""
    48→    if not matches:
    49→        return
    50→    target = smell_counts.setdefault(smell_id, [])
    51→    existing = {_match_key(match) for match in target}
    52→    for match in dedupe_smell_matches(matches):
    53→        key = _match_key(match)
    54→        if key in existing:
    55→            continue
    56→        target.append(match)
    57→        existing.add(key)
    58→
```

Excellent! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive summary document:

Based on my thorough investigation of the desloppify codebase, I have identified the complete infrastructure for adding new detectors and smell signals. Here's your comprehensive report:

---

## Desloppify Detector Infrastructure Report

### 1. Python Complexity Signals

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/complexity.py`

**Pattern:** Complexity signals are pure compute functions that detect structural issues and return `tuple[int, str] | None` (a metric value and descriptive label, or None if below threshold).

**Key Functions:**

#### `compute_max_params(content: str, lines: list[str]) -> tuple[int, str] | None`
- Parses function definitions with regex: `r"def\s+\w+\s*\("`
- Tracks parenthesis nesting depth to extract parameter lists
- Filters out `self`, `cls`, and `*args`/`**kwargs`
- Returns `(count, label)` if > 7 params, else None
- Example return: `(10, "function with 10 params")`

#### `compute_nesting_depth(content: str, lines: list[str]) -> tuple[int, str] | None`
- Detects indentation unit using GCD of all indents in file
- Calculates max nesting depth by `indent // indent_unit`
- Returns `(depth, label)` if > 4, else None
- Example return: `(7, "nesting depth 7")`

#### `compute_long_functions(content: str, lines: list[str]) -> tuple[int, str] | None`
- Regex matches function definitions: `r"^(\s*)def\s+(\w+)"`
- Tracks function indent level and scans body until next same-level code
- Returns `(loc, label)` for longest function if any > 80 LOC
- Example return: `(245, "long function (process_data: 245 LOC)")`

**Registration:** In `phases.py`, wrapped as `ComplexitySignal` objects in `PY_COMPLEXITY_SIGNALS` list with weight, threshold, and compute function:

```python
ComplexitySignal("long_functions", None, weight=1, threshold=80, compute=compute_long_functions)
```

---

### 2. Python Smell Detectors - Overall Architecture

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/smells.py`

The smell detection system is **dual-track**: regex-based (fast, surface-level) + AST-based (semantic, AST-walked).

#### `SMELL_CHECKS` Registry
A list of `dict` smell specifications, each defining:
```python
{
    "id": "eval_exec",              # Unique smell identifier
    "label": "eval()/exec() usage", # Human-readable display name
    "pattern": r"(?<!\.)...",       # Regex pattern (or None for multi-line/AST)
    "severity": "high"              # "high", "medium", or "low"
}
```

#### Entry Point: `detect_smells(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]`

**Flow:**
1. Initialize `smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]` (one key per smell ID)
2. **Regex-based detectors:**
   - Build multiline-string-tracking set with `_build_string_line_set()`
   - For each file, per smell check with `pattern`:
     - Match against each line with regex
     - Validate match not in string/comment with `_match_is_in_string()`
     - Append match dict: `{"file": filepath, "line": i+1, "content": line[:100]}`
3. **Multi-line detectors (pattern=None):**
   - `_detect_empty_except()` — walks except blocks, flags broad except: pass
   - `_detect_swallowed_errors()` — flags except blocks with only logging
4. **AST detectors:**
   - Call `detect_ast_smells(filepath, content, smell_counts)` — populates smell_counts in-place
5. **Cross-file detectors:**
   - Collect constants with `collect_module_constants()`
   - Detect duplicates with `detect_duplicate_constants()`
6. **Output:** Sort by severity/count, truncate to 50 matches per smell:
   ```python
   {
       "id": "eval_exec",
       "label": "eval()/exec() usage",
       "severity": "high",
       "count": 3,
       "files": 2,
       "matches": [{"file": "x.py", "line": 42, "content": "..."}]
   }
   ```

### 3. Python Smell Registration in SMELL_CHECKS

**Complete list of 40+ smells** — examples:

| Smell ID | Severity | Pattern Type | Description |
|----------|----------|--------------|-------------|
| `eval_exec` | high | Regex | `eval()/exec()` usage |
| `magic_number` | low | Regex | Large hardcoded numbers |
| `hardcoded_url` | medium | Regex | URLs in source (not const) |
| `empty_except` | high | Multi-line | Bare except: pass |
| `monster_function` | high | AST | >150 LOC functions |
| `dead_function` | medium | AST | Body is pass/return None |
| `subprocess_no_timeout` | high | AST | subprocess call without timeout |
| `lru_cache_mutable` | medium | AST | @lru_cache reading mutable globals |
| `sys_exit_in_library` | high | AST | sys.exit() outside CLI entry |
| `silent_except` | high | AST | Except with pass/continue, no log |

### 4. AST-Based Smell Detectors

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/detectors/smells_ast/`

#### Registry Architecture (`_dispatch.py`)

Two registries: `NODE_DETECTORS` and `TREE_DETECTORS` — each is a tuple of detector specs.

**`NodeCollector` contract:**
```python
Callable[[filepath: str, node: ast.AST, tree: ast.Module], list[SmellMatch]]
```
- `node` is a single FunctionDef/AsyncFunctionDef
- Returns list of `SmellMatch` dicts
- Run once per function node in the AST

**`TreeCollector` contract:**
```python
Callable[[filepath: str, tree: ast.Module, all_nodes: tuple[ast.AST, ...]], list[SmellMatch]]
```
- `tree` is the full module AST
- `all_nodes` is `ast.walk(tree)` tuple (all descendants)
- Run once per file
- More expensive, used for cross-node analysis

#### SmellMatch TypedDict
```python
class SmellMatch(TypedDict):
    file: str
    line: int
    content: str
```

#### `_detect_ast_smells()` Orchestration
```python
def _detect_ast_smells(filepath: str, content: str, smell_counts: dict[str, list]):
    tree = ast.parse(content)
    all_nodes = tuple(ast.walk(tree))
    fn_nodes = tuple(node for node in all_nodes 
                     if isinstance(node, ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef))
    
    # Run node detectors (per function)
    for spec in NODE_DETECTORS:
        matches = []
        for fn_node in fn_nodes:
            matches.extend(spec.collect(filepath, fn_node, tree))
        merge_smell_matches(smell_counts, spec.smell_id, matches)
    
    # Run tree detectors (whole-module)
    for spec in TREE_DETECTORS:
        matches = spec.collect(filepath, tree, all_nodes)
        merge_smell_matches(smell_counts, spec.smell_id, matches)
```

#### Example Node Detector: `_detect_monster_functions()`
```python
def _detect_monster_functions(filepath: str, node: ast.AST) -> list[dict]:
    """Flag functions longer than 150 LOC."""
    if not (hasattr(node, "end_lineno") and node.end_lineno):
        return []
    loc = node.end_lineno - node.lineno + 1
    if loc > 150:
        return [{"file": filepath, "line": node.lineno, "content": f"{node.name}() — {loc} LOC"}]
    return []
```

#### Example Tree Detector: `_detect_lru_cache_mutable()`
- Scans for `@lru_cache`/`@cache` decorators on functions
- Collects module-level mutable assignments (lists, dicts, sets, calls)
- Walks function AST to find Name nodes referencing module mutables not in params
- Returns match if found
