### 5. Finding Type Definition

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py`

```python
class Finding(TypedDict):
    id: str                          # detector::file::name (stable ID)
    detector: str                    # e.g., "smells"
    file: str                        # Relative path
    tier: int                        # 1-4 (1=quick fix, 4=judgment)
    confidence: str                  # "high", "medium", "low"
    summary: str                     # Human text, <1 line
    detail: dict[str, Any]           # Arbitrary context data
    status: FindingStatus            # "open" | "fixed" | "auto_resolved" | "wontfix" | "false_positive"
    note: str | None                 # User annotation
    first_seen: str                  # ISO UTC timestamp
    last_seen: str                   # ISO UTC timestamp
    resolved_at: str | None          # ISO UTC timestamp if fixed
    reopen_count: int                # Count of re-openings
    suppressed: NotRequired[bool]
    suppressed_at: NotRequired[str | None]
    suppression_pattern: NotRequired[str | None]
    resolution_attestation: NotRequired[dict[str, str | bool | None]]
    lang: NotRequired[str]
    zone: NotRequired[str]
```

**Tier Mapping (for smells):**
```python
SMELL_TIER_MAP = {
    "high": Tier.QUICK_FIX,    # 1 — auto-fixable
    "medium": Tier.JUDGMENT,   # 2 — needs review
    "low": Tier.JUDGMENT       # 3 — low priority
}
```

### 6. Converting Smells to Findings

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/_framework/finding_factories.py`

**Function:** `make_smell_findings(entries: list[dict], stderr_fn) -> list[Finding]`

**Process:**
1. For each smell entry (from `detect_smells()`):
   - Group matches by file: `by_file: dict[str, list[match]]`
   - For each file:
     - Determine confidence: "medium" if severity != "low" else "low"
     - Map severity to tier: `SMELL_TIER_MAP[severity]`
     - Create Finding:
       ```python
       make_finding(
           "smells",                      # detector
           file,                          # file path
           e["id"],                       # smell_id as name (e.g., "eval_exec")
           tier=tier,
           confidence=conf,
           summary=f"{len(matches)}x {e['label']}",  # "3x eval()/exec() usage"
           detail={
               "smell_id": e["id"],
               "severity": e["severity"],
               "count": len(matches),
               "lines": [m["line"] for m in matches[:10]]
           }
       )
       ```

**Factory function creates Finding with stable ID:**
```python
def make_finding(detector: str, file: str, name: str, *, tier: int, 
                 confidence: str, summary: str, detail: dict | None = None) -> Finding:
    rfile = rel(file)
    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
    now = utc_now()
    return {
        "id": finding_id,
        "detector": detector,
        "file": rfile,
        "tier": tier,
        "confidence": confidence,
        "summary": summary,
        "detail": detail or {},
        "status": "open",
        "note": None,
        "first_seen": now,
        "last_seen": now,
        "resolved_at": None,
        "reopen_count": 0,
    }
```

### 7. Detector Registry (Canonical Source)

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/registry.py`

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DetectorMeta:
    name: str                  # "smells", "structural", etc.
    display: str               # Terminal display name
    dimension: str             # Scoring dimension ("Code quality", "File health")
    action_type: str           # "auto_fix" | "refactor" | "reorganize" | "manual_fix"
    guidance: str              # Narrative coaching text
    fixers: tuple[str, ...] = ()  # Associated fixer IDs
    tool: str = ""             # "move" or empty
    structural: bool = False   # Merge under "structural" in display
    needs_judgment: bool = False  # Findings need LLM judgment
```

**Example for "smells":**
```python
"smells": DetectorMeta(
    "smells",
    "smells",
    "Code quality",
    "auto_fix",
    "fix code smells — dead useEffect, empty if chains",
    fixers=("dead-useeffect", "empty-if-chain"),
    needs_judgment=True,
)
```

**Display order:** `DISPLAY_ORDER` list defines canonical ordering (logs → unused → exports → ... → smells → ... → subjective_review)

### 8. How Detectors Are Registered in Phases

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/python/__init__.py` and `phases_quality.py`

**Phase registration pattern:**
```python
class PythonConfig(LangConfig):
    def __init__(self):
        super().__init__(
            phases=[
                DetectorPhase("Code smells", phase_smells),
                DetectorPhase("Mutable state", phase_mutable_state),
                # ...
            ]
        )
```

**Phase function signature:**
```python
def phase_smells(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
    """Run file/code smell detectors plus cross-file signature variance."""
    entries, total_files = smells_detector_mod.detect_smells(path)
    ruff_entries = detect_with_ruff_smells(path)
    results = make_smell_findings(entries + ruff_entries, log)
    # ... append signature variance findings ...
    return results, {"smells": adjust_potential(lang.zone_map, total_files)}
```

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/languages/typescript/detectors/smells.py`

**Pattern:** Identical structure to Python, with TS-specific smells in `TS_SMELL_CHECKS`:

```python
TS_SMELL_CHECKS = [
    {
        "id": "empty_catch",
        "label": "Empty catch blocks",
        "pattern": r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}",
        "severity": "high",
    },
    {
        "id": "any_type",
        "label": "Explicit `any` types",
        "pattern": r":\s*any\b|<\s*any\b|...",
        "severity": "medium",
    },
    # ... ~25 more TS-specific smells ...
]
```

**Key difference:** Multi-line helpers use brace-tracking (not indentation), implemented in `_smell_helpers.py`:
- `scan_code()` — generator yielding tokens with brace/bracket depth
- `_detect_async_no_await()` — multi-line AST-like analysis
- `_detect_dead_useeffect()` — React-specific hooks detection

### 10. How to Add a New Detector: Step-by-Step Pattern

#### **Option A: Simple Regex-Based Smell (Fastest)**

1. Add entry to `SMELL_CHECKS` in `smells.py`:
   ```python
   _smell("my_new_smell", "Human label", "medium", r"my_pattern")
   ```
2. No further code needed — regex matching handled by `detect_smells()` loop

#### **Option B: Multi-Line Smell (Requires Custom Function)**

1. Add entry to `SMELL_CHECKS` with `pattern=None`:
   ```python
   _smell("my_multiline_smell", "Human label", "high")
   ```
2. Implement detector function in `smells.py`:
   ```python
   def _detect_my_multiline_smell(filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list]):
       for i, line in enumerate(lines):
           if some_condition(lines, i):
               smell_counts["my_multiline_smell"].append({
                   "file": filepath,
                   "line": i + 1,
                   "content": line.strip()[:100]
               })
   ```
3. Call it in `detect_smells()` after regex pass:
   ```python
   _detect_my_multiline_smell(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   ```

#### **Option C: AST-Based Smell (Most Powerful)**

1. Add entry to `SMELL_CHECKS` with `pattern=None`:
   ```python
   _smell("my_ast_smell", "Human label", "high")
   ```
2. Implement collector function in `smells_ast/_tree_quality_detectors.py` (or appropriate submodule):
   ```python
   def _detect_my_ast_smell(filepath: str, tree: ast.Module, all_nodes: tuple[ast.AST, ...]) -> list[SmellMatch]:
       results: list[SmellMatch] = []
       for node in all_nodes:
           if isinstance(node, ast.FunctionDef) and my_condition(node):
               results.append({
                   "file": filepath,
                   "line": node.lineno,
                   "content": f"my context: {node.name}"
               })
       return results
   ```
3. Register in `_dispatch.py`:
   ```python
   TREE_DETECTORS = (
       # ... existing ...
       _TreeDetectorSpec(
           "my_ast_smell",
           lambda filepath, tree, all_nodes: _detect_my_ast_smell(filepath, tree, all_nodes)
       ),
   )
   ```
4. Import at top of `_dispatch.py`:
   ```python
   from desloppify.languages.python.detectors.smells_ast._tree_quality_detectors import (
       _detect_my_ast_smell,
   )
   ```

#### **Option D: Register New Detector Dimension (Advanced)**

1. Add to `registry.py`:
   ```python
   DETECTORS["my_dimension"] = DetectorMeta(
       "my_dimension",
       "My dimension name",
       "Code quality",
       "manual_fix",
       "guidance text",
   )
   ```
2. Implement phase function in language's `phases.py`:
   ```python
   def phase_my_dimension(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
       entries, total = detect_my_dimension(path)
       results = [
           make_finding("my_dimension", e["file"], ..., tier=2, ...)
           for e in entries
       ]
       return results, {"my_dimension": total}
   ```
3. Add to language config's phases list in `__init__.py`

---

### 11. Summary: Key Data Structures & Patterns

| Concept | Type | Example | Purpose |
|---------|------|---------|---------|
| **SMELL_CHECKS** | `list[dict]` | `{"id": "eval_exec", "pattern": r"...", "severity": "high"}` | Registry of all detectable smells |
| **SmellMatch** | TypedDict | `{"file": "x.py", "line": 42, "content": "eval(..."}` | Individual smell occurrence |
| **Finding** | TypedDict | `{"id": "smells::x.py::eval_exec", "detector": "smells", "tier": 1, ...}` | Normalized finding in state |
| **DetectorMeta** | dataclass | `DetectorMeta("smells", "Code quality", "auto_fix", ...)` | Detector metadata & classification |
| **ComplexitySignal** | dataclass | `ComplexitySignal("long_functions", compute=compute_long_functions)` | Structural file metrics |
| **NodeCollector** | Callable | `(filepath, node: FunctionDef, tree) -> list[SmellMatch]` | Per-function AST detector |
| **TreeCollector** | Callable | `(filepath, tree: Module, all_nodes) -> list[SmellMatch]` | Whole-file AST detector |

---

### Key Architectural Insights

1. **Dual-track smell detection:** Regex fast-path (99% of cases) + AST deep analysis (cross-node patterns)
2. **Stable Finding IDs:** Format is `detector::file::name` — enables deduplication & change tracking
3. **Tier mapping:** Severity directly maps to actionability (high=auto-fix tier, medium/low=judgment)
4. **Registry-driven:** All detector metadata centralized in `core/registry.py` — single source of truth
5. **Phase isolation:** Each detector runs in its own phase, outputs normalized findings independently
6. **Smell de-duplication:** `merge_smell_matches()` prevents duplicates in the smell_counts dict
7. **Multi-line string awareness:** `_build_string_line_set()` prevents regex false positives in docstrings/comments

This architecture enables rapid addition of new detectors while maintaining consistency across Python, TypeScript, and future language plugins.

   177→        "pattern": r"\.sort\(\s*\)",

   206→
   207→def _build_ts_line_state(lines: list[str]) -> dict[int, str]:
   208→    """Build a map of line numbers that are inside block comments or template literals.
   209→
   210→    Returns {0-indexed line: reason} where reason is "block_comment" or "template_literal".
   211→    Lines not in the map are normal code lines suitable for regex checks.
   212→
   213→    Tracks:
   214→    - Block comment state (opened by /*, closed by */)
   215→    - Template literal state (opened by backtick, closed by backtick,
   216→      with ${} nesting awareness)
   217→    """
   218→    state: dict[int, str] = {}
   219→    in_block_comment = False
   220→    in_template = False
   221→    template_brace_depth = 0  # tracks ${} nesting inside template literals
   222→
   223→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   224→        if in_block_comment:
   225→            state[i] = "block_comment"
   226→            if "*/" in line:
   227→                in_block_comment = False
   228→            continue
   229→
   230→        if in_template:
   231→            state[i] = "template_literal"
   232→            # Scan for closing backtick or ${} nesting
   233→            j = 0
   234→            while j < len(line):
   235→                ch = line[j]
   236→                if ch == "\\" and j + 1 < len(line):
   237→                    j += 2
   238→                    continue
   239→                if ch == "$" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "{":
   240→                    template_brace_depth += 1
   241→                    j += 2
   242→                    continue
   243→                if ch == "}" and template_brace_depth > 0:
   244→                    template_brace_depth -= 1
   245→                    j += 1
   246→                    continue
   247→                if ch == "`" and template_brace_depth == 0:
   248→                    in_template = False
   249→                    # Rest of line is normal code — don't mark it
   250→                    # but we already marked the line; that's fine for
   251→                    # line-level filtering
   252→                    break
   253→                j += 1
   254→            continue
   255→
   256→        # Normal code line — check for block comment or template literal start
   257→        j = 0
   258→        in_str = None
   259→        while j < len(line):
   260→            ch = line[j]
   261→
   262→            # Skip escape sequences
   263→            if in_str and ch == "\\" and j + 1 < len(line):
   264→                j += 2
   265→                continue
   266→
   267→            # String tracking
   268→            if in_str:
   269→                if ch == in_str:
   270→                    in_str = None
   271→                j += 1
   272→                continue
   273→
   274→            # Line comment — rest is not code
   275→            if ch == "/" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "/":
   276→                break
   277→
   278→            # Block comment start
   279→            if ch == "/" and j + 1 < len(line) and line[j + 1] == "*":
   280→                # Check if it closes on same line
   281→                close = line.find("*/", j + 2)
   282→                if close != -1:
   283→                    j = close + 2
   284→                    continue
   285→                else:
   286→                    in_block_comment = True
   287→                    break
   288→
   289→            # Template literal start
   290→            if ch == "`":
   291→                # Scan for closing backtick on same line
   292→                k = j + 1
   293→                found_close = False
   294→                depth = 0
   295→                while k < len(line):
   296→                    c = line[k]
   297→                    if c == "\\" and k + 1 < len(line):
   298→                        k += 2
   299→                        continue
   300→                    if c == "$" and k + 1 < len(line) and line[k + 1] == "{":
   301→                        depth += 1
   302→                        k += 2
   303→                        continue
   304→                    if c == "}" and depth > 0:
   305→                        depth -= 1
   306→                        k += 1
   307→                        continue
   308→                    if c == "`" and depth == 0:
   309→                        found_close = True
   310→                        j = k + 1
   311→                        break
   312→                    k += 1
   313→                if found_close:
   314→                    continue
   315→                else:
   316→                    in_template = True
   317→                    template_brace_depth = depth
   318→                    break
   319→
   320→            if ch in ("'", '"'):
   321→                in_str = ch
   322→                j += 1
   323→                continue
   324→
   325→            j += 1
   326→
   327→    return state
   328→
   329→
   330→def _detect_empty_if_chains(filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> None:
   331→    """Find if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
   332→    index = 0
   333→    while index < len(lines):
   334→        stripped = lines[index].strip()
   335→        if not re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\(", stripped):
   336→            index += 1
   337→            continue
   338→
   339→        if re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", stripped):
   340→            chain_start = index
   341→            cursor = index + 1
   342→            while cursor < len(lines):
   343→                next_stripped = lines[cursor].strip()
   344→                if re.match(r"else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", next_stripped):
   345→                    cursor += 1
   346→                    continue
   347→                if re.match(r"(?:\}\s*)?else\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", next_stripped):
   348→                    cursor += 1
   349→                    continue
   350→                break
   351→            smell_counts["empty_if_chain"].append(
   352→                {"file": filepath, "line": chain_start + 1, "content": stripped[:100]}
   353→            )
   354→            index = cursor
   355→            continue
   356→
   357→        if re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", stripped):
   358→            chain_start = index
   359→            chain_all_empty = True
   360→            cursor = index
   361→            while cursor < len(lines):
   362→                current = lines[cursor].strip()
   363→                if cursor == chain_start:
   364→                    if not re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", current):
   365→                        chain_all_empty = False
   366→                        break
   367→                elif re.match(r"\}\s*else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", current):
   368→                    pass
   369→                elif re.match(r"\}\s*else\s*\{\s*$", current):
   370→                    pass
   371→                elif current == "}":
   372→                    tail = cursor + 1
   373→                    while tail < len(lines) and lines[tail].strip() == "":
   374→                        tail += 1
   375→                    if tail < len(lines) and re.match(r"else\s", lines[tail].strip()):
   376→                        cursor = tail
   377→                        continue
   378→                    cursor += 1
   379→                    break
   380→                elif current == "":
   381→                    cursor += 1
   382→                    continue
   383→                else:
   384→                    chain_all_empty = False
   385→                    break
   386→                cursor += 1
   387→            if chain_all_empty and cursor > chain_start + 1:
   388→                smell_counts["empty_if_chain"].append(
   389→                    {"file": filepath, "line": chain_start + 1, "content": lines[chain_start].strip()[:100]}
   390→                )
   391→            index = max(index + 1, cursor)
   392→            continue
   393→
   394→        index += 1
   395→
   396→
   397→def _detect_dead_useeffects(filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> None:
   398→    """Find useEffect calls with empty/whitespace/comment-only bodies."""
   399→    for line_no, line in enumerate(lines):
   400→        stripped = line.strip()
   401→        if not re.match(r"(?:React\.)?useEffect\s*\(\s*\(\s*\)\s*=>\s*\{", stripped):
   402→            continue
   403→
   404→        paren_depth = 0
   405→        end_line = None
   406→        for cursor in range(line_no, min(line_no + 30, len(lines))):
   407→            for _, ch, in_string in scan_code(lines[cursor]):
   408→                if in_string:
   409→                    continue
   410→                if ch == "(":
   411→                    paren_depth += 1
   412→                elif ch == ")":
   413→                    paren_depth -= 1
   414→                    if paren_depth <= 0:
   415→                        end_line = cursor
   416→                        break
   417→            if end_line is not None:
   418→                break
   419→
   420→        if end_line is None:
   421→            continue
   422→
   423→        text = "\n".join(lines[line_no:end_line + 1])
   424→        arrow_pos = text.find("=>")
   425→        if arrow_pos == -1:
   426→            continue
   427→        brace_pos = text.find("{", arrow_pos)
   428→        if brace_pos == -1:
   429→            continue
   430→
   431→        depth = 0
   432→        body_end = None
   433→        for idx, ch, in_string in scan_code(text, brace_pos):
   434→            if in_string:
   435→                continue
   436→            if ch == "{":
   437→                depth += 1
   438→            elif ch == "}":
   439→                depth -= 1
   440→                if depth == 0:
   441→                    body_end = idx
   442→                    break
   443→        if body_end is None:
   444→            continue
   445→
   446→        body = text[brace_pos + 1:body_end]
   447→        if _strip_ts_comments(body).strip() == "":
   448→            smell_counts["dead_useeffect"].append(
   449→                {"file": filepath, "line": line_no + 1, "content": stripped[:100]}
   450→            )
   451→
   452→
   453→def _script_is_documented(readme_text: str, script_name: str) -> bool:
   454→    escaped = re.escape(script_name)
   455→    command_patterns = [
   456→        rf"\bnpm\s+(?:run\s+)?{escaped}\b",
   457→        rf"\bpnpm\s+{escaped}\b",
   458→        rf"\byarn\s+{escaped}\b",
   459→        rf"\bbun\s+run\s+{escaped}\b",
   460→    ]
   461→    if any(re.search(pattern, readme_text, flags=re.IGNORECASE) for pattern in command_patterns):
   462→        return True
   463→    return bool(re.search(rf"`{escaped}`", readme_text))
   464→
   465→
   466→def _detect_non_ts_asset_smells(path: Path, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]) -> int:
   467→    """Scan adjacent non-TS assets (CSS/docs) for common repo-health smells."""
   468→    scanned_files = 0
   469→    css_files = find_source_files(path, [".css", ".scss", ".sass", ".less"])
   470→    scanned_files += len(css_files)
   471→
   472→    for filepath in css_files:
   473→        try:
   474→            full = Path(filepath) if Path(filepath).is_absolute() else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
   475→            content = full.read_text()
   476→            lines = content.splitlines()
   477→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
   478→            log_best_effort_failure(logger, f"read stylesheet smell candidate {filepath}", exc)
   479→            continue
   480→
   481→        if len(lines) >= 300:
   482→            smell_counts["css_monolith"].append(
   483→                {
   484→                    "file": filepath,
   485→                    "line": 1,
   486→                    "content": f"{len(lines)} LOC stylesheet",
   487→                }
   488→            )
   489→
   490→        important_count = content.count("!important")
   491→        if important_count >= 8:
   492→            first_line = next(
   493→                (idx + 1 for idx, line in enumerate(lines) if "!important" in line),
   494→                1,
   495→            )
   496→            smell_counts["css_important_overuse"].append(
   497→                {
   498→                    "file": filepath,
   499→                    "line": first_line,
   500→                    "content": f"{important_count} !important declarations",
   501→                }
   502→            )
   503→
   504→    readme_path = PROJECT_ROOT / "README.md"
   505→    package_path = PROJECT_ROOT / "package.json"
   506→    if not readme_path.is_file() or not package_path.is_file():
   507→        return scanned_files
   508→
   509→    scanned_files += 1
   510→    try:
   511→        readme_text = readme_path.read_text()
   512→        package_payload = json.loads(package_path.read_text())
   513→    except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
   514→        log_best_effort_failure(logger, "read package/readme for docs drift smell", exc)
   515→        return scanned_files
   516→
   517→    scripts = package_payload.get("scripts")
   518→    if not isinstance(scripts, dict):
   519→        return scanned_files
   520→
   521→    key_scripts = [
   522→        script for script in ("dev", "build", "test", "lint", "typecheck") if script in scripts
   523→    ]
   524→    if len(key_scripts) < 2:
   525→        return scanned_files
   526→
   527→    missing = [script for script in key_scripts if not _script_is_documented(readme_text, script)]
   528→    if len(missing) >= 2:
   529→        smell_counts["docs_scripts_drift"].append(
   530→            {
   531→                "file": "README.md",
   532→                "line": 1,
   533→                "content": f"Missing script docs: {', '.join(missing[:5])}",
   534→            }
   535→        )
   536→    return scanned_files
   537→
   538→
   539→def detect_smells(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   540→    """Detect TypeScript/React code smell patterns across the codebase.
   541→
   542→    Returns (entries, total_files_checked).
   543→    """
   544→    checks = TS_SMELL_CHECKS
   545→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {s["id"]: [] for s in checks}
   546→    files = find_ts_files(path)
   547→
   548→    for filepath in files:
   549→        if "node_modules" in filepath or ".d.ts" in filepath:
   550→            continue
   551→        try:
   552→            p = (
   553→                Path(filepath)
   554→                if Path(filepath).is_absolute()
   555→                else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
   556→            )
   557→            content = p.read_text()
   558→            lines = content.splitlines()
   559→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
   560→            log_best_effort_failure(
   561→                logger, f"read TypeScript smell candidate {filepath}", exc
   562→            )
   563→            continue
   564→
   565→        # Build line state for string/comment filtering
   566→        line_state = _build_ts_line_state(lines)
   567→
   568→        # Regex-based smells
   569→        for check in checks:
   570→            if check["pattern"] is None:
   571→                continue
   572→            for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   573→                # Skip lines inside block comments or template literals
   574→                if i in line_state:
   575→                    continue
   576→                m = re.search(check["pattern"], line)
   577→                if not m:
   578→                    continue
   579→                # Check if match is inside a single-line string or comment
   580→                if _ts_match_is_in_string(line, m.start()):
   581→                    continue
   582→                # Skip URLs assigned to module-level constants
   583→                if check["id"] == "hardcoded_url" and re.match(
   584→                    r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*\s*=",
   585→                    line.strip(),
   586→                ):
   587→                    continue
   588→                smell_counts[check["id"]].append(
   589→                    {
   590→                        "file": filepath,
   591→                        "line": i + 1,
   592→                        "content": line.strip()[:100],
   593→                    }
   594→                )
   595→
   596→        # Multi-line smell helpers (brace-tracked)
   597→        _detect_async_no_await(filepath, content, lines, smell_counts)
   598→        _detect_error_no_throw(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   599→        _detect_empty_if_chains(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   600→        _detect_dead_useeffects(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   601→        _detect_swallowed_errors(filepath, content, lines, smell_counts)
   602→        _detect_monster_functions(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   603→        _detect_dead_functions(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   604→        _detect_window_globals(filepath, lines, line_state, smell_counts)
   605→        _detect_catch_return_default(filepath, content, smell_counts)
   606→        _detect_switch_no_default(filepath, content, smell_counts)
   607→
   608→    non_ts_files = _detect_non_ts_asset_smells(path, smell_counts)
   609→
   610→    # Build summary entries sorted by severity then count
   611→    severity_order = {"high": 0, "medium": 1, "low": 2}
   612→    entries = []
   613→    for check in checks:
   614→        matches = smell_counts[check["id"]]
   615→        if matches:
   616→            entries.append(
   617→                {
   618→                    "id": check["id"],
   619→                    "label": check["label"],
   620→                    "severity": check["severity"],
   621→                    "count": len(matches),
   622→                    "files": len(set(m["file"] for m in matches)),
   623→                    "matches": matches[:50],
   624→                }
   625→            )
   626→    entries.sort(key=lambda e: (severity_order.get(e["severity"], 9), -e["count"]))
   627→    return entries, len(files) + non_ts_files
   628→
```

1→"""Multi-line smell detection helpers (brace-tracked).
     2→
     3→Shared utilities (string-aware scanning, brace tracking, comment stripping)
     4→plus a handful of smell detectors. Monster-function, dead-function,
     5→window-global, catch-return-default, and switch-no-default detectors live
     6→in _smell_detectors.py.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→import re
    12→from collections.abc import Generator
    13→
    14→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import strip_c_style_comments
    15→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_effects import (
    16→    detect_dead_useeffects as _detect_dead_useeffects_impl,
    17→)

    28→    track_brace_body as _track_brace_body_impl,
    29→)
    30→
    31→
    32→def scan_code(
    33→    text: str, start: int = 0, end: int | None = None
    34→) -> Generator[tuple[int, str, bool], None, None]:
    35→    """Yield (index, char, in_string) tuples, handling escapes correctly.
    36→
    37→    Skips escaped characters (\\x) by advancing +2 instead of +1.
    38→    Tracks single-quoted, double-quoted, and template literal strings.
    39→    Correct for \\\\\" (escaped backslash before quote) where prev_ch pattern fails.
    40→    """
    41→    i = start
    42→    limit = end if end is not None else len(text)
    43→    in_str = None
    44→    while i < limit:
    45→        ch = text[i]
    46→        if in_str:
    47→            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < limit:
    48→                yield (i, ch, True)
    49→                i += 1
    50→                yield (i, text[i], True)
    51→                i += 1
    52→                continue
    53→            if ch == in_str:
    54→                in_str = None
    55→            yield (i, ch, in_str is not None)
    56→        else:
    57→            if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
    58→                in_str = ch
    59→                yield (i, ch, True)
    60→            else:
    61→                yield (i, ch, False)
    62→        i += 1
    63→
    64→
    65→def _strip_ts_comments(text: str) -> str:
    66→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving strings.
    67→
    68→    Delegates to the shared implementation in utils.py.
    69→    """
    70→    return strip_c_style_comments(text)
    71→
    72→
    73→def _ts_match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
    74→    """Check if a match position falls inside a string literal or comment on a single line.
    75→
    76→    Mirrors Python's _match_is_in_string but for TS syntax (', ", `, //).
    77→    """
    78→    i = 0
    79→    in_str = None
    80→
    81→    while i < len(line):
    82→        if i == match_start:
    83→            return in_str is not None
    84→
    85→        ch = line[i]
    86→
    87→        # Escape sequences inside strings
    88→        if in_str and ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
    89→            i += 2
    90→            continue
    91→
    92→        if in_str:
    93→            if ch == in_str:
    94→                in_str = None
    95→            i += 1
    96→            continue
    97→
    98→        # Line comment — everything after is non-code
    99→        if ch == "/" and i + 1 < len(line) and line[i + 1] == "/":
   100→            return match_start > i
   101→
   102→        if ch in ("'", '"', "`"):
   103→            in_str = ch
   104→            i += 1
   105→            continue
   106→
   107→        i += 1
   108→
   109→    return False
   110→
   111→
   112→def _detect_async_no_await(
   113→    filepath: str, content: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   114→):
   115→    """Find async functions that don't use await.
   116→
   117→    Algorithm: for each async declaration, track brace depth to find the function
   118→    body extent (up to 200 lines). Scan each line for 'await' within those braces.
   119→    If the opening brace closes (depth returns to 0) without seeing await, flag it.
   120→    """
   121→    async_re = re.compile(r"(?:async\s+function\s+(\w+)|(\w+)\s*=\s*async)")
   122→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   123→        m = async_re.search(line)
   124→        if not m:
   125→            continue
   126→        name = m.group(1) or m.group(2)
   127→        brace_depth = 0
   128→        found_open = False
   129→        has_await = False
   130→        for j in range(i, min(i + 200, len(lines))):
   131→            body_line = lines[j]
   132→            prev_code_ch = ""
   133→            for _, ch, in_s in scan_code(body_line):
   134→                if in_s:
   135→                    continue
   136→                if ch == "/" and prev_code_ch == "/":
   137→                    break  # Rest of line is comment
   138→                elif ch == "{":
   139→                    brace_depth += 1
   140→                    found_open = True
   141→                elif ch == "}":
   142→                    brace_depth -= 1
   143→                prev_code_ch = ch
   144→            if "await " in body_line or "await\n" in body_line:
   145→                has_await = True
   146→            if found_open and brace_depth <= 0:
   147→                break
   148→
   149→        if found_open and not has_await:
   150→            smell_counts["async_no_await"].append(
   151→                {
   152→                    "file": filepath,
   153→                    "line": i + 1,
   154→                    "content": f"async {name or '(anonymous)'} has no await",
   155→                }
   156→            )
   157→
   158→
   159→def _detect_error_no_throw(
   160→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   161→):
   162→    """Find console.error calls not followed by throw or return."""
   163→    _detect_error_no_throw_impl(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   164→
   165→
   166→def _detect_empty_if_chains(
   167→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   168→):
   169→    """Find if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
   170→    _detect_empty_if_chains_impl(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   171→
   172→
   173→def _detect_dead_useeffects(
   174→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   175→):
   176→    """Find useEffect calls with empty or whitespace/comment-only bodies.
   177→
   178→    Algorithm: two-pass brace/paren tracking with string-escape awareness.
   179→    Pass 1: track paren depth to find the full useEffect(...) extent.
   180→    Pass 2: within that extent, find the arrow body ({...} after =>) using
   181→    brace depth, skipping characters inside string literals (', ", `).
   182→    Then strip comments from the body and check if anything remains.
   183→    """
   184→    _detect_dead_useeffects_impl(
   185→        filepath,
   186→        lines,
   187→        smell_counts,
   188→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   189→        strip_ts_comments_fn=_strip_ts_comments,
   190→    )
   191→
   192→
   193→def _detect_swallowed_errors(
   194→    filepath: str, content: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   195→):
   196→    """Find catch blocks whose only content is console.error/warn/log (swallowed errors).
   197→
   198→    Algorithm: regex-find each `catch(...) {`, then track brace depth with
   199→    string-escape awareness to extract the catch body (up to 500 chars).
   200→    Strip comments, split into statements, and check if every statement
   201→    is a console.error/warn/log call.
   202→    """
   203→    _detect_swallowed_errors_impl(
   204→        filepath,
   205→        content,
   206→        lines,
   207→        smell_counts,
   208→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   209→        strip_ts_comments_fn=_strip_ts_comments,
   210→    )
   211→
   212→
   213→def _track_brace_body(
   214→    lines: list[str], start_line: int, *, max_scan: int = 2000
   215→) -> int | None:
   216→    """Find the closing brace that matches the first opening brace from start_line.
   217→
   218→    Tracks brace depth with string-literal awareness (', ", `).
   219→    Returns the line index of the closing brace, or None if not found.
   220→    """
   221→    return _track_brace_body_impl(
   222→        lines,
   223→        start_line,
   224→        scan_code_fn=scan_code,
   225→        max_scan=max_scan,
   226→    )
   227→
```

    59→
    60→def _compute_ts_destructure_props(content, lines):
    61→    long_destructures = re.findall(r"\{\s*(\w+(?:\s*,\s*\w+){8,})\s*\}", content)
    62→    if not long_destructures:
    63→        return None
    64→    max_props = max(len(d.split(",")) for d in long_destructures)
    65→    return max_props, f"destructure w/{max_props} props"
    66→
    67→
    68→def _compute_ts_inline_types(content, lines):
    69→    inline_types = len(
    70→        re.findall(r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:type|interface)\s+\w+", content, re.MULTILINE)
    71→    )
    72→    if inline_types > 3:
    73→        return inline_types, f"{inline_types} inline types"
    74→    return None
    75→
    76→
    77→# ── Config data (single source of truth) ──────────────────
    78→
    79→
    80→TS_COMPLEXITY_SIGNALS = [
    81→    ComplexitySignal("imports", r"^import\s", weight=1, threshold=15),
    82→    ComplexitySignal(
    83→        "destructured props",
    84→        None,
    85→        weight=1,
    86→        threshold=8,
    87→        compute=_compute_ts_destructure_props,
    88→    ),
    89→    ComplexitySignal("useEffects", r"useEffect\s*\(", weight=3, threshold=3),
    90→    ComplexitySignal(
    91→        "inline types", None, weight=1, threshold=3, compute=_compute_ts_inline_types
    92→    ),
    93→    ComplexitySignal("TODOs", r"//\s*(?:TODO|FIXME|HACK|XXX)", weight=2, threshold=0),

    95→        "nested ternaries", r"[^?]\?[^?.:\n][^:\n]*[^?]\?[^?.]", weight=3, threshold=2
    96→    ),
    97→    ComplexitySignal("useRefs", r"\buseRef\s*[<(]", weight=2, threshold=6),
    98→]
    99→

   137→def phase_logs(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   138→    log_result = logs_detector_mod.detect_logs_result(path)
   139→    log_entries = log_result.entries
   140→    total_files = log_result.population_size
   141→    log_groups: dict[tuple, list] = defaultdict(list)
   142→    for e in log_entries:
   143→        log_groups[(e["file"], e["tag"])].append(e)
   144→    results = []
   145→    for (file, tag), entries in log_groups.items():
   146→        results.append(
   147→            make_finding(
   148→                "logs",
   149→                file,
   150→                tag,
   151→                tier=1,
   152→                confidence="high",
   153→                summary=f"{len(entries)} tagged logs [{tag}]",
   154→                detail={
   155→                    "count": len(entries),
   156→                    "lines": [e["line"] for e in entries[:20]],
   157→                },
   158→            )
   159→        )
   160→    log(f"         {len(log_entries)} instances → {len(results)} findings")
   161→    return results, {"logs": adjust_potential(lang.zone_map, total_files)}
   162→
   163→
   164→def phase_unused(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   165→    entries, total_files = unused_detector_mod.detect_unused(path)
   166→    return make_unused_findings(entries, log), {
   167→        "unused": adjust_potential(lang.zone_map, total_files),
   168→    }
   169→
   170→
   171→def phase_exports(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   172→    export_entries, total_exports = exports_detector_mod.detect_dead_exports(path)
   173→    results = []
   174→    for e in export_entries:
   175→        results.append(
   176→            make_finding(
   177→                "exports",
   178→                e["file"],
   179→                e["name"],
   180→                tier=2,
   181→                confidence="high",
   182→                summary=f"Dead export: {e['name']}",
   183→                detail={"line": e.get("line"), "kind": e.get("kind")},
   184→            )
   185→        )
   186→    log(f"         {len(export_entries)} instances → {len(results)} findings")
   187→    return results, {"exports": total_exports}
   188→
   189→
   190→def phase_deprecated(
   191→    path: Path, lang: LangRun
   192→) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   193→    dep_result = deprecated_detector_mod.detect_deprecated_result(path)
   194→    dep_entries = dep_result.entries
   195→    total_deprecated = dep_result.population_size
   196→    results = []
   197→    for e in dep_entries:
   198→        if e["kind"] == "property":
   199→            continue
   200→        tier = 1 if e["importers"] == 0 else 3
   201→        results.append(
   202→            make_finding(
   203→                "deprecated",
   204→                e["file"],
   205→                e["symbol"],
   206→                tier=tier,
   207→                confidence="high",
   208→                summary=f"Deprecated: {e['symbol']} ({e['importers']} importers)"
   209→                + (" → safe to delete" if e["importers"] == 0 else ""),
   210→                detail={"importers": e["importers"], "line": e["line"]},
   211→            )
   212→        )
   213→    log(
   214→        f"         {len(dep_entries)} instances → {len(results)} findings (properties suppressed)"
   215→    )
   216→    return results, {"deprecated": total_deprecated}
   217→
   218→
   219→def phase_structural(
   220→    path: Path, lang: LangRun
   221→) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   222→    structural: dict[str, dict] = {}
   223→
   224→    large_entries, file_count = large_detector_mod.detect_large_files(
   225→        path,
   226→        file_finder=lang.file_finder,
   227→        threshold=lang.large_threshold,
   228→    )
   229→    for e in large_entries:
   230→        add_structural_signal(
   231→            structural, e["file"], f"large ({e['loc']} LOC)", {"loc": e["loc"]}
   232→        )
   233→
   234→    complexity_entries, _ = complexity_detector_mod.detect_complexity(
   235→        path,
   236→        signals=TS_COMPLEXITY_SIGNALS,
   237→        file_finder=lang.file_finder,
   238→        threshold=lang.complexity_threshold,
   239→    )
   240→    for e in complexity_entries:
   241→        add_structural_signal(
   242→            structural,
   243→            e["file"],
   244→            f"complexity score {e['score']}",
   245→            {"complexity_score": e["score"], "complexity_signals": e["signals"]},
   246→        )
   247→        lang.complexity_map[e["file"]] = e["score"]
   248→
   249→    god_entries, _ = gods_detector_mod.detect_gods(
   250→        extract_ts_components(path), TS_GOD_RULES, min_reasons=2
   251→    )
   252→    for e in god_entries:
   253→        add_structural_signal(
   254→            structural,
   255→            e["file"],
   256→            f"{e['detail'].get('hook_total', 0)} hooks ({', '.join(e['reasons'][:2])})",
   257→            {
   258→                "hook_total": e["detail"].get("hook_total", 0),
   259→                "hook_reasons": e["reasons"],
   260→            },
   261→        )
   262→
   263→    concern_entries, _ = concerns_detector_mod.detect_mixed_concerns(path)
   264→    for e in concern_entries:
   265→        add_structural_signal(
   266→            structural,
   267→            e["file"],
   268→            f"mixed: {', '.join(e['concerns'][:3])}",
   269→            {"concerns": e["concerns"]},
   270→        )
   271→
   272→    results = merge_structural_signals(structural, log)
   273→
   274→    # Flat directories (too many files → missing sub-organization)
   275→    flat_entries, dir_count = flat_dirs_detector_mod.detect_flat_dirs(
   276→        path, file_finder=lang.file_finder
   277→    )
   278→    for e in flat_entries:
   279→        results.append(
   280→            make_finding(
   281→                "flat_dirs",
   282→                e["directory"],
   283→                "",
   284→                tier=3,
   285→                confidence="medium",
   286→                summary=f"Flat directory: {e['file_count']} files — consider grouping by domain",
   287→                detail={"file_count": e["file_count"]},
   288→            )
   289→        )
   290→    if flat_entries:
   291→        log(f"         flat dirs: {len(flat_entries)} directories with 20+ files")
   292→
   293→    # TS-specific: props bloat
   294→    props_thresh = lang.props_threshold
   295→    prop_entries, prop_count = props_detector_mod.detect_prop_interface_bloat(
   296→        path, threshold=props_thresh
   297→    )
   298→    for e in prop_entries:
   299→        pc = e["prop_count"]
   300→        # Tiered severity: 15-29=low, 30-49=medium, 50+=high
   301→        if pc >= 50:
   302→            conf, tier = "high", 4
   303→        elif pc >= 30:
   304→            conf, tier = "medium", 3
   305→        else:
   306→            conf, tier = "low", 3
   307→        results.append(
   308→            make_finding(
   309→                "props",
   310→                e["file"],
   311→                e["interface"],
   312→                tier=tier,
   313→                confidence=conf,
   314→                summary=f"Bloated {e.get('kind', 'props')}: {e['interface']} ({pc} fields)",
   315→                detail={
   316→                    "prop_count": pc,
   317→                    "line": e["line"],
   318→                    "kind": e.get("kind", "props"),
   319→                },
   320→            )
   321→        )
   322→
   323→    # TS-specific: passthrough components
   324→    pt_entries = detect_passthrough_components(path)
   325→    for e in pt_entries:
   326→        results.append(
   327→            make_finding(
   328→                "props",
   329→                e["file"],
   330→                f"passthrough::{e['component']}",
   331→                tier=e["tier"],
   332→                confidence=e["confidence"],
   333→                summary=f"Passthrough component: {e['component']} "
   334→                f"({e['passthrough']}/{e['total_props']} props forwarded, {e['ratio']:.0%})",
   335→                detail={
   336→                    "passthrough": e["passthrough"],
   337→                    "total_props": e["total_props"],
   338→                    "ratio": e["ratio"],
   339→                    "line": e["line"],
   340→                    "passthrough_props": e["passthrough_props"],
   341→                    "direct_props": e["direct_props"],
   342→                },
   343→            )
   344→        )
   345→    potentials = {
   346→        "structural": adjust_potential(lang.zone_map, file_count),
   347→        "flat_dirs": dir_count,
   348→        "props": max(prop_count, len(pt_entries)) if prop_count else len(pt_entries),
   349→    }
   350→    return results, potentials
   351→
   352→
   353→def _make_boundary_findings(
   354→    single_entries: list[dict],
   355→    path: Path,
   356→    graph: dict,
   357→    lang: LangRun,
   358→    shared_prefix: str,
   359→    tools_prefix: str,
   360→) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   361→    """Create boundary-candidate findings, deduplicated against single-use."""
   362→    single_use_emitted = set()
   363→    for e in single_entries:
   364→        is_size_ok = 50 <= e["loc"] <= 200
   365→        is_colocated = lang.get_area and (
   366→            lang.get_area(rel(e["file"])) == lang.get_area(e["sole_importer"])
   367→        )
   368→        if not is_size_ok and not is_colocated:
   369→            single_use_emitted.add(rel(e["file"]))
   370→
   371→    results = []
   372→    deduped = 0
   373→    boundary_entries, total_shared = coupling_detector_mod.detect_boundary_candidates(
   374→        path,
   375→        graph,
   376→        shared_prefix=shared_prefix,
   377→        tools_prefix=tools_prefix,
   378→        skip_basenames={"index.ts", "index.tsx"},
   379→    )
   380→    for e in boundary_entries:
   381→        if rel(e["file"]) in single_use_emitted:
   382→            deduped += 1
   383→            continue
   384→        results.append(
   385→            make_finding(
   386→                "coupling",
   387→                e["file"],
   388→                f"boundary::{e['sole_tool']}",
   389→                tier=3,
   390→                confidence="medium",
   391→                summary=f"Boundary candidate ({e['loc']} LOC): only used by {e['sole_tool']} "
   392→                f"({e['importer_count']} importers)",
   393→                detail={
   394→                    "sole_tool": e["sole_tool"],
   395→                    "importer_count": e["importer_count"],
   396→                    "loc": e["loc"],
   397→                },
   398→            )
   399→        )
   400→    if deduped:
   401→        log(f"         ({deduped} boundary candidates skipped — covered by single_use)")
   402→    return results, total_shared
   403→
   404→
   405→def phase_coupling(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   406→    results = []
   407→    graph = deps_detector_mod.build_dep_graph(path)
   408→    lang.dep_graph = graph
   409→    zm = lang.zone_map
   410→
   411→    # Single-use (shared helper)
   412→    single_entries, single_candidates = (
   413→        single_use_detector_mod.detect_single_use_abstractions(
   414→            path, graph, barrel_names=lang.barrel_names
   415→        )
   416→    )
   417→    single_entries = filter_entries(zm, single_entries, "single_use")
   418→    results.extend(
   419→        make_single_use_findings(
   420→            single_entries, lang.get_area, skip_dir_names={"commands"}, stderr_fn=log
   421→        )
   422→    )
   423→    shared_prefix = f"{SRC_PATH}/shared/"
   424→    tools_prefix = f"{SRC_PATH}/tools/"
   425→    coupling_entries, coupling_edges = coupling_detector_mod.detect_coupling_violations(
   426→        path, graph, shared_prefix=shared_prefix, tools_prefix=tools_prefix
   427→    )
   428→    coupling_entries = filter_entries(zm, coupling_entries, "coupling")
   429→    for e in coupling_entries:
   430→        results.append(
   431→            make_finding(
   432→                "coupling",
   433→                e["file"],
   434→                e["target"],
   435→                tier=2,
   436→                confidence="high",
   437→                summary=f"Backwards coupling: shared imports {e['target']} (tool: {e['tool']})",
   438→                detail={
   439→                    "target": e["target"],
   440→                    "tool": e["tool"],
   441→                    "direction": e["direction"],
   442→                },
   443→            )
   444→        )
   445→
   446→    # TS-specific: boundary candidates (deduplicated against single-use)
   447→    boundary_findings, _ = _make_boundary_findings(
   448→        single_entries, path, graph, lang, shared_prefix, tools_prefix
   449→    )
   450→    results.extend(boundary_findings)
   451→
   452→    # TS-specific: cross-tool imports
   453→    cross_tool, cross_edges = coupling_detector_mod.detect_cross_tool_imports(
   454→        path, graph, tools_prefix=tools_prefix
   455→    )
   456→    cross_tool = filter_entries(zm, cross_tool, "coupling")
   457→    for e in cross_tool:
   458→        results.append(
   459→            make_finding(
   460→                "coupling",
   461→                e["file"],
   462→                e["target"],
   463→                tier=2,
   464→                confidence="high",
   465→                summary=f"Cross-tool import: {e['source_tool']}→{e['target_tool']} ({e['target']})",
   466→                detail={
   467→                    "target": e["target"],
   468→                    "source_tool": e["source_tool"],
   469→                    "target_tool": e["target_tool"],
   470→                    "direction": e["direction"],
   471→                },
   472→            )
   473→        )
   474→    if cross_tool:
   475→        log(f"         cross-tool: {len(cross_tool)} imports")
   476→
   477→    # Cycles + orphaned (shared helpers)
   478→    cycle_entries, _ = graph_detector_mod.detect_cycles(graph)
   479→    cycle_entries = filter_entries(zm, cycle_entries, "cycles", file_key="files")
   480→    results.extend(make_cycle_findings(cycle_entries, log))
   481→    orphan_entries, total_graph_files = orphaned_detector_mod.detect_orphaned_files(
   482→        path,
   483→        graph,
   484→        extensions=lang.extensions,
   485→        options=orphaned_detector_mod.OrphanedDetectionOptions(
   486→            extra_entry_patterns=lang.entry_patterns,
   487→            extra_barrel_names=lang.barrel_names,
   488→            dynamic_import_finder=deps_detector_mod.build_dynamic_import_targets,
   489→            alias_resolver=deps_detector_mod.ts_alias_resolver,
   490→        ),
   491→    )
   492→    orphan_entries = filter_entries(zm, orphan_entries, "orphaned")
   493→    results.extend(make_orphaned_findings(orphan_entries, log))
   494→
   495→    # Re-export facades (shared detector)
   496→    facade_entries, _ = facade_detector_mod.detect_reexport_facades(graph)
   497→    facade_entries = filter_entries(zm, facade_entries, "facade")
   498→    results.extend(make_facade_findings(facade_entries, log))
   499→
   500→    # TS-specific: pattern consistency
   501→    pattern_result = patterns_detector_mod.detect_pattern_anomalies_result(path)
   502→    pattern_entries = pattern_result.entries
   503→    total_areas = pattern_result.population_size
   504→    for e in pattern_entries:
   505→        results.append(
   506→            make_finding(
   507→                "patterns",
   508→                e["area"],
   509→                e["family"],
   510→                tier=3,
   511→                confidence=e.get("confidence", "low"),
   512→                summary=f"Competing patterns ({e['family']}): {e['review'][:120]}",
   513→                detail={
   514→                    "family": e["family"],
   515→                    "patterns_used": e["patterns_used"],
   516→                    "pattern_count": e["pattern_count"],
   517→                    "review": e["review"],
   518→                },
   519→            )
   520→        )
   521→
   522→    # TS-specific: naming consistency
   523→    naming_entries, total_dirs = naming_detector_mod.detect_naming_inconsistencies(
   524→        path,
   525→        file_finder=lang.file_finder,
   526→        skip_names=TS_SKIP_NAMES,
   527→        skip_dirs=TS_SKIP_DIRS,
   528→    )
   529→    for e in naming_entries:
   530→        results.append(
   531→            make_finding(
   532→                "naming",
   533→                e["directory"],
   534→                e["minority"],
   535→                tier=3,
   536→                confidence="low",
   537→                summary=f"Naming inconsistency: {e['minority_count']} {e['minority']} files "
   538→                f"in {e['majority']}-majority dir ({e['total_files']} total)",
   539→                detail={
   540→                    "majority": e["majority"],
   541→                    "majority_count": e["majority_count"],
   542→                    "minority": e["minority"],
   543→                    "minority_count": e["minority_count"],
   544→                    "outliers": e["outliers"],
   545→                },
   546→            )
   547→        )
   548→    log(f"         → {len(results)} coupling/structural findings total")
   549→    potentials = {
   550→        "single_use": adjust_potential(zm, single_candidates),
   551→        "coupling": coupling_edges + cross_edges,
   552→        "cycles": adjust_potential(zm, total_graph_files),
   553→        "orphaned": adjust_potential(zm, total_graph_files),
   554→        "patterns": total_areas,
   555→        "naming": total_dirs,
   556→        "facade": adjust_potential(zm, total_graph_files),
   557→    }
   558→    return results, potentials
   559→
   560→
   561→def phase_smells(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
   562→    smell_entries, total_smell_files = smells_detector_mod.detect_smells(path)
   563→    results = make_smell_findings(smell_entries, log)
   564→
   565→    # Cross-file: signature variance
   566→    functions = lang.extract_functions(path) if lang.extract_functions else []
   567→    sig_entries, _ = signature_detector_mod.detect_signature_variance(functions)
   568→    for e in sig_entries:
   569→        results.append(
   570→            make_finding(
   571→                "smells",
   572→                e["files"][0],
   573→                f"sig_variance::{e['name']}",
   574→                tier=3,
   575→                confidence="medium",
   576→                summary=f"Signature variance: {e['name']}() has {e['signature_count']} "
   577→                f"different signatures across {e['file_count']} files",
   578→                detail={
   579→                    "function": e["name"],
   580→                    "file_count": e["file_count"],
   581→                    "signature_count": e["signature_count"],
   582→                    "variants": e["variants"][:5],
   583→                },
   584→            )
   585→        )
   586→    if sig_entries:
   587→        log(
   588→            f"         signature variance: {len(sig_entries)} functions with inconsistent signatures"
   589→        )
   590→
   591→    # TS-specific: React state sync anti-patterns
   592→    react_entries, total_effects = react_detector_mod.detect_state_sync(path)
   593→    for e in react_entries:
   594→        setter_str = ", ".join(e["setters"])
   595→        results.append(
   596→            make_finding(
   597→                "react",
   598→                e["file"],
   599→                setter_str,
   600→                tier=3,
   601→                confidence="medium",
   602→                summary=f"State sync anti-pattern: useEffect only calls {setter_str}",
   603→                detail={"line": e["line"], "setters": e["setters"]},
   604→            )
   605→        )
   606→    if react_entries:
   607→        log(f"         react: {len(react_entries)} state sync anti-patterns")
   608→
   609→    # TS-specific: Context provider nesting depth
   610→    nesting_entries, _ = react_detector_mod.detect_context_nesting(path)
   611→    for e in nesting_entries:
   612→        providers_str = " → ".join(e["providers"][:5])
   613→        results.append(
   614→            make_finding(
   615→                "react",
   616→                e["file"],
   617→                f"nesting::{e['depth']}",
   618→                tier=3,
   619→                confidence="medium",
   620→                summary=f"Deep provider nesting ({e['depth']} levels): {providers_str}",
   621→                detail={"depth": e["depth"], "providers": e["providers"]},
   622→            )
   623→        )
   624→    if nesting_entries:
   625→        log(f"         react: {len(nesting_entries)} deep provider nesting")
   626→
   627→    # TS-specific: Hook return bloat
   628→    hook_entries, _ = react_detector_mod.detect_hook_return_bloat(path)
   629→    for e in hook_entries:
   630→        results.append(
   631→            make_finding(
   632→                "react",
   633→                e["file"],
   634→                f"hook_bloat::{e['hook']}",
   635→                tier=3,
   636→                confidence="medium",
   637→                summary=f"Hook return bloat: {e['hook']} returns {e['field_count']} fields",
   638→                detail={
   639→                    "hook": e["hook"],
   640→                    "field_count": e["field_count"],
   641→                    "line": e["line"],
   642→                },
   643→            )
   644→        )
   645→    if hook_entries:
   646→        log(f"         react: {len(hook_entries)} bloated hook returns")
   647→
   648→    # TS-specific: Boolean state explosion
   649→    bool_entries, _ = react_detector_mod.detect_boolean_state_explosion(path)
   650→    for e in bool_entries:
   651→        states_str = ", ".join(e["states"][:5])
   652→        results.append(
   653→            make_finding(
   654→                "react",
   655→                e["file"],
   656→                f"bool_state::{e['prefix']}",
   657→                tier=3,
   658→                confidence="low",
   659→                summary=f"Boolean state explosion: {e['count']} boolean useState hooks ({states_str})",
   660→                detail={
   661→                    "count": e["count"],
   662→                    "setters": e["setters"],
   663→                    "states": e["states"],
   664→                    "line": e["line"],
   665→                },
   666→            )
   667→        )
   668→    if bool_entries:
   669→        log(f"         react: {len(bool_entries)} boolean state explosions")
   670→
   671→    return results, {
   672→        "smells": adjust_potential(lang.zone_map, total_smell_files),
   673→        "react": total_effects,
   674→    }
   675→
   676→
   677→# Backward-compatible aliases for internal callers/tests.
   678→_phase_logs = phase_logs
   679→_phase_unused = phase_unused
   680→_phase_exports = phase_exports
   681→_phase_deprecated = phase_deprecated
   682→_phase_structural = phase_structural
   683→_phase_coupling = phase_coupling
   684→_phase_smells = phase_smells
   685→

1→"""TypeScript function-level smell detectors — monster functions, dead functions, etc."""
     2→
     3→import re
     4→
     5→from desloppify.languages.typescript.detectors._smell_helpers import (
     6→    _strip_ts_comments,
     7→    _track_brace_body,
     8→    _ts_match_is_in_string,
     9→    scan_code,
    10→)
    11→
    12→_MAX_CATCH_BODY = 1000  # max characters to scan for catch block body
    13→_MAX_SWITCH_BODY_SCAN = 5000
    14→
    15→
    16→def _find_block_end(content: str, brace_start: int, max_scan: int) -> int | None:
    17→    depth = 0
    18→    for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(content, brace_start, min(brace_start + max_scan, len(content))):
    19→        if in_s:
    20→            continue
    21→        if ch == "{":
    22→            depth += 1
    23→        elif ch == "}":
    24→            depth -= 1
    25→            if depth == 0:
    26→                return ci
    27→    return None
    28→
    29→
    30→def _line_no_and_preview(content: str, match_start: int) -> tuple[int, str]:
    31→    line_no = content[:match_start].count("\n") + 1
    32→    line_start = content.rfind("\n", 0, match_start) + 1
    33→    line_end = content.find("\n", match_start)
    34→    if line_end == -1:
    35→        line_end = len(content)
    36→    return line_no, content[line_start:line_end].strip()[:100]
    37→
    38→
    39→def _extract_returned_object_body(body: str) -> str | None:
    40→    return_obj = re.search(r"\breturn\s*\{", body)
    41→    if not return_obj:
    42→        return None
    43→    obj_start = body.find("{", return_obj.start())
    44→    obj_end = _find_block_end(body, obj_start, len(body))
    45→    if obj_end is None:
    46→        return None
    47→    return body[obj_start + 1:obj_end]
    48→
    49→def _find_function_start(line: str, next_lines: list[str]) -> str | None:
    50→    """Return function name for declarations/assignments, else None."""
    51→    stripped = line.strip()
    52→    if not stripped:
    53→        return None
    54→    if stripped.startswith(("interface ", "type ", "enum ", "class ")):
    55→        return None
    56→
    57→    declaration_match = re.match(
    58→        r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:default\s+)?(?:async\s+)?function\s+([A-Za-z_]\w*)\s*\(",
    59→        stripped,
    60→    )
    61→    if declaration_match:
    62→        return declaration_match.group(1)
    63→
    64→    assignment_match = re.match(
    65→        r"^(?:export\s+)?(?:const|let|var)\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\b",
    66→        stripped,
    67→    )
    68→    if not assignment_match:
    69→        return None
    70→
    71→    combined = "\n".join(
    72→        [stripped] + [next_line.strip() for next_line in next_lines[:2]]
    73→    )
    74→    eq_pos = combined.find("=", assignment_match.end())
    75→    if eq_pos == -1:
    76→        return None
    77→    after_eq = combined[eq_pos + 1 :].lstrip()
    78→    if re.match(r"(?:async\s+)?\([^)]*\)\s*=>", after_eq):
    79→        return assignment_match.group(1)
    80→    if re.match(r"function\b", after_eq):
    81→        return assignment_match.group(1)
    82→    return None
    83→
    84→def _find_opening_brace_line(lines: list[str], start: int, *, window: int = 5) -> int | None:
    85→    for idx in range(start, min(start + window, len(lines))):
    86→        if "{" in lines[idx]:
    87→            return idx
    88→    return None
    89→
    90→
    91→def _detect_monster_functions(
    92→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
    93→):
    94→    """Find functions/components exceeding 150 LOC via brace-tracking.
    95→
    96→    Matches: function declarations, named arrow functions, and React components.
    97→    Skips: interfaces, types, enums, and objects/arrays.
    98→    """
    99→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   100→        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   101→        if not name:
   102→            continue
   103→
   104→        brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(lines, i, window=5)
   105→        if brace_line is None:
   106→            continue
   107→
   108→        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=2000)
   109→        if end_line is not None:
   110→            loc = end_line - i + 1
   111→            if loc > 150:
   112→                smell_counts["monster_function"].append(
   113→                    {
   114→                        "file": filepath,
   115→                        "line": i + 1,
   116→                        "content": f"{name}() — {loc} LOC",
   117→                    }
   118→                )
   119→
   120→
   121→def _detect_dead_functions(
   122→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   123→):
   124→    """Find functions with empty body or only return/return null.
   125→
   126→    Matches function declarations and named arrow functions.
   127→    Skips decorated functions (TS decorators on line above).
   128→    """
   129→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   130→        # Skip decorated functions
   131→        if i > 0 and lines[i - 1].strip().startswith("@"):
   132→            continue
   133→
   134→        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
   135→        if not name:
   136→            continue
   137→
   138→        # Find opening brace
   139→        brace_line = None
   140→        for k in range(i, min(i + 5, len(lines))):
   141→            if "{" in lines[k]:
   142→                brace_line = k
   143→                break
   144→        if brace_line is None:
   145→            continue
   146→
   147→        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=30)
   148→        if end_line is None:
   149→            continue
   150→
   151→        # Extract body between braces
   152→        body_text = "\n".join(lines[brace_line : end_line + 1])
   153→        first_brace = body_text.find("{")
   154→        last_brace = body_text.rfind("}")
   155→        if first_brace == -1 or last_brace == -1 or first_brace >= last_brace:
   156→            continue
   157→
   158→        body = body_text[first_brace + 1 : last_brace]
   159→        body_clean = _strip_ts_comments(body).strip().rstrip(";")
   160→
   161→        if body_clean in ("", "return", "return null", "return undefined"):
   162→            label = body_clean or "empty"
   163→            smell_counts["dead_function"].append(
   164→                {
   165→                    "file": filepath,
   166→                    "line": i + 1,
   167→                    "content": f"{name}() — body is {label}",
   168→                }
   169→            )
   170→
   171→
   172→def _detect_window_globals(
   173→    filepath: str,
   174→    lines: list[str],
   175→    line_state: dict[int, str],
   176→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
   177→):
   178→    """Find window.__* assignments — global state escape hatches.
   179→
   180→    Matches:
   181→    - window.__foo = ...
   182→    - (window as any).__foo = ...
   183→    - window['__foo'] = ...
   184→    """
   185→    window_re = re.compile(
   186→        r"""(?:"""
   187→        r"""\(?\s*window\s+as\s+any\s*\)?\s*\.\s*(__\w+)"""  # (window as any).__name
   188→        r"""|window\s*\.\s*(__\w+)"""  # window.__name
   189→        r"""|window\s*\[\s*['"](__\w+)['"]\s*\]"""  # window['__name']
   190→        r""")\s*=""",
   191→    )
   192→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   193→        if i in line_state:
   194→            continue
   195→        m = window_re.search(line)
   196→        if not m:
   197→            continue
   198→        if _ts_match_is_in_string(line, m.start()):
   199→            continue
   200→        smell_counts["window_global"].append(
   201→            {
   202→                "file": filepath,
   203→                "line": i + 1,
   204→                "content": line.strip()[:100],
   205→            }
   206→        )
   207→
   208→
   209→def _detect_catch_return_default(
   210→    filepath: str, content: str, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   211→):
   212→    """Find catch blocks that return object literals with default/no-op values.
   213→
   214→    Catches the pattern:
   215→      catch (...) { ... return { key: false, key: null, key: () => {} }; }
   216→
   217→    This is a silent failure — the caller gets valid-looking data but the
   218→    operation actually failed.
   219→    """
   220→    catch_re = re.compile(r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   221→    for m in catch_re.finditer(content):
   222→        brace_start = m.end() - 1
   223→        depth = 0
   224→        body_end = None
   225→        for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(
   226→            content, brace_start, min(brace_start + _MAX_CATCH_BODY, len(content))
   227→        ):
   228→            if in_s:
   229→                continue
   230→            if ch == "{":
   231→                depth += 1
   232→            elif ch == "}":
   233→                depth -= 1
   234→                if depth == 0:
   235→                    body_end = ci
   236→                    break
   237→
   238→        if body_end is None:
   239→            continue
   240→
   241→        body = content[brace_start + 1 : body_end]
   242→        # Check if body contains "return {" — a return with object literal
   243→        return_obj = re.search(r"\breturn\s*\{", body)
   244→        if not return_obj:
   245→            continue
   246→
   247→        # Extract the returned object content
   248→        obj_start = body.find("{", return_obj.start())
   249→        obj_depth = 0
   250→        obj_end = None
   251→        for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(body, obj_start):
   252→            if in_s:
   253→                continue
   254→            if ch == "{":
   255→                obj_depth += 1
   256→            elif ch == "}":
   257→                obj_depth -= 1
   258→                if obj_depth == 0:
   259→                    obj_end = ci
   260→                    break
   261→
   262→        if obj_end is None:
   263→            continue
   264→
   265→        obj_content = body[obj_start + 1 : obj_end]
   266→        # Count default/no-op fields
   267→        noop_count = len(re.findall(r"\(\)\s*=>\s*\{\s*\}", obj_content))  # () => {}
   268→        false_count = len(
   269→            re.findall(r":\s*(?:false|null|undefined|0|''|\"\")\b", obj_content)
   270→        )
   271→        default_fields = noop_count + false_count
   272→
   273→        if default_fields >= 2:
   274→            line_no = content[: m.start()].count("\n") + 1
   275→            # Use content slice to get the line without re-splitting entire file
   276→            line_start = content.rfind("\n", 0, m.start()) + 1
   277→            line_end = content.find("\n", m.start())
   278→            if line_end == -1:
   279→                line_end = len(content)
   280→            smell_counts["catch_return_default"].append(
   281→                {
   282→                    "file": filepath,
   283→                    "line": line_no,
   284→                    "content": content[line_start:line_end].strip()[:100],
   285→                }
   286→            )
   287→
   288→
   289→def _detect_switch_no_default(
   290→    filepath: str, content: str, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
   291→):
   292→    """Flag switch statements that have no default case."""
   293→    switch_re = re.compile(r"\bswitch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   294→    for m in switch_re.finditer(content):
   295→        brace_start = m.end() - 1
   296→        depth = 0
   297→        body_end = None
   298→        for ci, ch, in_s in scan_code(
   299→            content,
   300→            brace_start,
   301→            min(brace_start + _MAX_SWITCH_BODY_SCAN, len(content)),
   302→        ):
   303→            if in_s:
   304→                continue
   305→            if ch == "{":
   306→                depth += 1
   307→            elif ch == "}":
   308→                depth -= 1
   309→                if depth == 0:
   310→                    body_end = ci
   311→                    break
   312→
   313→        if body_end is None:
   314→            continue
   315→
   316→        body = content[brace_start + 1 : body_end]
   317→        # Count case labels — only flag if there are actual cases
   318→        case_count = len(re.findall(r"\bcase\s+", body))
   319→        if case_count < 2:
   320→            continue
   321→
   322→        if re.search(r"\bdefault\s*:", body):
   323→            continue
   324→
   325→        line_no = content[: m.start()].count("\n") + 1
   326→        line_start = content.rfind("\n", 0, m.start()) + 1
   327→        line_end = content.find("\n", m.start())
   328→        if line_end == -1:
   329→            line_end = len(content)
   330→        smell_counts["switch_no_default"].append(
   331→            {
   332→                "file": filepath,
   333→                "line": line_no,
   334→                "content": content[line_start:line_end].strip()[:100],
   335→            }
   336→        )
   337→
