```
1→"""Extended TypeScript smell detection routines."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→
     7→
     8→def detect_error_no_throw(
     9→    filepath: str,
    10→    lines: list[str],
    11→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
    12→) -> None:
    13→    """Find console.error calls not followed by throw or return."""
    14→    for index, line in enumerate(lines):
    15→        if "console.error" in line:
    16→            following = "\n".join(lines[index + 1 : index + 4])
    17→            if not re.search(r"\b(?:throw|return)\b", following):
    18→                smell_counts["console_error_no_throw"].append(
    19→                    {
    20→                        "file": filepath,
    21→                        "line": index + 1,
    22→                        "content": line.strip()[:100],
    23→                    }
    24→                )
    25→
    26→
    27→def detect_empty_if_chains(
    28→    filepath: str,
    29→    lines: list[str],
    30→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
    31→) -> None:
    32→    """Find if/else chains where all branches are empty."""
    33→    index = 0
    34→    while index < len(lines):
    35→        stripped = lines[index].strip()
    36→        if not re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\(", stripped):
    37→            index += 1
    38→            continue
    39→
    40→        if re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", stripped):
    41→            chain_start = index
    42→            cursor = index + 1
    43→            while cursor < len(lines):
    44→                next_stripped = lines[cursor].strip()
    45→                if re.match(r"else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", next_stripped):
    46→                    cursor += 1
    47→                    continue
    48→                if re.match(r"(?:\}\s*)?else\s*\{\s*\}\s*$", next_stripped):
    49→                    cursor += 1
    50→                    continue
    51→                break
    52→            smell_counts["empty_if_chain"].append(
    53→                {
    54→                    "file": filepath,
    55→                    "line": chain_start + 1,
    56→                    "content": stripped[:100],
    57→                }
    58→            )
    59→            index = cursor
    60→            continue
    61→
    62→        if re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", stripped):
    63→            chain_start = index
    64→            chain_all_empty = True
    65→            cursor = index
    66→            while cursor < len(lines):
    67→                current = lines[cursor].strip()
    68→                if cursor == chain_start:
    69→                    if not re.match(r"(?:else\s+)?if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", current):
    70→                        chain_all_empty = False
    71→                        break
    72→                elif re.match(r"\}\s*else\s+if\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{\s*$", current):
    73→                    pass
    74→                elif re.match(r"\}\s*else\s*\{\s*$", current):
    75→                    pass
    76→                elif current == "}":
    77→                    lookahead = cursor + 1
    78→                    while lookahead < len(lines) and lines[lookahead].strip() == "":
    79→                        lookahead += 1
    80→                    if lookahead < len(lines) and re.match(
    81→                        r"else\s", lines[lookahead].strip()
    82→                    ):
    83→                        cursor = lookahead
    84→                        continue
    85→                    cursor += 1
    86→                    break
    87→                elif current == "":
    88→                    cursor += 1
    89→                    continue
    90→                else:
    91→                    chain_all_empty = False
    92→                    break
    93→                cursor += 1
    94→
    95→            if chain_all_empty and cursor > chain_start + 1:
    96→                smell_counts["empty_if_chain"].append(
    97→                    {
    98→                        "file": filepath,
    99→                        "line": chain_start + 1,
   100→                        "content": lines[chain_start].strip()[:100],
   101→                    }
   102→                )
   103→            index = max(index + 1, cursor)
   104→            continue
   105→
   106→        index += 1
   107→
   108→
   109→def detect_dead_useeffects(
   110→    filepath: str,
   111→    lines: list[str],
   112→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
   113→    *,
   114→    scan_code_fn,
   115→    strip_ts_comments_fn,
   116→) -> None:
   117→    """Find useEffect calls with empty or comment-only bodies."""
   118→    for index, line in enumerate(lines):
   119→        stripped = line.strip()
   120→        if not re.match(r"(?:React\.)?useEffect\s*\(\s*\(\s*\)\s*=>\s*\{", stripped):
   121→            continue
   122→
   123→        paren_depth = 0
   124→        end = None
   125→        for cursor in range(index, min(index + 30, len(lines))):
   126→            for _, ch, in_string in scan_code_fn(lines[cursor]):
   127→                if in_string:
   128→                    continue
   129→                if ch == "(":
   130→                    paren_depth += 1
   131→                elif ch == ")":
   132→                    paren_depth -= 1
   133→                    if paren_depth <= 0:
   134→                        end = cursor
   135→                        break
   136→            if end is not None:
   137→                break
   138→
   139→        if end is None:
   140→            continue
   141→
   142→        text = "\n".join(lines[index : end + 1])
   143→        arrow_pos = text.find("=>")
   144→        if arrow_pos == -1:
   145→            continue
   146→        brace_pos = text.find("{", arrow_pos)
   147→        if brace_pos == -1:
   148→            continue
   149→
   150→        depth = 0
   151→        body_end = None
   152→        for cursor, ch, in_string in scan_code_fn(text, brace_pos):
   153→            if in_string:
   154→                continue
   155→            if ch == "{":
   156→                depth += 1
   157→            elif ch == "}":
   158→                depth -= 1
   159→                if depth == 0:
   160→                    body_end = cursor
   161→                    break
   162→
   163→        if body_end is None:
   164→            continue
   165→
   166→        body = text[brace_pos + 1 : body_end]
   167→        if strip_ts_comments_fn(body).strip() == "":
   168→            smell_counts["dead_useeffect"].append(
   169→                {
   170→                    "file": filepath,
   171→                    "line": index + 1,
   172→                    "content": stripped[:100],
   173→                }
   174→            )
   175→
   176→
   177→def detect_swallowed_errors(
   178→    filepath: str,
   179→    content: str,
   180→    lines: list[str],
   181→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]],
   182→    *,
   183→    scan_code_fn,
   184→    strip_ts_comments_fn,
   185→) -> None:
   186→    """Find catch blocks whose only content is console.error/warn/log."""
   187→    catch_re = re.compile(r"catch\s*\([^)]*\)\s*\{")
   188→    for match in catch_re.finditer(content):
   189→        brace_start = match.end() - 1
   190→        depth = 0
   191→        body_end = None
   192→        for cursor, ch, in_string in scan_code_fn(
   193→            content,
   194→            brace_start,
   195→            min(brace_start + 500, len(content)),
   196→        ):
   197→            if in_string:
   198→                continue
   199→            if ch == "{":
   200→                depth += 1
   201→            elif ch == "}":
   202→                depth -= 1
   203→                if depth == 0:
   204→                    body_end = cursor
   205→                    break
   206→
   207→        if body_end is None:
   208→            continue
   209→
   210→        body = content[brace_start + 1 : body_end]
   211→        body_clean = strip_ts_comments_fn(body).strip()
   212→        if not body_clean:
   213→            continue
   214→
   215→        statements = [
   216→            stmt.strip().rstrip(";")
   217→            for stmt in re.split(r"[;\n]", body_clean)
   218→            if stmt.strip()
   219→        ]
   220→        if not statements:
   221→            continue
   222→
   223→        all_console = all(
   224→            re.match(r"console\.(error|warn|log)\s*\(", stmt) for stmt in statements
   225→        )
   226→        if all_console:
   227→            line_no = content[: match.start()].count("\n") + 1
   228→            smell_counts["swallowed_error"].append(
   229→                {
   230→                    "file": filepath,
   231→                    "line": line_no,
   232→                    "content": lines[line_no - 1].strip()[:100]
   233→                    if line_no <= len(lines)
   234→                    else "",
   235→                }
   236→            )
   237→
   238→
   239→def track_brace_body(
   240→    lines: list[str],
   241→    start_line: int,
   242→    *,
   243→    scan_code_fn,
   244→    max_scan: int = 2000,
   245→) -> int | None:
   246→    """Find closing brace that matches first opening brace from start_line."""
   247→    depth = 0
   248→    found_open = False
   249→    for line_idx in range(start_line, min(start_line + max_scan, len(lines))):
   250→        for _, ch, in_string in scan_code_fn(lines[line_idx]):
   251→            if in_string:
   252→                continue
   253→            if ch == "{":
   254→                depth += 1
   255→                found_open = True
   256→            elif ch == "}":
   257→                depth -= 1
   258→                if found_open and depth == 0:
   259→                    return line_idx
   260→    return None
   261→
   262→
   263→__all__ = [
   264→    "detect_dead_useeffects",
   265→    "detect_empty_if_chains",
   266→    "detect_error_no_throw",
   267→    "detect_swallowed_errors",
   268→    "track_brace_body",
   269→]
   270→
```

```
   174→
   175→def _build_string_line_set(lines: list[str]) -> set[int]:
   176→    """Build a set of 0-indexed line numbers that are inside multi-line strings.
   177→
   178→    Tracks triple-quote state across lines so regex-based checks can skip
   179→    lines that are inside multi-line string literals.
   180→    """
   181→    in_multiline: str | None = None  # '"""' or "'''" or None
   182→    string_lines: set[int] = set()
   183→
   184→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   185→        if in_multiline is not None:
   186→            string_lines.add(i)
   187→            # Check if this line closes the multi-line string
   188→            if in_multiline in line:
   189→                # Find the closing triple-quote (skip escaped ones)
   190→                pos = 0
   191→                while pos < len(line):
   192→                    idx = line.find(in_multiline, pos)
   193→                    if idx == -1:
   194→                        break
   195→                    # Check it's not escaped
   196→                    backslashes = 0
   197→                    j = idx - 1
   198→                    while j >= 0 and line[j] == "\\":
   199→                        backslashes += 1
   200→                        j -= 1
   201→                    if backslashes % 2 == 0:
   202→                        in_multiline = None
   203→                        break
   204→                    pos = idx + 3
   205→            continue
   206→
   207→        # Check if this line opens a multi-line string
   208→        pos = 0
   209→        while pos < len(line):
   210→            ch = line[pos]
   211→            if ch == "#":
   212→                break  # Rest is comment
   213→            # Skip string prefixes
   214→            if ch in ("r", "b", "f", "u", "R", "B", "F", "U") and pos + 1 < len(line):
   215→                next_ch = line[pos + 1]
   216→                if next_ch in ('"', "'"):
   217→                    pos += 1
   218→                    ch = next_ch
   219→                elif (
   220→                    next_ch in ("r", "b", "f", "R", "B", "F")
   221→                    and pos + 2 < len(line)
   222→                    and line[pos + 2] in ('"', "'")
   223→                ):
   224→                    pos += 2
   225→                    ch = line[pos]
   226→            if ch in ('"', "'"):
   227→                triple = line[pos : pos + 3]
   228→                if triple in ('"""', "'''"):
   229→                    # Check if it closes on the same line
   230→                    close_idx = line.find(triple, pos + 3)
   231→                    if close_idx == -1:
   232→                        # Opens a multi-line string
   233→                        in_multiline = triple
   234→                        break
   235→                    else:
   236→                        pos = close_idx + 3
   237→                        continue
   238→                else:
   239→                    # Single-line string — skip to closing quote
   240→                    end = line.find(ch, pos + 1)
   241→                    while end != -1 and end > 0 and line[end - 1] == "\\":
   242→                        end = line.find(ch, end + 1)
   243→                    pos = (end + 1) if end != -1 else len(line)
   244→                    continue
   245→            pos += 1
   246→
   247→    return string_lines
   248→
   249→
   250→def _match_is_in_string(line: str, match_start: int) -> bool:
   251→    """Check if a regex match position falls inside a string literal or comment on a single line."""
   252→    i, in_string = 0, None
   253→    while i < len(line):
   254→        if i == match_start:
   255→            return in_string is not None
   256→        ch = line[i]
   257→        if in_string is None:
   258→            if ch == "#":
   259→                return True  # In a comment, not real code
   260→            triple = line[i : i + 3]
   261→            if triple in ('"""', "'''"):
   262→                in_string = triple
   263→                i += 3
   264→                continue
   265→            if (
   266→                ch in ("r", "b", "f")
   267→                and i + 1 < len(line)
   268→                and line[i + 1] in ('"', "'")
   269→            ):
   270→                i += 1
   271→                ch = line[i]
   272→            if ch in ('"', "'"):
   273→                in_string = ch
   274→                i += 1
   275→                continue
   276→        else:
   277→            if ch == "\\" and i + 1 < len(line):
   278→                i += 2
   279→                continue
   280→            if in_string in ('"""', "'''"):
   281→                if line[i : i + 3] == in_string:
   282→                    in_string = None
   283→                    i += 3
   284→                    continue
   285→            elif ch == in_string:
   286→                in_string = None
   287→                i += 1
   288→                continue
   289→        i += 1
   290→    return in_string is not None
   291→
   292→
   293→def detect_smells(path: Path) -> tuple[list[dict], int]:
   294→    """Detect Python code smell patterns. Returns (entries, total_files_checked)."""
   295→    smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]] = {s["id"]: [] for s in SMELL_CHECKS}
   296→    files = find_py_files(path)
   297→    # Collect module-level constants for cross-file duplicate detection
   298→    constants_by_key: dict[tuple[str, str], list[tuple[str, int]]] = {}
   299→
   300→    for filepath in files:
   301→        try:
   302→            p = (
   303→                Path(filepath)
   304→                if Path(filepath).is_absolute()
   305→                else PROJECT_ROOT / filepath
   306→            )
   307→            content = p.read_text()
   308→            lines = content.splitlines()
   309→        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError) as exc:
   310→            log_best_effort_failure(
   311→                logger, f"read Python file for smell scan {filepath}", exc
   312→            )
   313→            continue
   314→
   315→        if _is_test_path(filepath):
   316→            continue
   317→
   318→        # Build set of lines inside multi-line strings to avoid false positives
   319→        multiline_string_lines = _build_string_line_set(lines)
   320→
   321→        for check in SMELL_CHECKS:
   322→            if check["pattern"] is None:
   323→                continue
   324→            for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   325→                # Skip lines inside multi-line strings
   326→                if i in multiline_string_lines:
   327→                    continue
   328→                m = re.search(check["pattern"], line)
   329→                if m and not _match_is_in_string(line, m.start()):
   330→                    # Skip URLs assigned to module-level constants (UPPER_CASE = "https://...")
   331→                    if check["id"] == "hardcoded_url" and re.match(
   332→                        r"^[A-Z_][A-Z0-9_]*\s*=", line.strip()
   333→                    ):
   334→                        continue
   335→                    smell_counts[check["id"]].append(
   336→                        {
   337→                            "file": filepath,
   338→                            "line": i + 1,
   339→                            "content": line.strip()[:100],
   340→                        }
   341→                    )
   342→
   343→        _detect_empty_except(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   344→        _detect_swallowed_errors(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   345→        detect_ast_smells(filepath, content, smell_counts)
   346→        detect_star_import_no_all(filepath, content, path, smell_counts)
   347→        detect_vestigial_parameter(filepath, content, lines, smell_counts)
   348→        collect_module_constants(filepath, content, constants_by_key)
   349→
   350→    # Cross-file: detect duplicate constants
   351→    detect_duplicate_constants(constants_by_key, smell_counts)
   352→
   353→    severity_order = {"high": 0, "medium": 1, "low": 2}
   354→    entries = []
   355→    for check in SMELL_CHECKS:
   356→        matches = smell_counts[check["id"]]
   357→        if matches:
   358→            entries.append(
   359→                {
   360→                    "id": check["id"],
   361→                    "label": check["label"],
   362→                    "severity": check["severity"],
   363→                    "count": len(matches),
   364→                    "files": len(set(m["file"] for m in matches)),
   365→                    "matches": matches[:50],
   366→                }
   367→            )
   368→    entries.sort(key=lambda e: (severity_order.get(e["severity"], 9), -e["count"]))
   369→    return entries, len(files)
   370→
   371→
   372→def _walk_except_blocks(lines: list[str]):
   373→    """Yield (line_index, except_line_stripped, body_lines) for each except block."""
   374→    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
   375→        stripped = line.strip()
   376→        if not re.match(r"except\s*(?:\w|\(|:)", stripped) and stripped != "except:":
   377→            continue
   378→        if not stripped.endswith(":"):
   379→            continue
   380→        indent = len(line) - len(line.lstrip())
   381→        j, body_lines = i + 1, []
   382→        while j < len(lines):
   383→            next_line = lines[j]
   384→            next_stripped = next_line.strip()
   385→            if next_stripped == "":
   386→                j += 1
   387→                continue
   388→            if len(next_line) - len(next_line.lstrip()) <= indent:
   389→                break
   390→            body_lines.append(next_stripped)
   391→            j += 1
   392→        yield i, stripped, body_lines
   393→
   394→
   395→def _is_broad_except(stripped: str) -> bool:
   396→    """Check if except clause catches broadly (bare, Exception, BaseException)."""
   397→    if stripped == "except:":
   398→        return True
   399→    m = re.match(r"except\s+(\w+)", stripped)
   400→    return bool(m and m.group(1) in ("Exception", "BaseException"))
   401→
   402→
   403→def _detect_empty_except(
   404→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list]
   405→):
   406→    """Find broad except blocks that just pass or have empty body."""
   407→    for i, stripped, body_lines in _walk_except_blocks(lines):
   408→        if (not body_lines or body_lines == ["pass"]) and _is_broad_except(stripped):
   409→            smell_counts["empty_except"].append(
   410→                {
   411→                    "file": filepath,
   412→                    "line": i + 1,
   413→                    "content": stripped[:100],
   414→                }
   415→            )
   416→
   417→
   418→def _detect_swallowed_errors(
   419→    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list]
   420→):
   421→    """Find except blocks that only print/log the error."""
   422→    _LOG_RE = r"(?:print|logging\.\w+|logger\.\w+|log\.\w+)\s*\("
   423→    for i, stripped, body_lines in _walk_except_blocks(lines):
   424→        if body_lines and all(re.match(_LOG_RE, s) for s in body_lines):
   425→            smell_counts["swallowed_error"].append(
   426→                {
   427→                    "file": filepath,
   428→                    "line": i + 1,
   429→                    "content": stripped[:100],
   430→                }
   431→            )
   432→
```

## Complete Analysis of TypeScript Smell Detector Infrastructure

Based on my thorough review of the codebase, here's what you need to know to implement the three new TS detectors:

---

### **1. Detection Architecture Overview**

The TS smell infrastructure has **two parallel paths**:

#### **Path A: Regex-based (simple patterns)**
- Lives in `TS_SMELL_CHECKS` list in `smells.py` (lines 33-204)
- Simple pattern matching with `pattern` field set to regex string
- Executed at lines 569-594 in `smells.py`
- Examples: `empty_catch`, `any_type`, `ts_ignore`, `magic_number`

#### **Path B: Multi-line helpers (brace-tracked)**
- Pattern is `None` in `TS_SMELL_CHECKS`
- Separate detector functions in `_smell_detectors.py` or `_smell_effects.py`
- Called explicitly at lines 596-606 in `smells.py`
- Uses `scan_code()` generator for string-aware character iteration

---

### **2. The `scan_code()` Generator — Core Infrastructure**

Located in `_smell_helpers.py` (lines 32-62):

```python
def scan_code(
    text: str, start: int = 0, end: int | None = None
) -> Generator[tuple[int, str, bool], None, None]:
    """Yield (index, char, in_string) tuples, handling escapes correctly."""
```

**Key properties:**
- Tracks single/double quotes and backticks (template literals)
- Properly handles escape sequences (`\\x` advances +2)
- Yields `(char_index, character, is_in_string_literal)` tuples
- **Critical:** Characters inside strings return `in_string=True`, so you skip them with `if in_string: continue`

**Pattern in detectors:**
```python
for idx, ch, in_string in scan_code(content, start_pos, end_pos):
    if in_string:
        continue  # Skip string literals
    if ch == "{":
        depth += 1
    elif ch == "}":
        depth -= 1
```

---

### **3. Multi-line Brace-Tracked Detectors — The Pattern**

#### **Example: `_detect_monster_functions()` (lines 91-118 in `_smell_detectors.py`)**

This is your template for nested closures and cyclomatic complexity:

```python
def _detect_monster_functions(
    filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]
):
    """Find functions exceeding 150 LOC via brace-tracking."""
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        # Step 1: Identify function start (using regex + next_lines context)
        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
        if not name:
            continue

        # Step 2: Find the opening brace (search forward a few lines)
        brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(lines, i, window=5)
        if brace_line is None:
            continue

        # Step 3: Track brace depth from opening brace to closing brace
        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=2000)
        if end_line is not None:
            # Step 4: Compute metric and threshold check
            loc = end_line - i + 1
            if loc > 150:
                smell_counts["monster_function"].append({
                    "file": filepath,
                    "line": i + 1,
                    "content": f"{name}() — {loc} LOC",
                })
```

**Helper: `_find_function_start()` (lines 49-82)**
- Matches: `function foo()`, `const foo = () =>`, `const foo = async (...) =>`
- Returns function name or `None`
- Handles decorators by checking `next_lines` context

**Helper: `_track_brace_body()` (lines 213-226 in `_smell_helpers.py`)**
```python
def _track_brace_body(
    lines: list[str], start_line: int, *, max_scan: int = 2000
) -> int | None:
    """Find closing brace matching the first opening brace from start_line."""
    return _track_brace_body_impl(
        lines, start_line, scan_code_fn=scan_code, max_scan=max_scan
    )
```
Returns **line index of the closing brace**, or `None` if not found.

---

### **4. Function-Level Scope Tracking (Dead Functions Pattern)**

Example: `_detect_dead_functions()` (lines 121-169 in `_smell_detectors.py`):

```python
def _detect_dead_functions(filepath, lines, smell_counts):
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        # Skip decorated functions
        if i > 0 and lines[i - 1].strip().startswith("@"):
            continue
        
        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
        if not name:
            continue

        # Find opening brace
        brace_line = None
        for k in range(i, min(i + 5, len(lines))):
            if "{" in lines[k]:
                brace_line = k
                break
        if brace_line is None:
            continue

        # Track body (small max_scan=30 for dead functions)
        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=30)
        if end_line is None:
            continue

        # Extract body text
        body_text = "\n".join(lines[brace_line : end_line + 1])
        first_brace = body_text.find("{")
        last_brace = body_text.rfind("}")
        if first_brace == -1 or last_brace == -1:
            continue

        # Clean body and check for empty/no-op patterns
        body = body_text[first_brace + 1 : last_brace]
        body_clean = _strip_ts_comments(body).strip().rstrip(";")

        if body_clean in ("", "return", "return null", "return undefined"):
            smell_counts["dead_function"].append({
                "file": filepath,
                "line": i + 1,
                "content": f"{name}() — body is {body_clean or 'empty'}",
            })
```

**Key insight:** Function scope is determined by **brace-depth tracking**, not AST. The body extends from `first_brace` to `last_brace` in the extracted text.

---

### **5. How Detectors Are Registered**

In `smells.py`:

1. **Define in `TS_SMELL_CHECKS`** (lines 33-204):
   ```python
   {
       "id": "monster_function",
       "label": "Monster function (>150 LOC)",
       "pattern": None,  # Signal: use custom detector
       "severity": "high",
   }
   ```

2. **Implement detector function** in `_smell_detectors.py` or `_smell_effects.py`:
   ```python
   def _detect_xxx(filepath: str, content: str, smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]):
       # Detection logic
   ```

3. **Call in `detect_smells()` orchestrator** (lines 596-606):
   ```python
   _detect_monster_functions(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   _detect_dead_functions(filepath, lines, smell_counts)
   # ... add your detectors here
   ```

---

### **6. Is TS Using AST-Based Detection?**

**No**, TypeScript detection is **100% regex + brace-tracking**, NOT AST-based. There is no tree-sitter integration in the TS detector module.

Evidence:
- `smells.py` only imports from `_smell_detectors.py` and `_smell_helpers.py`
- No AST visitor pattern like Python has in `smells_ast.py`
- All function boundary detection uses `_find_function_start()` (regex-based) + `_track_brace_body()` (brace-depth tracking)

---

### **7. Pattern for Adding the 3 New Detectors**

#### **Pattern 1: Nested Closures**

Pseudocode for detection:
```python
def _detect_nested_closures(filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]):
    """Find functions containing nested arrow functions or function expressions."""
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
        if not name:
            continue
        
        brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(lines, i, window=5)
        if brace_line is None:
            continue
        
        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=500)
        if end_line is None:
            continue
        
        # Extract function body
        body_text = "\n".join(lines[brace_line : end_line + 1])
        body = body_text[body_text.find("{") + 1 : body_text.rfind("}")]
        
        # Count nested closures: `() =>`, `function`, `(x) => {`
        nested_count = 0
        for idx, ch, in_string in scan_code(body):
            if in_string:
                continue
            # Match arrow function: () =>
            # Match function keyword inside body
        
        if nested_count >= 2:  # Threshold for "nested closure"
            smell_counts["nested_closure"].append({
                "file": filepath,
                "line": i + 1,
                "content": f"{name}() — {nested_count} nested closures",
            })
```

**Implementation approach:**
- Search for `() =>` or `function` keywords inside function body
- Use `scan_code()` to skip strings
- Count depth of nesting (requires tracking scope)

---

#### **Pattern 2: Mutable Ref Hack**

Pseudocode:
```python
def _detect_mutable_ref_hack(filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]):
    """Find useRef().current assignments (using mutable refs to escape closures)."""
    useref_re = re.compile(r"useRef\s*\(")
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        if "useRef" not in line:
            continue
        # Look for pattern: ref.current = ... (after useRef declaration)
        # Within next N lines, search for `.current\s*=`
        window = "\n".join(lines[i:min(i+50, len(lines))])
        if re.search(r"\.current\s*=", window):
            smell_counts["mutable_ref_hack"].append({
                "file": filepath,
                "line": i + 1,
                "content": line.strip()[:100],
            })
```

**Implementation approach:**
- Regex for `useRef` declarations
- Search following lines for `.current = ` assignments
- Track scope to avoid false positives in comments

---

#### **Pattern 3: High Cyclomatic Complexity**

Pseudocode:
```python
def _detect_high_cyclomatic_complexity(filepath: str, lines: list[str], smell_counts: dict[str, list[dict]]):
    """Find functions with high cyclomatic complexity (too many branches)."""
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        name = _find_function_start(line, lines[i + 1 : i + 3])
        if not name:
            continue
        
        brace_line = _find_opening_brace_line(lines, i, window=5)
        if brace_line is None:
            continue
        
        end_line = _track_brace_body(lines, brace_line, max_scan=500)
        if end_line is None:
            continue
        
        # Extract function body
        body_text = "\n".join(lines[brace_line : end_line + 1])
        body = body_text[body_text.find("{") + 1 : body_text.rfind("}")]
        
        # Count decision points: if, else, case, catch, for, while, ?:
        complexity = 1  # Base complexity
        for idx, ch, in_string in scan_code(body):
            if in_string:
                continue
            # Count: if, else if, case, catch, for, while, &&, ||, ?
        
        if complexity > 15:  # Threshold
            smell_counts["high_cyclomatic_complexity"].append({
                "file": filepath,
                "line": i + 1,
                "content": f"{name}() — complexity {complexity}",
            })
```

**Implementation approach:**
- Extract function body using brace tracking
- Iterate through body with `scan_code()` to skip strings
- Count decision keywords: `if`, `else`, `case`, `catch`, `for`, `while`
- Count operators: `&&`, `||`, `? ... :`
- Apply threshold (typical: 10-15)

### **9. Registration Checklist**

To add a new detector `high_cyclomatic_complexity`:

1. ✅ Add entry to `TS_SMELL_CHECKS` in `smells.py` (line 33+):

   ```python
       "id": "high_cyclomatic_complexity",

       "severity": "high",
   }
   ```

2. ✅ Implement `_detect_high_cyclomatic_complexity()` in `_smell_detectors.py`

3. ✅ Call it in `detect_smells()` function (after line 605):


4. ✅ Add import at top of `smells.py`:

   ```python
       ...

   )
   ```

---

### **10. Key Utility Functions Summary**

| Function | Purpose | Input | Output |
|----------|---------|-------|--------|
| `scan_code()` | String-aware character iteration | `(text, start, end)` | `(idx, char, is_in_string)` |
| `_find_function_start()` | Identify function declarations/assignments | `(line, next_lines)` | Function name or `None` |
| `_find_opening_brace_line()` | Locate opening `{` forward from line | `(lines, start_line, window)` | Line index or `None` |
| `_track_brace_body()` | Find matching closing `}` | `(lines, start_line, max_scan)` | Line index of `}` or `None` |
| `_strip_ts_comments()` | Remove `//` and `/* */` comments | `text` | Text with comments removed |
| `_ts_match_is_in_string()` | Check if regex match is inside string | `(line, match_start)` | Boolean |

### **Final Code Location Reference**

```
desloppify/
├── languages/typescript/
│   ├── detectors/
│   │   ├── smells.py                 # ← Main orchestrator (call detectors here)
│   │   │                             # ← TS_SMELL_CHECKS defined here
│   │   ├── _smell_detectors.py       # ← Implement brace-tracked detectors here
│   │   │                             # ← monster_function, dead_function patterns
│   │   ├── _smell_effects.py         # ← Alternative location (e.g., detect_swallowed_errors)
│   │   └── _smell_helpers.py         # ← scan_code(), helper utilities
│   └── phases.py                     # ← High-level orchestration (calls phase_smells)
```

This infrastructure gives you everything needed to detect multi-line structural patterns without requiring an AST parser. The brace-tracking approach is lightweight and fast.
